# Decision log

Earlier decisions (D01–D20) live in Discovery Brief v0.3 in the claude.ai Project. Add new decisions here as they are made.

| ID | Status | Entry |
|---|---|---|
| D19 | Re-scoped | AI provider choice does not block Milestones 1–2 |
| D21 | Partly done | Dataverse via a read-only application user (done). Outlook via delegated `Calendars.ReadBasic` and Google via desktop OAuth `calendar.events.readonly` (proposed, set up in step 3) |
| D22 | Accepted | Stack option A: local TypeScript app opened as a Chromium app window, host layer thin enough to move to Tauri later |
| D23 | Done | Cache last good snapshot per source as files; SQLite arrives with the plan layer (step 5, D71) |
| D24 | Superseded by D60 | PAT column security not a priority now (read-only tokens). Step 4 reads PATs for DevOps; column security is still off (Q16) |
| D25 | Done | Application user "Coopilot Dataverse Reader", role Coopilot Reader: Read (Org) on Focus Time, Account, User, Account Worker. Verified Oct 31 |
| D26 | Confirmed | Workweek is Mon–Thu; Friday is an optional overflow/catch-up day shown separately |
| D27 | Confirmed | Focus Time rows are recurring weekly patterns expanded per week; blank end date means ongoing |
| D28 | Confirmed | Unassigned Mon–Thu half-days show as "Open" (client or internal) |
| D29 | Confirmed | Half-days are soft commitment units; the day shape in config is for placement and capacity only |
| D30 | Proposed | Monday AM is a standing Internal block from Coopilot config; a Dataverse client block in the same slot wins and raises a double-booked flag |
| D31 | Done | Application user sits in its own business unit with no team roles; Contacts refused. Verified Oct 31 |
| D32 | Confirmed | Person and client come from the Account Worker link (`coop_user`, `coop_account`), falling back to Focus Time's own columns. the product owner is identified by `systemuserid`, never by name |
| D33 | Proposed | Blocks for the same client in one half-day merge into one (Dataverse copy kept) and are not double-booked. Only different clients raise the double-booked flag. So a Dataverse Internal block on Monday AM silently satisfies the standing Internal block |
| D34 | Proposed | Event placement buckets per day: early (midnight to 09:00), AM [09:00, 12:30), PM [12:30, 17:00), evening (17:00 to midnight). Boundaries are half-open, so a meeting ending at 12:30 is AM only. An event crossing a boundary appears in each bucket, clipped, and only the clipped minutes count |
| D35 | Proposed | Risk rule details: rules run only on half-days with a focus block (Open half-days and Friday get no risk flags); overlapping meetings count once; missing showAs counts as busy; personal events count as busy time for the focus-window rule but not as meeting minutes; one personal-conflict risk per personal event |
| D36 | Proposed | On Saturday and Sunday the app opens on, and `t` jumps to, the coming week's Monday AM |
| D37 | Done | Step 1 stack: TypeScript 7.0.2 (strict), Vite 8.3.5 + React 19.3.1, Vitest 5.0.5, playwright-core 1.64.1 driving /usr/bin/chromium. No date library: Temporal is built into Node 26 and Chromium 155. Exact pins, local installs only |
| D38 | Done | Code layout uses the top-level folders from the architecture map (`week/`, `ui/`, `sources/`, `config/`, `actions/`) plus `fixtures/`. Sample data reaches the UI through `sources/sample.ts` and `actions/week-load.ts`, the same path real sources will use. Config is compiled-in defaults until the TOML loader exists |
| D39 | Done | Dev server binds 127.0.0.1:5317 with Vite's Host check (a foreign Host header gets 403). It serves only static UI, no data API, so the per-launch token and Origin check arrive with the first local API in a later step |
| D40 | Done | Dataverse token via a plain client-credentials POST to Entra with built-in fetch (no MSAL). Access token in memory only, renewed 5 minutes before expiry. Only the OAuth `error` code is read from failures, never `error_description` |
| D41 | Done | Keyring through the system `secret-tool` (Secret Service), no native binding. The secret comes back on stdout, never in argv; only the `dataverse-client-secret` account under service `coopilot` can be read |
| D42 | Done | Local host: `node:http` on 127.0.0.1:5317. A 256-bit per-launch token travels in the launch URL, which is written to `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/coopilot/launch.json` (0600) and never printed. The first request swaps it for an HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie, and every request must carry it. Host allowlist (DNS rebinding), Origin and Sec-Fetch-Site checks, a POST-only API that needs a same-origin Origin, and a strict CSP for the built UI. `npm start` opens `chromium --app=<launch URL>`, so the token is visible in the local process list (same user only) while the window is open |
| D43 | Done | Client key and label = account `coop_reportingnickname`, falling back to `name`. The full name shows in details. Real nicknames: Cooptimize (internal), W&C, Quiddity, CSC. The standing block and colors use these keys, so the standing Monday AM block is `client = "Cooptimize"` and merges with the real Cooptimize pattern (D33) |
| D44 | Proposed | An invalid config shows no focus blocks at all (no sample patterns, no standing blocks) plus a banner naming each bad field. Sample meetings stay, labeled "sample", until step 3 |
| D45 | Done | The query filters by person on the server (`_coop_systemuser_value` or `coop_AccountWorker/_coop_user_value` equals my systemuserid), so teammates' rows are never fetched. Normalization re-applies "Account Worker wins" exactly |
| D46 | Done | Results stay in memory for 5 minutes per week; the Refresh button forces a fetch. The snapshot is per week and is written only after a complete, successful fetch, so a failed or partial fetch never overwrites it. Config is re-read on every request |
| D47 | Done | Entra issues tokens for any `*.dynamics.com` resource, so a wrong `environment_url` fails at DNS. ENOTFOUND on the Dataverse host after a successful token is reported as "Dataverse environment not found: check environment_url in config", not as offline |
| D48 | Done | Outlook and Google sign-in is authorization code + PKCE (S256) through a one-shot listener on 127.0.0.1 with a random port, written with built-in `fetch`, `crypto` and `http`. No MSAL or Google client library. Redirects: `http://localhost` for Microsoft (registered under Mobile and desktop, any port matches) and `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` for Google. The listener checks Host and `state`, then closes |
| D49 | Done | Refresh tokens live only in the keyring (`outlook-refresh-token`, `google-refresh-token`); access tokens only in memory. Microsoft rotates refresh tokens, so a new one replaces the old on every refresh. The Google desktop client secret is read from the keyring (`google-client-secret`) and Coopilot never writes or deletes it |
| D50 | Done | Scopes: Microsoft `offline_access Calendars.ReadBasic` (no body, attachments or attendees); Google `calendar.events.readonly`. Requests select only the fields used: Outlook `subject,start,end,isAllDay,showAs,responseStatus,isCancelled`; Google `id,status,summary,transparency,start,end,attendees(self,responseStatus)` (the self entry only matters, to detect my declines) |
| D51 | Done | Normalization: cancelled events and ones I declined are dropped. Outlook `workingElsewhere` counts as free, `tentativelyAccepted` and `tentative` as tentative. Google `transparent` counts as free. Outlook is fetched with `Prefer: outlook.timezone` set to the day shape zone; Google uses `singleEvents=true` over Mon 00:00 to Sat 00:00 local. Paging links are followed only when they stay on the provider API; a failed later page fails the whole fetch, so a partial week is never cached |
| D52 | Done | `auth.disconnect(outlook or google)` is local only: it forgets that source's refresh token, its in-memory token and its cached snapshots. It does not revoke the grant at the provider (revoking is a write; remove access in the account settings if wanted). Dataverse cannot be disconnected from the UI |
| D53 | Done | Sample meetings are used only for a calendar whose config section is missing or has an empty `client_id`. Once configured, that source is real (live, cached or needs sign-in) and never falls back to samples. The fixture stays for tests |
| D54 | Done | Q7 and Q8: an Outlook out-of-office event puts the half-days it covers at risk (timed: if it overlaps; all-day: the whole day). Other all-day Outlook events are a watch ("All-day event") only if busy or showAs is missing; free and tentative are ignored. Personal events on Open half-days and Friday show but never flag |
| D55 | Done | Status bar per calendar: LIVE, CACHED (with the snapshot time), NEEDS SIGN-IN with Connect or Reconnect, and Disconnect behind a confirm. While sign-in runs, the bar says to finish in the browser; it gives up after 5 minutes |
| D56 | Done | Freshness (step 3.5): the window refetches all sources when it regains focus (at most once per 60 s), quietly every 5 minutes while visible (a manual or focus refresh resets that clock), and on "r" or Refresh. Only one refresh runs at a time. Focus and background refreshes do not show "Loading" |
| D57 | Done | A refresh never blanks the grid: the data on screen stays until new data arrives. If the server cannot be reached, the last good week stays with "Could not reach the Coopilot server. Showing what was loaded earlier"; if a source fails, the server already returns its snapshot with the CACHED or error state. A slower older response never overwrites a newer one. The status bar shows "updated N min ago" per source (the exact time is in the tooltip) |
| D58 | Done | One badge per half-day card: the most serious risk, then "+N more". Order: at risk before watch; within a level out of office, personal conflict, double-booked block, no focus window, all-day event, meetings. Double-booked and all-day event were not in the requested order; they sit where shown. The details panel lists every reason in the same order |
| D59 | Done | The week view shows personal assigned work: DevOps work items assigned to me, open (state category not Completed or Removed), any type, no sprint filter, in each client's focus blocks. Ownership (epics and features I own, contributors' progress, target dates) is a separate future area, not this view |
| D60 | Done | PATs: Dataverse `devopsTargets(clientKeys)` is the only query that selects `coop_devopstoken`. It runs at DevOps fetch time, only for clients with a block this week, only for accounts I am an active Account Worker on, and wraps each PAT in `Pat` at once (String, JSON and inspect show `[PAT]`). The cached focus block path never selects it. The PAT is used only to build `Authorization: Basic base64(":" + PAT)` for `https://dev.azure.com/<org>/`, with redirects not followed. Not kept between fetches |
| D61 | Done | Identity per client = my Account Worker `coop_devopsemail`, used as `[System.AssignedTo] = '<email>'` in WIQL. Blank means "DevOps identity missing for <client>" and no call. Verified Oct 31: matches in the Cooptimize, Quiddity and CSC orgs |
| D62 | Done | Open = per work item type, states whose category (from that project's `workitemtypes` metadata) is not Completed or Removed. WIQL has one clause per type, since state names differ per type ("Ready for Go-live" is Completed for Bug but Resolved for Issue in one real project). Items are re-checked after the batch |
| D63 | Done | Board order = `typeFields.Order.referenceName` from the project's process configuration (StackRank or BacklogPriority), detected per project. No order field: sorted by last change. Unranked items go last. Type, state and order metadata are kept in memory for 1 hour |
| D64 | Done | API: api-version 7.1. WIQL (`$top=1000`, ids only) then `workitemsbatch` (200 ids per call, documented maximum) for System.Id, Title, WorkItemType, State, Parent, ChangedDate and the order field, `errorPolicy: omit`. The item link is built as `https://dev.azure.com/<org>/<project name>/_workitems/edit/<id>`. Project paths use the project name first, then the ID: CSC's stored ID is wrong and two accounts have none (Q15) |
| D65 | Done | Isolation: each client is fetched in its own try/catch and cached in its own file (`devops/<slug>-<hash>.json`, items only). Results are keyed by exact client key, the same key focus blocks use (`clientKeyOf`: nickname, else name); two accounts with one key are refused, not merged. A block shows only its own client's items; Open half-days and Friday show none. If DevOps fails as a whole, the week still loads |
| D66 | Done | Failure messages: "DevOps PAT expired or invalid for X" (401, 203, redirect or non-JSON), "No access to DevOps project P for X" (403, 404), "DevOps organization O unreachable" (network), "DevOps identity missing for X", "DevOps not set up for X (missing ...)", "No Account Worker for X in Dataverse", "DevOps settings unavailable (Dataverse ...)". Failures fall back to the client's last good items with their time |
| D67 | Done | Refresh: DevOps follows the step 3.5 triggers, throttled more because items do not depend on the week: a normal load (changing weeks) reuses results up to 5 minutes old; a forced refresh refetches a client at most once a minute; metadata is cached for 1 hour. That keeps a refresh to about 2 DevOps calls per client and avoids refetching on every week change |
| D68 | Done | UI: each block shows its client's top 3 items by board order and "+N more"; the details panel lists all, each a link that opens in a new browser tab (only `https://dev.azure.com/` links). The status bar has one "DevOps N of M live" entry; hover or click for each client's status and message |
| D69 | Done | Responsive week view (step 4.5). Three layouts chosen from the window width by what the content needs (`ui/viewport.ts`): wide (rail 330 px plus a 300 px hour timeline per Mon–Thu column, so 2,902 px and up) keeps the original timeline grid; medium (compact cards, 210 px per Mon–Thu column, 190 px Friday, so 1,134 px and up) shows client, free time, one badge, meetings and personal events as short time-stamped lists (4 and 3, then "+N more") and the top 3 DevOps items, with no timeline; narrow shows one day with a day bar (previous, Mon–Fri tabs, next), rail plus timeline down to 662 px and the compact card below that. Layout switches live on resize. Keys are the same in every layout: in narrow, left and right change the day. Selection survives a layout switch |
| D70 | Done | Keyboard hints in the top bar hide below 1,730 px, where they would collide with the centered week title. Friday's hover tooltip on a card's badge is replaced by the reason shown inline on the selected card, since cards clip overflow |
| D71 | Done | Plan store: SQLite via built-in `node:sqlite` (SQLite 3.54 in Node 26.12, no new dependency) at `$XDG_DATA_HOME/coopilot/plan.db` (data, not cache), 0600 in a 0700 folder, WAL. Numbered migrations; a store from a newer schema is refused, not guessed at. Timestamps are zoned strings with offset and zone. Export with `scripts/plan-export.ts` |
| D72 | Done | A pick is kind `item` (DevOps id, title, type and URL copied at pick time so the plan reads without DevOps), `draft` (local draft story) or `intention` (typed text). Status planned, done, moved or dropped; `carried_from` points at the pick it came from |
| D73 | Done | Capacity of a half-day = its free gaps (no busy meeting or personal event) counted from now on. Fit: planned picks only; tight under 30 min spare; over by the excess. S 60, M 120, L 210 minutes; M default; nothing above L ("split it first"), enforced in validation and in the schema |
| D74 | Done | Carry-over = picks still planned on an earlier date within 7 days. Moving a pick to another day marks the original "moved" and creates a new pick there, so nothing is offered twice. Remove before commit deletes; after commit keeps it as dropped. A carried pick removed again before commit goes back to its old day |
| D75 | Done | Block client: the focus block's client; an Open half-day takes its first pick's client. Mixed clients are allowed and flagged. Open half-days pick from this week's DevOps clients; standing-block clients (Cooptimize) are now always fetched (answers Q19) |
| D76 | Done | Story level from process configuration backlog categories: requirementBacklog, taskBacklog and bugWorkItems are pickable; portfolioBacklogs are not; types on no backlog are not; no backlog metadata at all means nothing is blocked. Enforced on the server: pick.add looks the item up in that client's loaded DevOps results and refuses portfolio items with a plain reason |
| D77 | Done | Children of my portfolio items: open items (any assignee) whose `System.Parent` is one of them, two levels deep, via WIQL plus the batch call (reads only), at most 200 parents per query. Cached with the items. Verified on the real orgs Oct 31 (Cooptimize 6, W&C 8, CSC 13 children) |
| D78 | Done | Plan the day (then "ignition") opens itself on the first focus of a workday (Mon–Thu) from 05:00 when today isn't committed, once per day (marker in the store, so a dismissed panel stays closed across reloads and restarts); `i` opens it any time (today, or the selected day when today isn't on screen). Reminder: one `notify-send` from the day-shape start until the usual end, once per day (marker). The window checks every 30 s for a new day and moves to it |
| D79 | Done | Always on with the least invasive Omarchy-supported pieces, no Hyprland edits: a systemd user service (`graphical-session.target`, restart on failure, rebuilds the UI before start) and an XDG autostart entry (Omarchy runs these through uwsm's xdg-desktop-autostart.target) that runs `scripts/open-window.ts`, which waits for the server's launch file and opens the app window. Installed Oct 31 with the product owner's approval |
| D80 | Done | Q20: portfolio types can be made pickable by name in config, `[plan] pickable_portfolio_types` (matched without case), applied at each DevOps fetch; a listed type is not opened into children. Set to `["Recurring"]`: Cooptimize's Recurring is pickable; Domain (and Epic, Feature, Ongoing) stay unpickable |
| D81 | Done | Q21: Open half-days offer every client I'm an active Account Worker on. A new Dataverse read (`myClients`: my Account Worker rows, then those accounts' name, nickname and whether `coop_devopsorg` is set; never the PAT; 5 minutes in memory) adds them; those with DevOps set up are fetched too (more DevOps calls, accepted), the rest take typed intentions. Verified Oct 31: Antigua and HM Electronics added |
| D82 | Done | Q22: draft stories stay local in the plan store until an authorized write step; nothing creates work items in DevOps |
| D83 | Done | Q23: the reminder fires only from the day-shape start until 90 minutes after it (09:00 to 10:30 today), once per day; a server started later that day sends none |
| D84 | Done | Quiddity 0 items (was 2): not the Blocked states. WIQL for my Quiddity email returns 0 items in any state, before any filter; my Account Worker `coop_devopsemail` ends in @quiddity.org while my Quiddity DevOps identity (the PAT owner, 40 items) ends in @quiddity.com. Coopilot keeps using the Dataverse email and does not guess; fix the email in Dataverse. None of those 40 items is in a Blocked state today, so the "treat as open" state list (Q18) was not built |
| D85 | Done | Step 5.5: one launch path, `coopilot-open` (`system/coopilot-open`, linked from `~/.local/bin`). It starts `coopilot.service` if it isn't active, waits up to 60 s for a live launch file that answers on 127.0.0.1, then runs Omarchy's `omarchy-launch-or-focus` with `omarchy-launch-webapp` as the launch command. The launcher entry, the key binding and login autostart all run it. The launch link is read from the 0600 launch file inside the script's `--window` step, so it never passes through `eval` and is never printed. Resolves Q24 |
| D86 | Done | Window identity: the app window runs in its own Chromium data directory (`~/.local/share/coopilot/browser`), apart from browsing profiles. Chromium ignores `--class` for `--app` windows and names them after host and profile, so the class is a stable `chrome-127.0.0.1__-Default` (`StartupWMClass` in the launcher entry). The focus pattern is `^(coopilot\|chrome-127\.0\.0\.1__-default)$`: a plain "coopilot" would also match a terminal titled after the project |
| D87 | Done | After a service restart the open window's token is stale. `coopilot-open` records which server pid the window was opened for (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/coopilot/window-server-pid`) and replaces a window from an earlier run instead of focusing it. The "needs a fresh launch link" screen now points to the launcher or Super + Alt + C |
| D88 | Done | Omarchy integration, user config only (Oct 31, approved): `~/.local/share/applications/Coopilot.desktop`, placeholder icon `~/.local/share/icons/hicolor/256x256/apps/coopilot.png` (logo later), one line in `~/.config/hypr/bindings.lua` binding Super + Alt + C (was free) to `coopilot-open`, and the XDG autostart entry's Exec switched from `scripts/open-window.ts` to `coopilot-open`. `scripts/open-window.ts` stays for `npm start` setups |
| D89 | Done | Edge profiles: Edge is the native package (`/usr/bin/microsoft-edge-stable`, profiles in `~/.config/microsoft-edge`), detected from its .desktop entry; a Flatpak install would use `flatpak run com.microsoft.Edge` and `~/.var/app/com.microsoft.Edge/config/microsoft-edge`. Local State is read for profile directory and display name only (`node scripts/edge-profiles.ts`). Config `[edge]`: `command = ["uwsm-app", "--", "/usr/bin/microsoft-edge-stable"]` (through uwsm-app Edge runs in its own unit, so restarting the Coopilot service never closes it) and profiles Cooptimize → Default, Quiddity → Profile 1, CSC → Profile 3, confirmed by the product owner. W&C, Antigua and HM Electronics are left unmapped: they open with `omarchy-launch-browser` (default browser) and show "No Edge profile set for <client>" |
| D90 | Done | Action `link.open(clientKey, url)`: only `https://dev.azure.com/<org>/...` with no credentials or port, where `<org>` is that client's DevOps organization (from its Dataverse settings at the last fetch, or from its cached item links). Another client's org, any other host or scheme, and a client whose org isn't known yet are refused. Edge is started from an argument array with `--profile-directory=<dir>` and the link, never a shell. Every work item link uses it: week cards, the details panel, Plan the day picks, and a small open link (↗) beside each Plan the day candidate. Supersedes the new-tab links in D68 |
| D91 | Done | Block cards no longer show the source line ("Dataverse focus block · …", "Standing block · Coopilot config", "Free for client or internal work", "Also booked … · Dataverse") in any layout. The source stays in the details panel |
| D92 | Recorded | **Follow-up** (concept, not built): something I owe someone, an update, an answer or a follow-through, with who, what, channel and an optional due date, open until closed. Opened by a rule (a pick marked done while its DevOps item wasn't updated; a block slipped or moved and someone is affected; an item blocked; a promise noted in capture) or by hand. Closed automatically when a signal proves it (the DevOps state or a comment changed; a Coopilot-drafted message confirmed sent) or marked closed by me with an optional note. Waiting-on follow-ups (others owe me) are wanted later |
| D93 | Recorded | Future direction (not built): an AI layer that reads Teams, email and DevOps to detect follow-ups and pull together context, staged as rules, then signals, then AI proposals I confirm, then an orchestrator. Every stage goes through the same named actions and confirmations as the UI; AI never executes on its own (CLAUDE.md hard rules) |
| D94 | Done | Step 6 rename: "ignition" is "Plan the day" everywhere (UI, code, docs, glossary): `ui/PlanDay.tsx`, `ui/planday.ts`, actions `planday.start` and `planday.commit`, data field `planDay`, CSS `pd-*`. Store migration 2 renames the once-a-day marker `ignition_auto` to `plan_day_auto`, so a dismissed panel stays dismissed. `p` opens it; `i` stays as an alias. Plan the week, Check-in and Close out are reserved names, shown as disabled "coming soon" tabs |
| D95 | Done | Top bar: views (Week, Plan the day, and the three reserved views disabled), week arrows, the week title, Today, Refresh, Commands (Ctrl K) and a disabled Settings. The old key-hint row is gone; keys still work and every key is shown beside its menu entry. Below 1640 px the "coming soon" tabs hide |
| D96 | Done | On a weekend, Today keeps showing next week (D36) and the top bar says "Today is Sat, Oct 31 · showing next week". The "Today" badge now marks only the real date, so no day is badged on a weekend |
| D97 | Done | Plan the day targets today on a workday (Mon–Thu) before the usual end; otherwise the next workday (Friday evening, Saturday and Sunday go to Monday; Thursday evening goes to the next Monday, since Friday is the overflow day). The panel says which day ("Plan Monday, November 2 · Next workday"). Commit, carry-over and picks work the same for that date. The automatic open (D78) stays for today only. `plan/target.ts` |
| D98 | Done (hours and spreading superseded by D105, D109) | PTO source (step 6): Dataverse `coop_timeofftrackers` filtered by `_coop_employee_value eq <my systemuserid>` and active state, overlapping Mon–Fri of the week, selecting only id, start date, end date, hours and title; then `coop_timeofftrackerdetails` of those trackers (tracker lookup, date, hours). Never selects `coop_outofofficemessage`; never reads `coop_timeofffact`. Detail rows win per day (a duplicate day counts once, the larger); hours are text and parsed ("8", "4.5", "4:30", "2h"); unreadable rows are skipped. A tracker with no details covers its weekdays, filling whole days in order until its hours run out (no hours: every weekday is full). Same behavior as other sources: last good snapshot in the cache, plain status line, a PTO failure changes nothing else |
| D99 | Done (partial part superseded by D105) | PTO effects: a full day (PTO ≥ the day-shape length) shows "PTO · title" in a distinct color with a hatch, dims its blocks, raises no risks, and refuses picks on the server ("…is a PTO day… Nothing can be planned on it", for add and move). Partial PTO shows "PTO 4h" and comes off the afternoon first, then the morning, because the time of day isn't recorded (Q28). Free time is capped at the half's length minus its PTO share, so an Outlook out-of-office event for the same hours isn't counted twice |
| D100 | Done | One free-time number everywhere: free gaps after now (when the half is under way) capped by length minus PTO (`week/free.ts`). Every layout's card shows "2h meetings · 2h 15m free · longest 1h", and Plan the day's capacity and fit check use the same number |
| D101 | Done | Action registry `actions/registry.ts`: one list of named actions with title, group, target and shortcut. The dispatcher rejects names not in it and switches over it exhaustively (a missing case is a type error); a test checks the dispatch cases equal the registry. The command menu adds one entry per registry action, so new actions appear automatically. This is the allowlist the chat panel will use |
| D102 | Done | Menus: a "⋯" button and right-click on every block card, pick and work item (week cards, details, Plan the day picks, carry-over, candidates and portfolio rows). Picks: open in Edge, set size, move (other half, Friday, any half-day of the week; full PTO days disabled with the reason), mark done, drop. Work items: open in Edge, add to the plan (selected half-day first), add a draft story under an empty epic or feature. Popup menus use the HTML popover API so they sit above the details dialog. Command menu: Ctrl+K or the top-bar button, word search, steps for actions that need a target |
| D103 | Done | Outlook show-as: busy as before; tentative lighter with a dashed outline; free very faint and dotted; out of office its own color (purple, a lighter tone in dark mode), a double left rule and a diagonal hatch, and the words "out of office" in the meta line, so it reads without color; working elsewhere muted with a dotted rule and now its own value (was mapped to free). Working elsewhere and free never count as busy for risks. A legend sits above the events in the details panel |
| D104 | Done | React effects must return nothing or a cleanup: Chromium's `scrollIntoView` now returns a Promise, and an expression-bodied effect handed it to React as a cleanup, which crashed the app when the command menu closed. Effects use block bodies |
| D105 | Done (half-days from out of office: D453) | Q28: no partial PTO. Every PTO day is a whole day off. Hours are no longer selected or parsed (`coop_timeoffhours`, `coop_hours` dropped from the queries), the "afternoon first" rule and per-half PTO minutes are gone; a slot carries `pto: true` and has no free time. Supersedes the partial parts of D98, D99 and D100 |
| D106 | Done | Q29: the next workday skips PTO days. `planTarget(now, dayEnd, ptoDates)`: today counts only if it isn't PTO, and the next workday is the next Mon–Thu that isn't PTO. The week load sends `ptoAhead` (PTO dates from this week's Monday about five weeks ahead, one extra PTO query, memoized) plus the shown week's PTO, so the target stays right as the clock passes the end of day. A PTO today is not a planning workday: no automatic open and no reminder. If the PTO read fails the target just doesn't skip; the server still refuses picks on a PTO day it knows |

Later slips that day update the open follow-up's draft. A make-up tonight on the same day is not a slip. **Blocker:** a DevOps item assigned to me whose state or tags contain "blocked" (word match) opens "Nudge on blocker for #id"; it closes when the item is no longer blocked or is completed, and opens again later only if the last one closed by a signal. **Flex session ended:** unchanged (D126). **Manual:** "Add a follow-up" (who up to 100 characters, what up to 200, optional due date), in Close out, the Follow-ups view and the command menu (`followup.add`). Fit per client: available blocks = the sum of each of its blocks' free share (free minutes after PTO, meetings and unavailable time over working minutes; Open half-days its features are placed on count) minus its unpaid borrowed minutes over its average block length, to one decimal; verdict fits, tight (under half a block to spare) or over, "Nothing committed" when none. | D416 | Done | R1: the app is renamed from Coopilot to FocusTime on Nov 2, 2026. On the first start of the renamed build (`server/rename.ts`), before anything else: the old folders are copied, never moved or overwritten, and keep a MIGRATED.txt note, without the app window's browser profile. Each keyring account the code knows (four fixed accounts plus a write PAT per client with a saved DevOps identity) is written under the new service, read back, and only then cleared from the old one. Failures keep the old entry and put a plain note on the sign-in screen. Kept on purpose: the Outlook marker property `CoopilotId` and the `tb-`/`inv-` prefixes (renaming would orphan events); the body line "Timebox planned in Coopilot." on events written before (counted as the same as the new line, so no event is rewritten); the DevOps undo comment "Removed by Coopilot: …" (already posted); config section and key names, write log tables and kinds, and migration numbers; the Entra registration "Coopilot Desktop" and the Dataverse app user "Coopilot Dataverse Reader" (display names outside the app, Q97, Q98); the Dataverse table "Focus Time"; and the names in earlier decisions. Coop and Cooptimize are unchanged |
| D417 | Done | A1 host boundary: every operating system touch goes through one `Host` (`host/types.ts`). The parts are secrets, settings, paths, snapshots, photos, files, the sign-in loopback, notifications with click routing, links, media, launch, the clock and its default zone, and process facts. There are two implementations. `host/local` is the developer loop: the keyring, XDG folders, notify-send, playerctl, xdg-open and the Chromium window, moved from `secrets/`, `cache/` and `server/`. `host/azure` is a stub for the hosted app: desktop-only operations throw `HostUnavailable`, "… is not available in the hosted app". `server/main.ts` picks one from `FOCUSTIME_HOST=local\|azure` (default local) and is the only file that builds a host. Core modules (`week/`, `plan/`, `actions/`, `sources/`, `store/`, and the `config/`, `log/` and `links/` files they reach) import only the interface; `host/boundary.test.ts` fails on an OS module, an implementation, `server/`, or a desktop program named in core. Why: the hosted app needs the same core with no machine under it, and a test keeps it that way |
| D418 | Done | A1: the local machine is the developer loop, not the product. `system/` (the service, the launcher, desktop entries and key bindings) is for development only, per its README. The service and desktop entries are templates `install.sh` fills in. `focustime-open` reads its window helpers from `~/.config/focustime/launcher.env` (data, never sourced; Chromium `--app` without it); on the developer's Omarchy machine it names `omarchy-launch-webapp` and `omarchy-launch-or-focus`, so behavior there is unchanged. Product code, docs and fixtures name no desktop, workstation, owner or real client (`fixtures/names.test.ts`; history stays in this log and `docs/azure/`). The New York and `?? "UTC"` fallbacks are gone, and Settings lists the browser's zones. Nothing changes for the developer: their file names America/New_York. `week/zone.test.ts` runs the week in Eastern, Central, Mountain and Pacific, in standard and daylight time and across the Nov 1, 2026 change. It fails on a Region/City zone name in product code (allowed: the Windows-to-IANA map, demo and style guide sample data, fixtures) or a read of the zone that skips `userZone`. `npm run test:zones` runs the suite under each zone. | D421 | Done | A2: sign-in is OpenID Connect against the Cooptimize tenant only (single tenant), auth code flow as a confidential client with PKCE, state and nonce, through `openid-client` (MIT). The ID token's signature is checked against the tenant's keys (`enableNonRepudiationChecks`, since the library skips it on the code flow by default), then issuer, audience, expiry, nonce and the tenant id. Why: one tenant is the product's audience, and a hand-rolled JWT check is the usual source of auth bugs. The sign-in asks for Graph `.default` with OpenID and offline access; source tokens ask only for scopes the registration grants (Calendars.ReadWrite covers reading), checked by a test against `infra/scripts/app-registration.sh` |
| D422 | Done | A2: sessions are server side, in the plan store's `sessions` table, with only a SHA-256 of the session id kept. 12 hours idle, 7 days absolute, 2 hours for a demo. `__Host-` cookies: the session HttpOnly SameSite=Lax, the CSRF token readable SameSite=Strict and sent as `X-CSRF` on every state change; Host and Origin checked; sign out ends the session and revokes nothing at Microsoft. Why: a session can be ended server side at once (Delete my data, sign out), and no token is ever in the browser |
| D423 | Done | A2: every plan table has `user_id`, first in every primary and unique key, with indexes led by it, and one filter: `store/scope.ts` rewrites every statement the store prepares to bind `$uid` from the session's person, and refuses what it can't scope (subqueries, CTEs, a statement naming `user_id`). No query path takes a user id from the client. Rows from before A2 were left unowned (`''`) by migration 24 and claimed by the store's owner (the recorded Microsoft account, else the config file's work email) at their first sign-in, never merged into someone who has rows. Why: one place to get right, and a test generated from the table list that fails for an unscoped new table |
| D424 | Done | A2: a person's settings are rows in the plan store (`user_settings`, one per top-level section of the settings text, so every existing Settings edit and its validation works unchanged), not a config file. App-level keys (the Dataverse environment and app registration, the Entra tenant and client, the Google client id, the demo switch, the public URL) come from the app config or environment and are added when a person's settings are read, never stored per person. The config file seeded its owner's rows once and is ignored after. Why: the hosted app has many people and no files; app keys must not drift per person |
| D425 | Done | A2: a person's secrets (Microsoft and Google refresh tokens, DevOps write PATs) are sealed in `user_secrets` with AES-256-GCM under one app data key from a KeyProvider (local: the secret store's `data-key` entry; hosted: wrapped by a Key Vault key in A4; tests: a fake), a fresh IV per value, and the person and account bound in as associated data. App client secrets stay in the host's secret store and can't be set from a person's Settings. Secrets never leave the server: not in exports, logs or the UI. Why: one key to protect and rotate instead of a vault entry per person, and a copied row is useless to another person |
| D426 | Done | A2: Delete my data is soft at once (sign-in refused, sessions ended, secrets cleared, background work stopped, a `deletions` row with only a hash of the id) and hard after 30 days (every row and the users row removed), both logged without the id. Export my data is every row of theirs in every user table except sealed secrets, without user id columns. Why: the retention decision (a grace period against mistakes, then nothing kept) |
| D427 | Done | A2: background work runs from one scheduler (`server/scheduler.ts`, fast pass 5 s, slow 30 s, no overlap, people side by side) over per-person tick code (`server/ticks.ts`) that reads what is due from the person's rows each pass, so a restart loses nothing; the timer pass runs first at start. A clicked notification's view is kept in the person's store for 2 minutes. Why: the hosted app restarts and scales to zero; a test stops and restarts a server on a store file and the timer and reminder fire once |
| D428 | Done | A3: the plan store is SQL Server everywhere except the demo: Azure SQL in the hosted app, a local SQL Server 2025 container (rootless Podman, `system/sqlserver/compose.yaml`) for the developer loop and the tests. One async store interface (`store/sql/conn.ts`) with two drivers: `mssql` (MIT) for SQL Server and `node:sqlite` for the demo's in-memory store, the fallback switch and `focustime export-local`. The store's SQL is written once in the common subset and translated to T-SQL in one place (`store/sql/tsql.ts`: parameters, MERGE upserts, TOP, bracketed keywords). The database comes from the app config's `[database]` or `FOCUSTIME_DB_*`, never per person. Why: one engine in production and development, so the tests that matter run on what ships; SQLite stays where nothing is shared |
| D429 | Done | A3: SQL Server's schema starts from one baseline migration, version 26 (`store/sql/baseline.ts`: SQLite's 26 migrations folded into the schema they end at), not 26 ports. Later changes are entries in `SQLSERVER_MIGRATIONS` run by `store/sql/migrate.ts`: a `schema_version` table, one transaction per migration with the version row in it, skipped when applied, a newer database refused. Types: FocusTime's stamps datetime2(7) in UTC (shown in the person's zone), dates date, ids int identities with the same numbers, flags bit, JSON nvarchar(max) with ISJSON, times a person or calendar gave kept as text with offset and zone (nvarchar(100)); user_id first in every key or index, with a foreign key to users. SQLite's migrations stay for the demo and the import path. Why: nothing was ever on SQL Server, so history would only replay SQLite's workarounds (table rebuilds, dedupes) |
| D430 | Done | A3: the hosted app reaches Azure SQL with an Entra token for its managed identity (`@azure/identity` `DefaultAzureCredential`, MIT; `managed_identity_client_id` for a user-assigned one), never a SQL password; a SQL login is allowed only with `auth = "sql"` for a local server, its password the host secret `sql-password`. Transient errors (40613, 40197, 40501, 49918 to 49920, 4060, 4221, 926, 615, 1205, dropped connections) retry the whole transaction with waits of 5, 10, 20 and 25 s (60 s in all); a paused serverless database's resume shows "Waking up the database" in the page (`/api/db-status`, answered from memory). Why: no secret to rotate in Azure, and a paused database is the normal first call of the day |
| D431 | Done | A3, the idle rule: the scheduler's passes run only while someone signed in has used FocusTime in the last 15 minutes. Otherwise it works out the next moment anyone's background work could be due (`plan/wake.ts`: a running timer's end and 5-minute recheck, meeting warnings, the reminder windows on working hours, week nudges, midnight), stores it (`scheduler_state`, one row) and makes no database call until then; a restart before that time reads the one row and sleeps. Hard deletes and the session sweep ride on the slow pass, at most hourly. Why: Azure SQL serverless pauses only when nothing queries it; early wakes are harmless, late ones aren't, so every candidate counts |
| D432 | Done | A3: the migration format is the export JSON (`focustime-export-1`, schema version 26, one person, no secrets): Settings > Data > Export my data, or `focustime export-local` from a SQLite store (opened read-only, immutable when nothing is writing it, never migrated). `focustime import --file --user <oid> [--dry-run]` loads it for that person in one transaction, parents first, ids kept, links within a table filled after, rows already there left alone (idempotent), refused when an id is someone else's or the file holds secrets. Secrets are re-sealed after the move (the owner's PATs and Google token copied from the keyring at the next sign-in). Why: one format a person can already download, readable without FocusTime, the same for A5's move to Azure and between environments |
| D433 | Done | A4: dev scales to zero, prod runs one replica always. The dev Container App runs 0 to 1 replicas, so an idle dev costs only storage and the paused database; prod (A5) runs min 1, max 1: the in-process scheduler (D427, D431) runs only while a replica is up, so a reminder or a timer end due while scaled to zero would wait for the next request. Max is 1 in both because the scheduler and the session sweep assume one replica. Single revision mode; probes on `/healthz` and `/readyz`, answered before the Host check and without a database call, so a probe never wakes a paused database |
| D434 | Done | A4: Azure SQL is Entra-only (no SQL logins, no password anywhere), its Entra admin the security group "FocusTime DB Admins" (the product owner, Josh Knox, Aaron Jennings, and each environment's deploy identity). The app's system identity is a contained database user with db_datareader, db_datawriter and EXECUTE on dbo, made by `scripts/db-user.ts` in the deploy workflow with the admin group's token, never by hand; the schema changes only through `focustime migrate` in the workflow. Public endpoint with "Allow Azure services" only; the deploy runner adds a rule for its own address and removes it at the end of the job. Why: no secret to leak or rotate, a group instead of a person as admin, and the app can't change its own schema |
| D435 | Done | A4: deploys use OIDC. Each environment has a user-assigned identity (`ft-dev-deploy` in FocusTime-dev) with a federated credential for this repo's main branch, Contributor and User Access Administrator on its resource group only, and membership of the DB admins group. GitHub holds repository variables (tenant, subscription, client id, resource group, group id), never a secret. The app's secrets live in Key Vault (RBAC mode, purge protection): `ft-entra-client-secret` (made by `app-registration.sh` through a pipe), `ft-google-client-secret` and `ft-dataverse-client-secret` (copied from the keyring by `copy-secrets.sh`, never printed), read by the Container App as Key Vault references; the data key is wrapped by the Key Vault key `ft-data-key`. Why: no long-lived credential to steal from GitHub or the image |
| D436 | Done | A4: dev's database is General Purpose serverless, 0.5 to 1 vCore, auto-pause after 60 minutes, 32 GB, local backup redundancy, 7 days of point-in-time restore, no zone redundancy. Why: dev is idle most of the day and serverless bills only storage while paused; the first request after a pause waits about a minute behind "Waking up the database" (D430). Prod's tier is decided in A5 from the A4 report's projection (serverless against Standard S0 and S1) |

A feature's estimate is ½ to 20 in half-block steps (the ± stepper moves half a block, the menu offers ½ to 10); both stores' `week_features.blocks` take halves (SQLite migration 27, SQL Server 29), whole-block estimates stay valid as they are, nothing is converted; the export reads schema 26 and 27. Why a loader and not a static import: browsers with Temporal shouldn't download it, and ES module imports run before any check in main.tsx could. |

Retry". Every save offers Undo in a small toast, which saves the previous value back the same way (the settings backup is kept as before). List Add and Remove (People to exclude, standing blocks, playlists) save at once, with Undo. Leaving Settings blurs the edited field and waits for its save; a field still showing an error keeps you there, and a save that fails after its form has closed comes back as a notice. Secrets keep one explicit button: DevOps write PATs "Check and save" (checked against the DevOps identity before storing), app secrets their own; their behavior is unchanged. Rollback and roll forward are the same workflow with another SHA; each run's summary names the one it replaced. The stub's `environment: production` went with it. |

Min 1 so the in-process scheduler (reminders, timer ends) keeps running; max 1 because the scheduler and the session sweep have no lease yet, and a second replica would send each reminder twice. A lease (one row in the plan store, renewed by the holder) comes before max 2. Dev stays 0 to 1. |

| D475 | Done | fix/vm-schedule (hotfix): FocusTime never modifies a VM's auto-shutdown schedule. Skip tonight turned the schedule off and relied on a next-morning restore to turn it back on; if the restore never ran, the VM stayed on. The schedule is the user's own, so FocusTime only reads it (the chip's "off at 7:30 PM"). Removed: Delay 1h, Delay 2h, Skip tonight, Restore (menu, Today's warning, the "Auto-shutdown is still changed" notification and its Restore button, the next-morning restore tick, `vm.restore`), and Stop (deallocate). The Azure client allows a fixed list of calls (`ALLOWED`, `sources/azure/client.ts`): Resource Graph query by owner tag, read the VM, read its instance view, read its auto-shutdown schedule, start, and read a start's progress. Anything else, any other method or path (every PUT, PATCH, DELETE and POST action on Microsoft.DevTestLab/schedules included), throws before a token is asked for or a request is sent. Restores left from earlier changes are never run; at server start each one still waiting is marked cancelled once (`store/vm-restores.ts`) and logged as refused in that person's Azure write log with what the schedule should be. Older write-log rows stay as history. A safe way to skip one shutdown is an open question (Q99) |
| D476 | Done | P1: the phone is for checking and doing; planning stays on a bigger screen. Under 600 px wide (`isPhoneWidth`, `usePhone` in `ui/viewport.ts`, and `@media (max-width: 599.98px)`) FocusTime has a bottom bar (Today, Week, Capture, ⋯ with Inbox, Roadmap, Focus, Settings, Guide, About) and a top bar of the mark, the VM chip as a dot and the avatar. Today › Now leads with one card (the block under way, its next pick, Start focus or the running timer with Done and +15, Mark done), then the day's blocks as the agenda, then the follow-ups due; This week's chips and Plan it links stay on desktop. Week is one day at a time (arrows, the day tabs, or a sideways swipe), today first, each half-day a card listing its meetings. Capture is a full-screen sheet with the same sort choices; panels open full screen with Back. Plan the week, Plan the day, Plan ahead, Roadmap and the big-screen Settings sections (Working hours, Standing blocks, Clients, Data) are read-only (inert) under "Planning works best on a bigger screen" with Copy link and Back; Check-in, Close out and the other Settings sections work. Chosen over a squeezed desktop: planning needs the week, the right pane and drag, which a phone can't hold. |

| D478 | Done | P1: the service worker caches the app shell only. `/sw.js` is served by the server (`server/pwa.ts` from `server/sw.js`) with the build's short sha as its cache name (`focustime-shell-<sha>`; a local build without one is named by a hash of its files) and the shell as the exact list of files the UI build wrote (the page, hashed scripts, styles and fonts, icons, the manifest). Only those paths are answered from the cache; /api and /auth are never intercepted, nothing is put in a cache after install, and page loads go to the network first (sign-in redirects, fresh builds), the cached shell only when that fails. Offline, one line says "You're offline. FocusTime needs a connection." (from the browser's offline state or a call that got no answer at all). Sign-out removes this device's push and clears every FocusTime cache. No offline data: a person's plan never sits in the browser. Registered on built pages only (the dev UI changes under Vite). |
| D479 | Done | P1: in-app notifications. What the notifier delivers, after its gate (switches, quiet hours, once per key, held during focus and flushed as one), also goes into the person's `notices` table (newest 50) and shows under ⋯ › Notifications with an unread dot on ⋯, and as a toast for 7 seconds while the app is open (the UI polls `notices.list` every 30 s while visible). Opening the list marks everything read; what was new keeps its mark while the list is open. A notice whose occasion was handled is marked done (Q91): the hosted host now keeps a shown notice pending until FocusTime closes it, as a desktop notification waits to be clicked, so the notifier's sweep can close it. |
| D480 | Done | P1: Web Push is optional per device and off until tapped. Settings › Notifications › Notify this device asks the browser's permission only on that tap (iPhone: only in FocusTime from the home screen), subscribes with the app's VAPID key, and the server keeps the subscription sealed with the data key in `push_devices` (bound to the person and a random device id the browser keeps); the UI sees only the device's label and dates, with Remove; sign-out removes this device. Sent with node:crypto only (RFC 8291 aes128gcm, RFC 8292 ES256; no library, checked against the RFC's example), only to Apple's, Google's, Mozilla's and Microsoft's push services (anything else is refused, so a subscription can't point the server elsewhere); 404 or 410 removes the device. The VAPID key is a Key Vault secret (ft-vapid-key, made by `infra/scripts/vapid-key.sh`, piped, never printed) read at run time with the app's identity rather than a Container Apps secret reference, so an environment without it deploys and says push isn't set up. Not in the demo or the developer loop. |
| D481 | Done | P1: no details in push by default. A push message passes through the browser maker's push service and shows on a lock screen, so it says only what kind of thing it is (Follow-ups due, Plan the day, Meeting soon), never a client name, a work item title, a meeting subject or a person; "Show details in notifications" (`[notify] push_details`, off) sends the notice's own words. The route is a view (with at most a date, an id or a Settings section). In-app notifications always show the full text: they are behind the sign-in. |

Read only with delegated Microsoft Graph Chat.ReadBasic, the least scope that lists my chats (GET /me/chats?$expand=members, group chats kept, paged by nextLink to 10 pages) and a chat's members (GET /chats/{id}/members): a user can consent to it, unlike ChatMember.Read (admin). lastMessagePreview and $orderby on it are never used, because they carry message text; "last activity" is the newer of lastUpdatedDateTime and my viewpoint.lastMessageReadDateTime. The chat's name and link come from Teams on add, never from the request. Members are cached (id, name, email, tenant) with their fetched-at time and read again when Settings › Clients opens and on Refresh, never on a timer. Logs carry only op, status, count and duration. "Open in Teams" uses the chat's webUrl when it is a teams.microsoft.com link, else the documented chat deep link. |

A member with no tenantId can't be shown to be ours, so it counts as external. `plan/chats.ts isExternal` is pure and table-tested. |

The sign-in asks for `.default`, so a new scope needs `infra/scripts/app-registration.sh` (now with Chat.ReadBasic) run with its dry run first; a test keeps the list and the script's GRAPH_SCOPES the same. |
| D487 | Done | fix/ci-speed: CI's tests run on runners in parallel, the unit and store tests (with SQL Server) on one and the browser tests (Chromium and WebKit) in four shards; the repo's runners have two cores, where vitest runs one file at a time, so one browser job took about ten minutes; the `test` job is the gate that needs both and records the tested tree for deploy-dev, as before. The browser tests build the UI once per run (test/e2e-build.ts, under a second) and every demo server serves that build instead of compiling the UI with Vite (`FOCUSTIME_E2E_DIST=dev` keeps Vite); the harness blocks service workers so a built run behaves like a dev one. On one four-core runner the suite took 16 to 18 minutes and browser waits timed out at random (Close out, first-run setup), on main as well as branches. e2e/capture-address.test.ts opened `?now=…/#/capture`, which made the pinned clock unreadable (a trailing slash), so that page ran on the real clock and depended on the time of day; it now keeps the pinned clock. The SQL Server 2025 container sometimes dies at start on these runners (a fatal error in sqlservr, PR #12 and #19); the wait step starts a dead one again, up to three starts. |

Setting a kind replaces that slot's chat; a chat belongs to one client and one kind. The picker puts the chats whose members suggest the slot's kind first (any external member, by tenant, suggests the customer chat; D485) and tags each Internal or With the client; the person decides. A Delivery team chat with someone from outside Cooptimize says so ("1 person from outside Cooptimize is in this internal chat"); a customer chat with no one from outside says so too. A row saved before T-2.1 takes the kind its members suggest when read, or the other one when that is taken. AP-1 will watch both chats for asks (the person's choice, Nov 3). |

External is a member whose tenantId isn't Cooptimize's, has none, or whose chat roles include guest (Graph doesn't say which tenant a guest's tenantId names, so the role decides). Members are read again when the Clients section opens only if older than a day, and on Refresh. Permissions (Graph docs and the tenant's own scope list, Nov 4): listing chats and members needs Chat.ReadBasic (user-consentable, granted tenant-wide on Nov 4); reading messages needs Chat.Read (user-consentable); ChatMember.Read needs admin consent and isn't needed. |
| D490 | Done | AP-1: Chat.Read is asked for by each person, not added to the app registration. The first time someone switches Read for asks on, FocusTime sends them to Microsoft with a sign-in that asks for `openid profile email offline_access https://graph.microsoft.com/Chat.Read` alone (Entra doesn't mix `.default` with named scopes of the same resource; `/auth/microsoft/login?consent=chat_read`, only with a signed-in, non-demo session) and back to Settings › Clients, where the chat is switched on. The refresh token that comes back serves every scope the person has allowed, so their other tokens keep working. Chosen over adding it to `.default` (everyone would be asked at sign-in for something few use) and over admin consent (not needed: Chat.Read is user-consentable). Settings › Connections lists it as "Missing" with where to allow it until then. Only the person's own delegated access; no app-only Teams permission. |
| D491 | Done | AP-1: Asks are read only and rule-based, with no AI. FocusTime reads a client chat's new messages only after its person switches Read for asks on for that chat (off by default), and never posts, reacts or marks anything read. A message is an ask when its author is external (another tenant, no tenant, or a guest, D489) and it isn't mine, it isn't only an acknowledgement (the person's list, "thanks", "ok"… with emoji or punctuation), and one of these matches, in this order: it @mentions a Cooptimizer in the chat, it has a question mark, it uses a request phrase (the person's list, whole words, any case), or it has an attachment (an inline image or a shared file; cards and quoted replies aren't). Each ask records the rule that matched and shows it ("Matched: asks a question"). Internal messages aren't asks in AP-1 (promises are AP-2). `plan/asks.ts`, table-tested. |
| D492 | Done | AP-1: what is stored, and for how long. Per person and per chat: message id, chat id, client, author id, author tenant id, created time, plain text (HTML stripped, at most 28,000 characters), has image, has file, reply-to id; nothing else from Graph (no names, reactions, subjects, URLs or attachments themselves). Messages that didn't become asks are deleted 14 days after they were written, by a once-a-day cleanup in the scheduler; asks keep their message, with the resolution (created, linked, handled with a note, or dismissed). Delete stored messages (Settings › Clients, per client) and removing a chat delete its text and asks at once; DevOps items made from them stay. Chat text is client data: it shows only on that client's ask cards and in Show context (the 5 earlier messages of the same chat), never in logs, Application Insights or error messages, which carry counts and kinds only (not even chat ids). |
| D493 | Done | AP-1: polling only in working hours. Each chat with Read for asks on is read every N minutes (`[asks] poll_minutes`, default 5, 1 to 60) by the scheduler's slow pass, only inside the person's working hours on days that aren't PTO, asking Graph for messages whose lastModifiedDateTime is after the newest one seen (`$filter` with `$orderby` on the same field, descending, 50 a page, at most 5 pages), starting from the moment it was switched on (no backfill). Refresh in Inbox › Asks reads at once, any time. A 429 or 503 waits out Retry-After (capped at an hour), else doubles from a minute; a failing chat says why on its Settings line and Connections and never stops the others. The idle rule (D431) wakes the scheduler for the next read only while in working hours. About 12 Graph calls per chat per hour at the default. |
| D494 | Done | AP-1: resolving an ask. Create bug, story or feature goes through the write framework (`ask_create`, preview, confirm, read back, audit log, Undo) with that client's write PAT: the type is the process's bug type (bugWorkItems), its requirement type or its lowest portfolio type; the title is the message's first line (at most 80 characters, cut at a word); the description, or a Bug's Repro Steps where the type has them, holds the text, who wrote it, when, and the Teams link; assigned to me; no parent, so the project's default area and iteration. Idempotent: a create from an ask that may have landed is retried from the audit log (it finds the item by type, title, me and date) and a second preview is refused; once confirmed the ask is resolved as created, and Undo (Removed) opens it again. Without the client's write PAT the card says "Add DevOps write access for <client> in Settings" and nothing is sent. Link searches that client's open items and stores the link only; Handled (an optional note) and Dismiss are local; each has Undo. |
| D495 | Done | AI-0: one provider adapter, structured output only. Every AI request goes through `ai/service.ts` `run(task, input, schema)`, which returns a value checked against the task's JSON schema or a typed error (`AiError`, one plain message per kind); output that fails the schema is never shown as if it worked and is never retried. The schema must be one OpenAI's strict mode takes (root object, every property required, `additionalProperties: false`). One adapter: OpenAI's Responses API (`POST /v1/responses`, recommended for new projects) with `text.format` json_schema `strict: true` and `store: false` (OpenAI keeps no application state; its abuse monitoring logs are kept up to 30 days, and API data isn't used for training). A stub provider (`ai/stub.ts`, no network) answers in the developer loop and CI unless a key is configured. The model, reasoning effort, timeout, max output tokens per task, prices per token and the app cap are app settings (`[ai]` in the app config, `FOCUSTIME_AI_*`), defaulting to gpt-6-luna at $0.10 / $0.01 cached / $0.50 per million tokens with low effort. One retry with backoff on a transient error (429 that isn't a quota, 408, 409, 5xx, a network failure), waiting Retry-After when given (up to 20 s, longer fails as busy); never on a quota error, a timeout or a bad answer. The key: Key Vault ft-openai-key per environment (infra/scripts/openai-key.sh), read at run time with the app's identity; the keyring entry openai-api-key in the developer loop. No key: "AI isn't set up", and nothing else changes. Verified against developers.openai.com/api/docs on Nov 4. |
| D496 | Done | AI-0: one client per request, enforced in `ai/` (`ai/guard.ts`), not by callers. Every task declares its client, or "none" for an internal-only task. Before anything is sent, a deterministic check of the instructions and input refuses another client's key, Dataverse or display name, DevOps project or work item id (from what FocusTime already read; whole words, any case); for "none", every client is another client. It also refuses token, key and secret shapes (Authorization headers, bearer tokens, JWTs, OpenAI, DevOps, GitHub, Google and AWS keys, Entra client secrets, private keys, password and key assignments, SAS signatures, long random strings), the person's VM name and Azure resource ids, and any email address whose domain isn't Cooptimize's. A refusal is logged with its kind and nothing is sent. |
| D497 | Done | AI-0: both switches off by default. A request runs only when the person's Use AI (Settings › AI, `[ai] use`) is on and, for a client task, that client's Allow AI (Settings › Clients, `[ai] allowed_clients`) is on; a "none" task needs only Use AI. Allow AI saves one client at a time, so two quick switches never undo each other. The demo has no AI. |
| D498 | Done | AI-0: monthly budget caps, per person and per app. A person's cap (`[ai] monthly_cap_usd`, default 10 USD, 0 to 1000) against the sum of their own request log this calendar month (UTC); the app's cap (app setting `app_cap_usd`, default 100 USD) against `ai_app_spend`, an app table with one running total per month that every request's cost is added to. At or over either: "AI budget reached for this month", logged, nothing sent; it starts again on the 1st. Cost is estimated from the usage OpenAI reports (cached input at its own price, reasoning inside output) in millionths of a dollar, and counted even when the answer was unusable. OpenAI's own project spend limit stays as a second stop (runbook). |
| D499 | Done | AI-0: no prompt or answer text is stored or logged. `ai_requests` (a user table) keeps per request: when, the task's fixed name, its client (null for "none"), model, input and output tokens, estimated cost, latency, outcome (ok, refused, invalid, provider_error, over_budget) and the error kind; never the instructions, the input or the answer. Application Insights gets the task, outcome, tokens and latency only. Settings › AI shows the last 20 rows. Test AI shows only OK with time and cost, never the answer. Requests stopped by a switch or a missing key aren't logged (nothing was attempted). |
| D500 | Done | AP-2: the AI guard refuses an item id only when it is known to belong to a different client. The ids come from a persisted map (`devops_item_clients`, a user table: client, item id, first seen) written whenever DevOps is read live (never from a cached fallback), merged with the last read in memory, so it survives a restart. An id the map doesn't know, or one that belongs to the request's own client, passes; numbers in dates, times, amounts, decimals and percentages are never taken for item ids. Before, any 3+ digit number that matched another client's id stopped the request, which refused ordinary messages with years or amounts. |
| D501 | Done | AP-2: AI sorts only ask candidates, never every message. One `asks.classify` request per new ask the AP-1 rules picked out, only with Use AI and that client's Allow AI on. The context is that client's only: the message, up to 5 earlier messages of the same chat with authors as "client", "Cooptimize" or "me" (never names; email addresses replaced with "[email address]"), the client's process (Agile, Scrum, CMMI or Basic, read from its item types, never guessed) with its type and state names, and up to 50 open items (id, title, type, state; most recently changed first). The answer is strict JSON: kind (bug, request, question, incident, none), urgency (now, this_week, backlog), item_type, a title of at most 80 characters, a description, likely_existing_item (an id from the list with a confidence, or null) and a reason. An id that wasn't in the list rejects the whole answer ("the AI's answer wasn't usable"). |
| D502 | Done | AP-2: AI drafts never write. Create from a sorted ask prefills the type, title and description (the message, author, time and Teams link always go with it) and Link prefills the likely item, but every DevOps write still goes through the write framework's preview and confirm (D475's rule for writes is unchanged). "Looks like #id <title>" shows only at confidence 0.6 or more. |
| D503 | Done | AP-2: an ask the AI calls "none" is hidden, not deleted: it is marked sorted, leaves the open count, and is listed under "Hidden by AI (N)" with "Show it anyway". Its message is kept like any ask (the 14-day rule applies only to messages that never became asks). When AI is off, over budget, refused or failed, the card is the AP-1 card with "Not sorted: <plain reason>" and a Sort again; nothing retries on its own, so a failing provider costs one request per ask. |
| D504 | Done | AP-2: an open ask sorted with urgency "now" is a walk-on on Today's attention line ("Walked in", the client, the AI's title, Start focus adds it as a walk-on pick to the block under way and starts focus) and one in-app notification (kind "Urgent asks", key per message) through the notifier's gate, held during focus unless Settings › Notifications "Let urgent asks through" is on (off by default). Urgency windows: now lasts 2 hours, this_week until the end of Friday in the person's zone, backlog never ends; an open ask past its window counts in Check-in's "Walked in, not resolved (N)", which links to Asks. Only asks found by the scheduler's read notify; Refresh shows them without a notification. |
| D505 | Done | AP-2: Promises. The person's own messages in chats read for asks that match a commitment phrase (editable per person, `[asks] promise_phrases`; "I'll", "I will", "let me", "I can", "by Friday", "tomorrow", "tonight", "end of day" and "next week" by default) become promises in Inbox › Promises. With AI on for the client, `promises.extract` (the same context, plus today's date and the chat members' display names so it can say to whom; never their addresses) returns is_promise, what, due_hint (a date in the person's zone, kept only from the message's day to 90 days on), to_whom (only a name from the chat), answers_message_id (only an offered earlier message) and a reason; "not a promise" makes no card. Without AI: the rule alone, what is the first line, no due date. The ask a promise answers shows "Promised: <what>". |
| D506 | Done | AP-2: a promise closes only by its follow-up or its linked item. Make it a follow-up uses the existing follow-up model (who, what and due prefilled, channel Teams); the promise closes when that follow-up is closed as done. Link ties it to a DevOps item; it closes when the item's state is done in DevOps. Done and Dismiss close it by hand, each with Undo. FocusTime never matches a later message to a promise on its own (AP-3 may suggest it). |
| D507 | Done | AP-2: cost. Only candidates are sent: at 50 external messages a day of which 10 are ask candidates, about 10 classify requests of roughly 3,000 to 4,000 input and 300 to 500 output tokens each at gpt-6-luna's list prices (0.10 USD per million in, 0.50 out) is about 0.0065 USD a day, plus a few promise requests of the same size, so under one cent a day per person and chat, well inside the 10 USD monthly default. Nothing new logs text: the request log keeps counts and cost (D499). |
| D508 | Done | Risk rules: FocusTime's time boxes and events with no one else aren't meetings. A time box on Outlook (its tb- marker; recorded ones were already removed before the week build, step 12) and an event I organize with no other attendees (the editing tier "Mine" rule) set `CalEvent.notMeeting` ("timebox" or "solo"). Meeting load doesn't count them, and the no-focus-window rule treats them as free. Only the risk rules change: the slot's freeGaps and longestFreeMinutes, which Plan the day, fit, Check-in and Timebox my day use, still treat them as taken, so nothing is planned over a hold like a dentist appointment. The Outlook read now selects isOrganizer and attendees (only the word is kept, never addresses; nothing new is cached) and, on a Calendars.ReadWrite read token (the hosted app), expands the marker; Graph needs Calendars.Read or more for extended properties, so the developer loop's ReadBasic read doesn't ask and sees only recorded time boxes. D470's "earlier" events (the local app's time boxes) are now not meetings for the risk rules; their editing tier is unchanged. Thresholds and Settings are unchanged. |
| D509 | Done | F1: ⋯ › Send feedback (and Send feedback in Commands) creates one story in Cooptimize's DevOps, as a child of Feature #496 in Cooptimize Internal. Write (Idea, Problem or Question; one text box; "Include where I am", ticked), then a preview of the exact title, description and tags, held on the server for 10 minutes so what is sent is what was shown; then Send. The story type is read from the process (the requirement backlog's first type), never assumed. Title: the type and the first line, up to 80 characters. Area: the feature's. Iteration: the project's default team's default iteration, else the project. Tags: "FocusTime feedback" and the type. The parent link is in the same create call, and the item is read back before "Sent. Thanks." with a link to #N. A failure says so plainly with Copy text and is never retried on its own. |
| D510 | Done | F1: nothing from a person's plan, clients, work items or screen goes in feedback. The payload is built only from what they typed, their name and email, the optional where lines (the view's name, ⋯ › About's version line, the browser and the screen size) and the environment; the action refuses any other input field. A test sends from a person with clients, plans and work items and checks none of it is in what was created. |
| D511 | Done | F1: feedback is created by the app's own managed identity (ft-dev-app, ft-prod-app), with a bearer token for Azure DevOps that is never logged, kept or returned. Each identity is added to the cooptimize organization at Basic (Stakeholder can't set an iteration path) and to Cooptimize Internal's Contributors (runbook). A setup check at start and in Settings > Connections (read only) confirms #496 exists, is a Feature, and that the identity may create a child story (a validateOnly create, nothing saved). If not, the dialog still opens and says "Feedback isn't set up yet. Message the FocusTime team in Teams." with Copy text. |
| D512 | Done | F1: environments. App-level settings (FOCUSTIME_FEEDBACK_ORG, _PROJECT, _PARENT_ID, _LIVE), set by the Bicep only. Prod: live. Dev: preview only, unless deploy-dev is run by hand with feedback_live ticked, which lasts until the next deploy; dev titles start "[dev]". The developer loop is preview only and checks nothing. The demo sends to a pretend backlog in memory and says so; nothing leaves FocusTime. |
| D513 | Done | F1: at most 10 sends per person per day (their local day), counted before the create so a send that reached DevOps counts whatever happened next. At the limit the preview and Send say so plainly and point to Teams. |
| D514 | Done | Shared polling, Option A: one job reads every registered client chat for everyone, inside the Container App's scheduler (`actions/request-poll.ts`, run at the start of each slow pass before anyone's ticks, `server/scheduler.ts` `jobs`). A chat is read every 5 minutes while any of its followers (FocusTime users who are members of the chat) is inside their own working hours, each in their own zone and week, so a weekend counts only when someone works it; never outside the union, and the first read of the day catches up (Graph is asked for everything changed after the newest lastModifiedDateTime stored). PTO no longer pauses a chat: someone else on it may be working. The job's next due time joins the idle rule's wake (start of the next union span when outside), so SQL serverless stays awake through the union of working hours and pauses outside it; no new Azure resources. Background polling runs in prod; dev and the developer loop read on Refresh only (`FOCUSTIME_REQUESTS_POLL=on|off` overrides). D431's text is left as it was, at the user's request. |

Every add, relabel and removal is logged in `client_chat_changes` (when, who, client, chat id, what; never a topic or a member). A chat's messages and Requests are shown only to a member of the chat (by Entra object id, from its member list, read again daily) who follows its client; when the followed clients can't be read (Dataverse down), membership alone. Read for asks and its per-person switch are gone. |
| D516 | Done | Shared message store: `teams_messages`, keyed by (chat id, message id), with client, sender id, display name and tenant, created and last modified times, the raw HTML body (at most 64,000 characters, stored, not rendered) and its plain text, attachment flags, reply-to id, mentions (ids and names) and fetched-at. Each message is stored once whoever's sign-in read it; a copy with a newer lastModifiedDateTime replaces the body, text, attachments and mentions (an edit) and never makes a second Request; an older or equal copy changes nothing. Nothing created before the chat was registered is kept. Unmatched messages are deleted after 14 days; a message that became a Request, or someone's promise (or the request a promise answers), stays. A Request the AI hid as needing nothing (open, not shown anyway) goes with its message under the 14-day rule, as AP-2's code and test did (D503's wording said it was kept; the code wins). |
| D517 | Done | Token selection: the registering person's delegated token first (while they are still a follower), then the other followers' by name. A refused token (signed out, consent missing, 401, 403, 404) moves to the next; a throttle (429, 503 with Retry-After) or an unreachable Teams waits out the backoff without trying anyone else. When none works the chat says "Needs reconnecting" with its last successful fetch kept, no exception escapes, and the other chats still poll. The token's owner is recorded on the chat (`token_user`) and shown as "Polling via <name>" when it isn't the registering person. Tokens are never kept by the job: each person's existing account is used (access tokens in memory per person, refresh tokens sealed in their secrets), built for the cycle. Each cycle logs chats polled, messages fetched, candidates created, failures and the token owners by name (the one exception to "no person in a log", asked for by the user); never a chat id, topic, member or message text. |

Anyone who sees the chat resolves it for everyone, by name ("Dismissed by Ben"). The candidate rules run in the job with the token owner's `[asks]` phrases; the AI sort runs once per new Request under the token owner's Use AI, Allow AI and caps and is saved on the shared row with whose settings sorted it (Sort again uses the presser's). Promises stay each person's own: the job makes one only for a FocusTime user's own message, under their own phrases, AI and plan store. Messages are deduplicated by message id. A message several people resolved takes the state of the most recent action (its person as resolved by); the AI sort comes from the latest sort. The per-person tables (`delivery_chats`, `chat_messages`, `asks`) stay until a later release drops them with `safe-drop`; their daily 14-day cleanup still runs. No Dataverse change. |
