| D586 | Done | DV-1: one changed-since check per client per tick. A WIQL on `[System.ChangedDate] >= <since>` with `timePrecision=true`, covering all of a client's projects in one query (`[System.TeamProject] IN (...)`; one project uses `=`), then a batch read (200 ids a call, `errorPolicy: omit`) of only the changed ids. The watermark is the WIQL answer's `asOf`, not the newest ChangedDate seen, so a quiet project doesn't re-read the same item every minute; `since` is the watermark less 2 minutes (`OVERLAP_MS`), and an item whose stored `System.Rev` is equal or higher is skipped, so the overlap costs no change log rows. A result at the WIQL cap (20,000) reloads the scope next time. The first load reads the scope (open items, or changed in the last 90 days) without change log rows. |
| D587 | Done | DV-1: the interval. A Rule, "DevOps check interval (minutes)" (`devops.poll_minutes`, 1 by default, 1 to 30) in `ORG_SPECS`: in working hours (the union of the client's followers' own hours, `inUnion` as the Teams poller, D514). Outside them every 10 minutes and only while someone has used FocusTime in the last few minutes; otherwise no check at all and `wake()` returns the next start of hours, so the database can pause (D431). The first check after catches up from the watermark in batches of 200. The Rule isn't in the Admin form yet (ADM-2 owns the screen); it is settable through `admin.set` and audited. |
| D588 | Done | DV-1: limits (`plan/devops-pace.ts`; learn.microsoft.com/azure/devops/integrate/concepts/rate-limits, checked Nov 5). Every response's headers are read (Retry-After, X-RateLimit-Delay, -Limit, -Remaining, -Cost). A 429 or 503, a delay, or less than 20% of the budget left doubles the client's interval (up to 15 minutes, or the Rule if longer); five calm checks halve it back, a step at a time. Retry-After is honored even on a 200, and holds even Check now and Refresh; a refusal without it waits 60 s. One limiter per client org (`createLimiter`): a client that slows down never touches another. A refused token (401 or 403) is tried again every 30 minutes, after one re-read of the token. |
| D589 | Done | DV-1: the daily reconcile. It runs at the first check of the day rather than at night, so it never wakes a paused database. |

Every browser test serves the run's one UI build (`test/e2e-build.ts`): `e2e/live.ts` (the real-session tests: setup, admin, AI, conversations and the rest) still compiled the UI through Vite per file, which on CI's two cores took most of each step's 60 s and summed past the admin test's 240 s. Service workers are blocked in every context. A timeout is raised only with a written reason, and in the describe option, not `vi.setConfig` (`forEachDriver(..., { timeout })`: SQL Server's describe option used to win over the file's 60 s). | D608 | Done | HK-1: telemetry fields. Kept the hand-written sender (`log/appinsights.ts`, no SDK or dependency: the closed fields are the redaction boundary). Every request and every background job run is an operation (`log/operation.ts`, AsyncLocalStorage) with W3C ids: a request continues a valid incoming `traceparent`, else starts a trace; each job run starts its own. Every trace, dependency and exception made inside one carries its `ai.operation.id`, its span as parent, and its name. Requests are named `<METHOD> <route>`, `POST /api/action <action>` for a registered action. Jobs are request items with `kind=job`, the outcome as result code (ok, failed, partial, skipped), counts as measurements and short flags: `devops_check` (or `devops_reconcile` for a client's first check of the day: changed, removed, changes, calls; slowed yes or no; load full or changed), `requests_poll` and `requests_refresh` (polled, messages, candidates, promises, failures, conversations, unsorted, aiCalls: the AI pipeline's counts), `conversations_upkeep`, `people_refresh`. A client appears only as `clientRef(key)`, `c-` and 10 hex of a SHA-256 (a key is the client's Dataverse nickname or name); `focustime client-ref <key>` prints it to search by. Sampling stays at 100%. Expected ingest per month, from dev's and prod's last 3 days (about 25 MB each, about 0.25 GB a month, a third of it console logs) plus DevOps checks as jobs (about 2.5 KB a check with its trace and two dependencies; about 10 clients each minute in working hours): prod about 0.6 GB, dev about 0.3 GB (it sleeps outside use). Why: serverless billed the 0.5 vCore floor ($0.626 an hour) for every awake hour while the work was tiny (prod's busiest day 9.8 CPU seconds, worst minute 0.06 vCore; dev's worst minute, a migration, 0.09 vCore), so dev ran $6 to $7.50 a day and prod $6.60 on a full day; S0 is $0.4839 a day and S1 $0.9677 (Central US list, Nov 5). S0 is about 0.1 vCore and S1 about 0.2 (Microsoft's rule: 100 Standard DTU need at least 1 vCore); the app's pool is at most 10 against 60 and 90 workers. Lacking columnstore and in-memory OLTP doesn't matter (FocusTime uses neither). No app change: the tier switch drops connections for under 30 s and the driver already retries 40613, 40197 and dropped sockets for a minute. Point-in-time restore stays dev 7, prod 14. The idle rule (D431) and DV-1's outside-hours behavior stay: they still save container time and Graph and DevOps calls, though the database no longer pauses. S2 when DTU stays over 80% in working hours, for a large import or a restore (runbook). Rollback: revert the tier commit (sql.bicep's comment has the serverless values). |

## CV-4

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D710 | Done | CV-4 (the product owner's direction): FocusTime keeps no chat images. An image is read from Teams only when needed, always with a chat member's own delegated Chat.Read: on view (`GET /media/chat-images/<id>` keeps its session, member and client checks, FocusTime's own being the main gate, then the viewer's token), for the AI step, or for a DevOps create. On view the stripped bytes are held in memory only (`ImageCache`), 5 minutes per image and viewer (never shared between viewers), about 64 MB in all, the oldest out first; a failure is remembered 5 minutes too; the response is `no-store`. One retry on a throttle (Retry-After, 5 seconds at most). A refused token or a removed message is 404, and the page shows "Image in Teams" with Open in Teams (the message's deep link). The row keeps only the type and size read from the bytes, a skipped reason (not a PNG, JPEG or GIF, over 10 MB) and the AI's description. The poll no longer reads images. Replaces D623's storage. |
| D711 | Done | CV-4: the migration `20261107T1400_images_no_bytes` sets bytes NULL on rows that had them, puts them back to pending (attempts 0) and keeps their type and size; it adds `description` (200) and `described_at`. It logs only how many it cleared (`images_bytes_cleared`): named migrations take an optional `count` (both runners), as the numbered ones did. The bytes column is dropped in a later release (D468, Q-CV4-1); until then `store/images-bytes.test.ts` fails if any code writes it. Named 1400 on Nov 7 though the clock read Nov 5 14:00 UTC: it alters CV-2's table, so it must sort after `20261106T1020`, and main's newest was `20261107T1347`. |
| D712 | Done | CV-4: Admin › AI's per-client "Send attachments to AI" is a live Rule (`attachments_ai`, off by default). When it is on and AI may run for the client, the poll reads the batch's new images with the same token that read the chat, strips them, and adds them to the same `conversations.update` request: one user message, the input JSON, then each image after a fixed label written by code ("Image i1, in message m3:") as `input_image` (a data URL, `detail: "high"`), `store: false`. Because the batch is one JSON document, "after its message" is by label, not position. At most 4 a message and 10 a call; animated GIFs (OpenAI takes still ones only) and images already described aren't sent; the rest get no description. The service refuses anything but PNG, JPEG or GIF data URLs, or more than 10 (`bad_task`), before a request. The schema asks for `images` only when images go. Switch off, AI off, or the chat's daily cap: nothing is read or sent, only counted (`chat_images_ai` sent and not_sent). The guard reads text only and can't inspect images: the switch is the consent, and the Guide and Admin › AI say so. |
| D713 | Done | CV-4: an image's description is at most 120 characters, one line, with email addresses and secret shapes removed; an image ref the request didn't send is dropped (never stored), without rejecting the rest of the answer. Stored on the image row; shown on the card ("3 images: <the first description>") and under the thumbnail, only to members of the chat (the viewer's checks); never in logs, exports or Application Insights. A failed request stores none. |
| D714 | Done | CV-4: creating a DevOps item from a conversation or a promise (`conversation_create`) offers the images of the messages going with it, an internal chat's only with "include internal": the preview lists them as thumbnails with a checkbox each, all ticked (10 at most), and re-previews with `images` when one is unticked. On confirm, before the create, each ticked image is read with the creator's own token (the member check again), kept in memory only, stripped, and uploaded with the client's write PAT (`POST _apis/wit/attachments?fileName=&uploadType=simple`, `application/octet-stream`, 7.1); each becomes an AttachedFile relation in the same create patch with the comment "From Teams, <name>, <date time>", and the description gains "Attached: N images from the chat". The patch and an Attachments line per image (image id, file name, size, attachment id and URL, never bytes) are saved on the log entry before the create, so Retry never uploads again. An image that can't be read or uploaded is reported and left out; the item is still created. The read-back checks every relation. 10 MB an image, 10 a create. |
| D715 | Done | CV-4: undoing such a create sets it to Removed and removes its AttachedFile relations in the same patch (by index, highest first, under `test /rev`); the read-back checks they are gone. The undo's preview and result say the files stay in DevOps, unlinked: the attachments API has no delete. |
| D716 | Done | CV-4: cost. Image tokens are counted in the request's input tokens (the request log and the app's cap, as before). At the default gpt-6-luna prices ($0.10 per million input) and a patch multiplier of 1.2 (assumed, Q-CV4-3): a 1024 x 1024 screenshot is about 1,229 tokens, about $0.00012; a full-HD one about 2,448 tokens, $0.00025; a call's 10 images at most about $0.003. The test run (stub, 1,229 tokens an image) logged 2 images as 2,458 tokens beyond the text. |

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D717 | Done | AH-3: Ahead's layout follows the window width divided by the text scale (so Larger text at 1920 lays out like a narrower window): wide from 1400 (separate Start and Target), Dates from 1200 (one "Oct 19 → Nov 13" column), Readiness as dots with a tooltip and screen-reader words from 900, two-line cards below 900 and on the phone, with the filters wrapping. The Item column is at least 320 px, its title on one or two lines with the #id beside it; the other columns are compact and fixed (dates without the weekday, which is in the tooltip; the size on one line, "3 SP", with the field's name in the tooltip). The Gantt's label column uses the same rule (26% of the width, 240 to 440 px scaled). |
| D718 | Done | AH-3: the Fix next side pane is removed; Fix next is its own mode (`#/ahead/fix`) and its count shows in the mode switch ("Fix next · 7") at every width, so below 1600 nothing else is needed (the user's answer). |

Each client can turn any of the four off with the client Rule `fix_gaps` in Admin › Clients (all on by default). |

Lower backlog items never show. An item never ranked in DevOps (no order value) isn't near the top. |
| D721 | Done | AH-3: Fix next is ranked by the process's order field (StackRank for Agile, Basic and CMMI, BacklogPriority for Scrum, ascending, read from processconfiguration as before), ties by target date, then id. A project with no order field is ranked by target date with the note "<project> has no backlog order, so these are ranked by target date." One entry per item with all its gaps; its action is the first gap. Grouped client › epic › feature with TypeIcons (ancestors shown as headers when they aren't entries), the top 10 per client, then Show more. Fix next ignores Ahead's filters (it is its own queue); Skip still puts an item aside for the session. |

Assigning a person in Ahead writes their linked login on that client. Leads and Team are by person already, so they follow. |

"Not them" and Remove record an `unlink` row, which hides a Dataverse identity (Dataverse is read-only) and stops that suggestion coming back. Stored in the app table `client_logins`. ## GD-1

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D733 | Done | GD-1: a request whose client is Cooptimize Internal may name clients: the guard's three client rules (names and nicknames, DevOps projects, work item ids) don't apply to it, in what is sent or in what comes back. Secret shapes, the VM and outside email addresses still refuse it. Internal is known only by clientAccounts()'s why "internal" (`isInternalKey`, `actions/client-accounts.ts`), never by its name; `GuardInput.internal` is optional, so `guard()`'s callers keep working. |
| D734 | Done | GD-1: an AI answer passes the client rule too (the request's client, Internal exempt). A refused answer was sent and paid for: logged as refused with its tokens, never shown, never retried ("The AI's answer mentions another client, so nothing is shown"). Only the client rule is checked on answers: an outside email address or a secret shape in an answer can only come from an image (the text was guarded), and CV-4 already scrubs those from what it keeps; refusing the whole answer for them would leave every chat with such a screenshot unsorted. |
| D735 | Done | GD-1: before any write into a client's DevOps (create, Break down's stories, comment, a state change's comment, description), the text FocusTime would write (title, description, acceptance criteria, repro steps, comment) is checked against the person's other clients; one named refuses the preview with "This mentions <other client>; remove it before saving to <client>'s DevOps" (code `other_client`). The other client's name appears only in that response, never in a log or the audit log (nothing is written). Internal's DevOps may name clients. Undos only put back what was there and aren't checked. |
| D736 | Done | GD-1: a guard refusal logs one line (`ai guard`, kind `<task>:<input or output>:<rule>`, and the matched client entry as its one-way client ref), never the text, so a refusal can be traced to the rule and the client (`focustime client-ref <key>`). Client keys compare without case in the guard, so a client's own names never count as another client's. |
| D737 | Done | GD-1: chat messages the guard refused before GD-1 (reason "refused_client", which caught every Internal chat) are tried once more on the chat's next poll, as their own batch so they never hold back new messages; Sort unsorted also retries them. Refusals from GD-1 on are stored as "other_client" (same words in Inbox), so a real mention is retried only by Sort unsorted, never on every poll. |
| D738 | Done | TB-1: the bot uses the Teams SDK 2.1.1 (`@microsoft/teams.apps`, `.api`, `.cards`, exact versions, lockfile committed), Microsoft's framework for Teams-only apps; the Bot Framework SDK is retired (support ended Dec 31, 2025) and the Microsoft 365 Agents SDK is for agents across several surfaces. Its packages are used only in `server/teams/`. It is mounted on FocusTime's own server through its adapter seam: `app.initialize()`, never `app.start()`, no second port (the product owner's OK, Nov 5). |
| D739 | Done | TB-1: the SDK's `@azure/msal-node` 5.6.0 has a high advisory (GHSA-xcx6-jx8p-x3h5, in the authorization code flow, which the bot never uses). An npm override points it at 7.1.2, the patched copy `@azure/identity` already brings; `npm audit` is clean and the SDK's paths the bot uses (a token provider, no MSAL) are unchanged. |
| D740 | Done | TB-1: the bot's credential is a user-assigned managed identity (`ft-<env>-bot-id`, msaAppType UserAssignedMSI, single tenant), not a client secret: nothing in Key Vault, nothing to rotate, no new app registration. The "FocusTime" registration stays for sign-in only. All of it is Bicep through the normal deploys (the product owner, Nov 5). |
| D741 | Done | TB-1: incoming activities are verified by the SDK (Bot Framework OpenID keys, issuer `https://api.botframework.com`, audience the bot's app id, RS256, expiry with 5 minutes' skew, the `serviceurl` claim matching the activity); a failure is a 401 before FocusTime reads anything. The SDK doesn't check key endorsements; FocusTime's gate requires `channelId` msteams, pins Cooptimize's tenant (the SDK's Entra-token path takes the tenant from the token), and sends replies only to Teams' connector hosts. |
| D742 | Done | TB-1: who may talk to the bot: the sender's Entra object id must be a FocusTime user (signed in once, not deleted, not a guest UPN) and a Cooptimizer by `cooptimizers()` (no guests, no automation accounts, inside the domains Rule). Others get "FocusTime is for Cooptimize accounts."; someone not signed in yet gets a link to sign in once; a group chat or channel gets "FocusTime only works in 1:1 chats" (the manifest is personal scope only, so this is a guard). |
| D743 | Done | TB-1: the SDK's logger is replaced by one that drops every argument (it would log whole activities at debug); only `teams_sdk warn|error` reach the log. Bot records carry a kind and counts, never text, people, conversation ids, tokens or headers (a test sends a message and checks the log). |
| D744 | Done | TB-1: Teams waits 10 to 15 seconds and dev takes about 30 to wake from zero. The endpoint answers 200 as soon as the token and gate pass, then sends typing, the reply, and "On it…" first when the assistant takes over 2.5 s; Teams' redeliveries are handled once by activity id. One person's turns run one at a time in the order they arrived, so replies come back in order (one replica). On dev the first message after it sleeps may be late or lost (send it again; documented); dev stays at zero (the product owner, Nov 5). |
| D745 | Done | TB-1: a proposal card is Adaptive Card 1.5 with the preview (title, who sees it, each field before → after, an optional note) and Confirm and Cancel as `Action.Execute` (an `Action.Submit` fallback). The row in `teams_bot_cards` is written before the card is sent; a tap claims it (open → running) in one UPDATE, so `assistant.confirm` runs once however often it is tapped and later taps get the stored outcome. Outcomes: Done (with Undo and links into the app), Couldn't do it (with the reason), Expired (15 minutes, AS-1's expiry), Cancelled, or, on `changed`, the new preview on the same card. A confirm over 8 s answers "Working on it…" and updates the card when it finishes. |
| D746 | Done | TB-1: quick commands without AI: what's next and today (the person's own `week.load` and the Now strip rule, so they match Today), help, open FocusTime. Everything else goes to AS-1's `handle`; AI off for FocusTime or a client returns the assistant's own words, the same as the panel. Until AS-1 merges the bot is wired with no assistant and says what it can do. |
| D747 | Done | TB-1: the 1:1 chat's address (conversation id, service URL, tenant) is kept from the first message or install (`teams_bot_chats`, a user table, removed on uninstall) for later proactive steps (NT-1, a morning brief), which will go through the notification gate. "Let FocusTime message me in Teams" (`[notify] teams_messages`, Settings › Notifications › In Teams) is off by default; nothing reads it to send in TB-1. Both bot tables are left out of the export (Teams' plumbing, rebuilt by the next message), so the export format doesn't change. |
| D748 | Done | TB-1: the Teams app package (`scripts/teams-package.ts`): manifest 1.30, a fixed app id per environment (an upload replaces, never adds), name "FocusTime" (dev "FocusTime (dev)"), the WeekMark icons (192 color, 32 white outline), the bot in the personal scope only, no tabs, files, SSO or Graph permissions. the product owner uploads prod in the Teams admin center; dev goes to testers through a setup policy. The privacy and terms links point at the app's About (an org-only app; no public pages). |
| D749 | Done | TB-1: `Microsoft.BotService` wasn't registered in the subscription and the deploy identity can't register providers, so `infra/scripts/bootstrap.sh` (step 1) now registers it; the product owner runs it (dry run first) once before the first deploy with the bot. |

## AS-1

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D750 | Done | AS-1: one assistant service (`assistant/service.ts`) for two front ends: the in-app panel (the registry's `assistant.*` actions) and TB-1's Teams bot (`server/assistant.ts`'s hub, by the person's Entra object id; the server builds everything else). The interface is locked in `assistant/types.ts` and `docs/assistant-interface.md`; a change is listed there with its date. |
| D751 | Done | AS-1: everything the assistant can do is one list (`assistant/allowlist.ts`): six reads (what's next, a day, an item, gaps, commitments, sends), eleven proposals (move, drop or keep a pick, confirm the day or week, add a must, an Ahead edit, a send, a DevOps comment, a description, a capture) and the close-the-loop stub ("Coming with LP-1"). Its dispatch runs only the actions in that list and what they read (`RUNS`); secrets, settings, admin, sign-in, data, the VM, the timer and the assistant's own actions are never reachable (`NEVER`, checked by a test). |
| D752 | Done | AS-1: injected instructions can't make a write. The first round (intent) sees only my words and the screen's ids, never chat, DevOps or capture text, and names the proposals my words ask for. Reads are always allowed; a proposal only when that round named it. The provider gets `allowed_tools` and `ai/service.ts runTools` refuses any other name without running it. An item id must come from my words, the screen or a read's structured fields; a send's recipient must be named in my words. Read text goes back to the model as quoted data, never as instructions. |
| D753 | Done | AS-1: one client per request, by the guard's own rule (`ai/guard.ts clientsNamed`: names, nicknames, DevOps projects of 3+ letters, known item ids) with GD-1's rule for Internal (clientAccounts()'s why "internal", never a name; keys without case). Two clients named is "Which client?" and nothing is sent. A request naming no client may read across my clients, but then the model hears only "N cards shown", never their facts. |
| D754 | Done | AS-1: every write is a proposal (the same preview the button shows, who sees it, before and after), stored 15 minutes in `assistant_proposals`. Confirm re-checks in order: mine and open, not expired, still on the allowlist, the client still mine and the proposal's, then a fresh preview compared with the one shown (DevOps: revision and before values; FocusTime: the pick or row). Any difference returns `changed` with the fresh proposal. Then it dispatches the same action the button calls, which does its own permission check. Edits only to the fields the proposal lists (a comment's text, a description's after, a pick's day and half). |
| D755 | Done | AS-1: a new named DevOps write, `description_edit`: only on items the shared `canEdit` allows, always the whole text before → after in the preview, `test /rev`, read back, the old text in the audit log, undo puts it back and is refused when the description changed since. CV-1's `description_update` still only appends. The comment write is step 14's `comment`. |
| D756 | Done | AS-1: an Ahead edit from the assistant goes into my change set (`ahead.stage`), never straight to DevOps ("Nothing is written to DevOps until you save your change set in Ahead"). A send goes through SW-1's `send.create` with its rules (Must only for a client's leads; the refusal is passed through). |
| D757 | Done | AS-1: three new reads, each on the allowlist as a read: `assistant.today` (what's next, due today, due tonight), `lookup.day` (my own picks, focus and resume note for a day) and `items.get` (only items on my clients, through Ahead's items). |
| D758 | Done | AS-1: history is two user tables (`assistant_turns`, `assistant_proposals`; a timestamped migration), kept 30 days rolling, cleared from Settings › Data (Assistant history › Clear), in Export my data and never shown to admins. Proposals older than a day after they settle or expire go sooner. The export's schema version stays 28: it follows the frozen numbered migrations (`store/import.test.ts`), the new tables only add, and an older build's import reads only the tables it knows (TB-1's tables aren't exported, so it didn't bump either). |
| D759 | Done | AS-1: voice is push-to-talk only (hold, or tap to start and tap to stop), 60 seconds and 2 MB at most, WebM or MP4 audio, sent to OpenAI's `gpt-transcribe` ($0.0045 a minute; the transcriptions endpoint keeps no data) through `ai/` with the caps and the request log (task `assistant_transcribe`, cost on whole seconds, never text). The audio is held in memory for the one request and never stored or logged; the transcript fills the box to edit and is sent only with Send. The gate: AI for FocusTime on and, when a client is in focus (the screen's client or the selected item's), that client's Allow AI; otherwise the mic is off with "Voice is off while <client> is in focus". `Permissions-Policy` now allows the microphone for FocusTime's own pages only (`microphone=(self)`). |
| D760 | Done | AS-1: the panel opens with Ctrl+J (Cmd+J), / when no text field has focus, or the top bar's Ask (a speech mark on the phone); Esc and × close it. A side panel at the right like the detail panel (440 px, 560 at 3840); on the phone a bottom sheet resting on the bottom bar with a full-width Hold to talk button. Answers are cards (item, block, pick, send, promise, note) with links into the app; proposals have Confirm, Edit and Cancel inline, and a DevOps write's Undo opens the write framework's undo preview. With AI off or not set up the panel says so and opens the command menu. |
| D761 | Done | AS-1: the demo gets a scripted assistant (`sources/demo/assistant.ts`: a few plain requests to the same tool calls, a fixed transcript, no network) used only by the assistant, so the demo shows lookups and proposals through the real service; the demo's other AI features stay off. The demo still can't preview DevOps writes (no writer), so the report's comment-preview screenshot uses the write framework's own words for a demo item. |
| D762 | Done | AS-1 into TB-1: the bot keeps its own small types and gets `teamsAssistant()` (`server/teams/assistant.ts`) over the hub: cards become a title, lines and absolute links, proposals keep their rows, who sees it and expiry, ids are strings in the bot and numbers in the service, and a DevOps write's Undo links to Settings › Data. The bot sends only `{ screen: "teams" }` as context. |
| D763 | Done | TB-1b: the Bot Connector's endorsement rule, which the Teams SDK 2.1.1 skips: a token for an incoming activity must be signed by a Bot Framework key whose `endorsements` include the activity's channel, and FocusTime takes only `msteams`. `server/teams/endorsements.ts` runs before the SDK's check, from the same keys address (kept a day; fetched again early for an unknown key at most every 5 minutes; none kept and none fetchable means refused); 401 and `auth teams_token not_endorsed` otherwise. Only Bot Framework's issuer passes: the SDK would also take Entra-issued tokens (Agent 365 agent identities), which the Teams channel never sends this bot. The tenant pin stays. |
| D764 | Done | TB-1b: handled activities are recorded in `teams_bot_seen` (a user table: a SHA-256 of Teams' activity id and when, never the activity) with a unique insert (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, a `MERGE WITH (HOLDLOCK)` on SQL Server), so a message Teams delivers again is answered once whichever replica gets it; rows older than a day go as new ones come. The in-memory check stays in front (it also covers refusals, which have no person). The store check runs in the background turn, after Teams has its 200, so the answer never waits on the database. If the store can't be reached the message is answered (a rare double answer beats none). Taps were already safe across replicas (the card claim). |
| D765 | Done | TB-1b: one person's turns keep their order in memory, which assumes one replica. Prod's max replicas is 1 (Bicep and live, Nov 5, Single revision mode); during a deploy the old and new revisions overlap for seconds, when two quick messages could be answered by both. Raising max replicas would move the order to the store first. |
| D766 | Done | TB-1b: Delete my data and Clear remove the bot's rows at once (`recordSoftDelete`: `teams_bot_chats`, `teams_bot_cards`, `teams_bot_seen`); the rest of the person's data waits the 30 days as before, and the hard delete and an admin's Remove take every user table, these included. They are left out of the export because they hold no content of the person's: a technical address of their 1:1 chat (conversation id, service URL, tenant, the bot's id), card state (a proposal id, its title, the outcome card) and hashes of handled activity ids, meaningful only to this tenant's bot and rebuilt by their next message. Clear in Settings › Data (Assistant history, AS-1) removes them too, in the same transaction as the assistant's turns and proposals (the cards are the same history from Teams; the count it shows stays the assistant's). |
| D767 | Done | TB-1b: the Teams manifest's privacy and terms links go to the app's own page, `/legal.html#privacy` and `#terms` (`public/legal.html`, a plain document with its own stylesheet, no script, served without signing in like the app's other static files): what FocusTime reads and keeps, the bot, AI, export and delete, and short terms of use for Cooptimize staff. Replaces the About link (D748). |

## CV-5

D776 to D790 (numbered after merge, in TD-1).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|

Otherwise under ⋯. Chatter offers it only under ⋯, which runs This is work first and opens the draft on the conversation that makes (`conversations.this_is_work` now returns `conversationId`). The "In discussion" and write-access gates are gone: the reason the CSC conversation and the backlog promise showed no Create. |

The draft schema adds `acceptance_criteria` (up to 5). |

When the item is Completed or Removed in DevOps the card shows "Item closed" (muted amber) and Mark done (Resolve) is its primary; nothing is resolved or done automatically. Link and create record the item and leave the state `open`; `linked` and `created` remain only on promises from before CV-5 whose item had closed. Escalation (Waiting on <Name>) and `needsItem` skip a commitment with an item (`openItemCommitments` adds `item_id IS NULL`). |
| D787 | Done | CV-5: a one-time move per person (`account_state` key `cv5_kept_reopened`), run the first time they open Conversations or Promises: their promises "Kept by #id" whose item is still open in the store become open with that item linked; those whose item closed, or that the store doesn't have, stay kept. When DevOps can't answer for a client it waits and tries again next time, changing nothing. No SQL migration. |
| D788 | Done | CV-5: AP-1's auto-close (`closePromises` in `actions/request-actions.ts`) is left as it is: it reads only AP-1's `promises` rows with `moved_to IS NULL`, which never show in Promises (the backfill moves a person's rows the first time they look); commitments are never closed by it. |
| D789 | Done | CV-5 (Part 4): the cause of the backlog promise was the gates above, not the classifier: "backlog" already matched `impliesItemByRule`. Two gaps fixed anyway: the backfill set `impliesItem` false for every moved promise, and the AI path tested only the messages, not the commitment's own what. Both now run the rule (tests for the "add X to the backlog and take it on" and "pick it up from the board" shapes). |

Captures, drafts and chatter never count; snoozed and hidden items never count. ## FB-1

D820 to D825 (numbered after merge, in TD-1).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D820 | Done | FB-1 (supersedes D511 and UI-8's identity finding; amends D509, D512 and UI-8's write-log entry): Send feedback no longer uses FocusTime's managed identity. The story is created as the person sending it, with their own DevOps write PAT for the client the feedback project belongs to, through the write framework (preview, confirm, read back, audit log, undo), the same create path as every other. Why: no service account or license, no DevOps admin step, the story shows who sent it, and one write path for everything. The app identities keep their other roles (SQL, Key Vault, ACR); nothing else used them for DevOps. |
| D821 | Done | FB-1: the client is found from the destination, never by name: the target's org and project are matched against the person's clients' DevOps settings (Dataverse, `withPat: false`, clientAccounts()'s clients); with no match it is Cooptimize Internal (clientAccounts()'s why "internal"). The destination stays config: the org.feedback Rule (Admin › Organization), bootstrapped from FOCUSTIME_FEEDBACK_ORG, _PROJECT and _PARENT_ID. |
| D822 | Done | FB-1: the create is a child of the configured parent at its feature tier (open, from the process model): the parent's area and iteration, the process's child type, title, description and tags, no assignee (it's for the FocusTime team). The dialog's preview shows the full text, who it's created as and who can see it; the write framework's held preview id stays on the server and Send confirms it with `devops.confirm`, which checks the PAT again. GD-1's scan applies as for any create (Internal may name clients). The daily limit (D513) stays. |
| D823 | Done | FB-1: the setup check is per person (Settings › Connections › Send feedback and the dialog): "Set up: stories go under Feature #<parent> …" with a working PAT and the parent read back; "Turn on DevOps writes for <client>" with Open Settings › Clients when the PAT is missing or no longer fits; "Your DevOps account can't reach <project>; ask an admin to add you." when DevOps answers 401 or 403 to the read. Without a working PAT nothing is sent to DevOps, the dialog offers Copy text only, and it never claims a send. |
| D824 | Done | FB-1: no preview-only mode anywhere. The feedback_live deploy input, the Bicep param feedbackLive, FOCUSTIME_FEEDBACK_LIVE and the Admin "Sending" switch are gone; a stored org.feedback Rule's `live` is read and ignored, and a stale FOCUSTIME_FEEDBACK_LIVE is ignored. Dev and prod both write for real (dev titles start "[dev]"); the developer loop writes too, with the developer's own PAT. The demo keeps its pretend backlog (`sources/demo/feedback.ts`). |
| D825 | Done | FB-1: the write log entry is a normal `create` under the client (with Undo and Retry, like every create). UI-8's older `feedback` entries stay as they were (no Undo or Retry). "Sent. Thanks. #id" and "Not sent: …" are unchanged. |

The app's real DevOps and Graph writers are handed an in-memory `fetch` (`sources/demo/devops.ts`, `sources/demo/outlook.ts`) that answers only the demo's own fictional orgs and the signed-in person's own calendar and throws for anything else; reads (Week, Plan, Ahead, Break down, Link search, linked items) come from the same memory, so a write reads back and undoes. `write.confirm`, `write.retry`, `devops.confirm` and `devops.retry` leave `DEMO_REFUSED`; secrets, write PATs, connecting, sign-in, sign-out, export, links and players stay in it. Demo write PATs are a `Pat("demo")` that can't be set or cleared. |
| D829 | Done | DM-1: `test/no-outbound.ts` (`noOutbound`, `useNoOutbound`): a test helper that fails any fetch, node http(s) request or socket and records only the method and target (never a query string or body). Every simulated write kind has a test under it (`server/demo-writes.test.ts`); any future write test can use it. |
| D830 | Done | DM-1: AI in the demo. The demo's data is fictional, so real calls are allowed: through `ai/` as always (the guard first, so a refusal is final and never gets a sample), only when the app has a real provider, the team's AI for FocusTime is on and the app's monthly cap isn't reached, and while the session has spent under 0.9 USD (its rules show 1 USD). The spend counts toward the app's cap. With no real call allowed, after 6 seconds, on a provider error or an answer that doesn't fit its schema, a stored sample for the same task answers (`sources/demo/ai.ts`). The demo's own team rules turn AI for FocusTime and every demo client's Allow AI on. Seeds run on samples only. Push-to-talk transcribes for real when allowed, else returns the script's dictated sentence. |
| D831 | Done | DM-1: the scripted assistant (AI off) handles the script's three steps: a lookup by #id, moving a pick named by its words to a weekday and half ("Move Northwind's date filter to Wednesday afternoon"), and a comment on an item ("Comment on #1112 saying …"). |
| D832 | Done | DM-1: Reset demo (`demo.reset`, Settings › Demo, the command menu): only in a demo session; builds a fresh demo context for the same session id, runs the seeds, swaps it in, closes the old store 10 seconds later; the page reloads on Today. ## TB-2

D837 to D849 (numbered after merge, in TD-1).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D837 | Done | TB-2 (amends TB-1's rule, approved Nov 5): the bot sends only in the person's own 1:1 chat: replies, card updates, and, with "Let FocusTime message me in Teams" and that kind's switch on, the brief, check-in, close out and nudges, through the notification gate. Every write it offers is a proposal card that runs only on Confirm, except the person's own plan-store changes from an explicit button tap or "capture:", which run at once with Undo (the same actions as the app's buttons). Never DevOps, Outlook or anything external without Confirm. The glossary's "never messages first" now says this. |
| D838 | Done | TB-2: four kinds, each its own switch under the master switch, all off by default (`[notify] teams_brief`, `teams_checkin`, `teams_closeout`, `teams_nudges`; `teams_friday`, `teams_brief_at` "start" or HH:MM, `teams_checkin_at`, `teams_closeout_minutes` 5 to 120). The brief at the day's first segment (or the set time) until the morning ends or an hour after; the check-in at 12:00 until two hours before the day ends or an hour after; close out 30 minutes before the day ends until it ends; each clamped to the person's hours. Past its window a kind is skipped, never sent late. Monday to Thursday; Friday only with Also on Fridays; never weekends or PTO days (`plan/teams-rhythm.ts`). |
| D839 | Done | TB-2: the notification gate decides (`plan/notify.ts` `gate()`, the same facts the notifier uses): quiet hours wait, focus holds, and a kind that waited goes once that's over if its window is still open. The idle rule's wake includes the next rhythm time (`nextRhythmWake`), every 5 minutes while one waits. |
| D840 | Done | TB-2: at most once. Each occasion (`brief:<date>`, `checkin:<date>`, `closeout:<date>`, `nudges:<hour>`, `nudge:<key>`, `test:<kind>:<ms>`) is claimed by a unique insert in `teams_rhythm` (a user table, `store/migrations/20261111T0900_teams_rhythm.ts`, left out of the export like the other bot tables) before the send, so two replicas (a deploy's overlap) send it once. A send Teams refuses is marked failed; a claim with no message id after 10 minutes is swept to lost and logged; neither is ever sent again. Rows are kept 30 days. |
| D841 | Done | TB-2: dev stays at zero replicas (option A): no cron scale rule. A scheduled kind goes only while dev is awake and inside its window, else it's skipped. Testing on dev is Settings › Notifications › In Teams › Send me a test now, which sends that kind at once (marked "(test)") past the switches and the gate; nudges use a sample when nothing is due. |
| D842 | Done | TB-2: send pacing (`pacedSender`): at most 10 sends a second across the app (Microsoft's limits are 7 a second per bot per chat and 50 a second per app per tenant; one person's sends are rarely more than one), and 429, 412, 502 and 504 are retried 3 times with exponential backoff from 2 s up to 20 s with ±20% jitter, or Retry-After when Teams gives one. Cards are sent alone, never text and a card together (message ids can go missing then). |
| D843 | Done | TB-2: a 403 with MessageWritesBlocked, ConversationBlockedByUser or BotDisabledByAdmin turns "Let FocusTime message me in Teams" off for that person (`settings.save`), marks the chat removed, and Settings › Notifications › In Teams says "FocusTime can't message you in Teams. Add the app again." The next message or install from that person clears it. |
| D844 | Done | TB-2: card taps. Done and Undo on a pick run `pick.mark_done` at once (the app's button) and the card updates in place; Not yet changes only the card. | D845 | Done | TB-2: `propose(tool, args)` on the assistant service (`docs/assistant-interface.md`): a proposal built exactly as one the model proposes and confirmed the same way, for FocusTime's own plan tools only (`PROPOSE_TOOLS`: move_pick, keep_pick, drop_pick, confirm_day, move_picks, close_day). New tools `move_picks` (several picks at once) and `close_day` (marks the chosen picks done, moves each unfinished one to its client's next block by UI-B's `nextBlockFor`, or leaves it on the unfinished list when there's none this week, then `closeout.commit`), each one Confirm with every step shown in the preview. AS-2 builds on `propose()`. |

## TD-1

Numbered D888 to D892 by AI-F1. "Pick" reads awkwardly; the product owner renamed it (Nov 6).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D888 | Done | TD-1: a planned piece of work on a half-day block is a "to-do" (plural "to-dos") in everything people read: the app's labels, counts, empty states, tooltips, aria-labels, errors, Settings, the command menu, the Guide, notifications, the Teams cards and quick replies, the Teams app's command list, the assistant's tool descriptions and previews, the demo's scripted replies and docs, the legal page and the app manifest. Labels only: code names (`pick.*` actions, `move_pick` and the other assistant tool names, types, CSS classes, `data-pick`), tables and columns, stored values (`paid_by = 'picks'`, the close note "Done as a pick", a follow-up's close reason) and URLs stay. "Pick" meaning choose ("Pick a day after today", "the people you pick", "picks it up") stays. |
| D889 | Done | TD-1: the command menu finds every to-do command by "to-do", "todo" and the old "pick" (`filterCommands` treats pick, picks, todo and todos as to-do), so no `aka` per command. |
| D890 | Done | TD-1: the assistant's fixed instructions say "a to-do is a planned piece of work on a half-day block (code name pick)", that people may say pick or to-do and both mean the same, and that replies call it a to-do. The tool names keep pick; their descriptions say to-do. |
| D891 | Done | TD-1: a guard (`scripts/words-guard.ts`, run by `npm run guards` and CI) fails on "pick" in the to-do sense in string literals and JSX text of the app's code (not tests or fakes), the demo docs and the public pages. It skips code tokens (`pick.move`, `kind: "pick"`, class names, `Pick<>`), SQL, comments, and "pick" as choose (followed by a, the, another, from, who, up and similar, or after you, can, may, to), with a short list of one-off phrases. A noun before it ("a pick", "today's picks", "3 picks") makes it the to-do sense whatever follows. |
| D892 | Done | TD-1: the Guide answers "Where did picks go?" beside QF-1's renames. |

## BH-1

Numbered D893 to D900 by AI-F1.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D893 | Done | BH-1: Ahead gets a fourth mode, Backlog (Grid · Gantt · Gaps · Backlog, `#/ahead/backlog`, "backlog" in the command menu): per client, its open items at one tier (Stories by default, Features or Epics) in that project's own backlog order, the order field DV-1 stores per process (StackRank or BacklogPriority, ascending, unset last, ties by target date then id). A project with no order field is listed by target date and says so, as Gaps does. Only Cooptimize's scope (AH-2), whatever "Show the whole project" says in Grid. The rank is the item's place in that scoped backlog, before the owner and Stale filters, so a filtered row keeps its real rank. Read-only order: nothing reorders (AH-4 later). Pure in `plan/backlog.ts` (`backlogGroups`). |
| D894 | Done | BH-1: layout. Clients side by side, as many columns as fit at about 34 rem each (three at 1920, two at 1366), one column on narrow windows and the phone. Each row has two lines: rank, type icon, title and #id, the gap dots (Gaps' gaps, as in Grid's dots), then state, assignee (AH-3 names), target date, size and last change. The client chips, the owner filter (Mine, Contributing, "Everything in scope", the same per-client memory as Grid's Mine, Contributing, All), the tier and Stale are saved with Ahead's filters (`tier`, `stale` in `AheadFilters`). The first 40 rows per client show, then Show all. Read only on a phone, like the rest of Ahead. |
| D895 | Done | BH-1: stale is an open item whose System.ChangedDate (stored with every item in `devops_items`) is at least N weeks old. N is the person's Settings › Ahead value (`[ahead] stale_weeks`, 1 to 26, 6 by default; the Settings section was labelled Roadmap and now reads Ahead, with the Roadmap thresholds beside it). Done (Resolved, Completed, Removed) is never stale; an unknown changed date is never stale. Why the changed date and not the change log: the log keeps 30 days and starts empty at a client's first load, so it can't tell 6 weeks; the changed date is DevOps's own and exact, so nothing is guessed. The log adds what the last change was (state, comment, target date and so on) while it has a row from the last 30 days, through `CentralReads.lastChanges` (the newest row per item, never who), read only when Backlog asks (`ahead.load { lastChanges: true }`). Any DevOps edit counts as a change, including a reorder. Each client shows "N stale"; the Stale chip filters to them. |
| D896 | Done | BH-1: bulk actions on the selected rows go through Ahead's change set (D657): `ahead.stage_bulk { items, op }` with op push 2 or 4 weeks (from the target as Ahead shows it, my pending change first), move to a date, set a date only where there's none, or assign to me (my client login where I have one). Each item gets exactly the checks a single edit has (rights per D656, the type's field, whom I may assign) and one staged change, or is skipped with its reason in words ("Only Northwind Foods's leads and the owner can change this.", "User Story has no target date in Portal's process", "No target date to push. Use Set a target date", "Already has a target date (Nov 20)", "Already yours"). At most 200 items a call. Saving is the usual Review and Save to DevOps in batches of 10 (preview, confirm, read back, audit log, undo; no new write). The `fields` preview now says "Assigned to <name>, not you." when the item's assignee isn't me and the patch doesn't change the assignee, with "They get a quiet note in FocusTime that the dates moved" when dates move (the D664 note). Not offered to the assistant. |
| D897 | Done | BH-1: Plan it adds the selected stories to next week's Plan (the Monday after this week) as nice to haves through `weekplan.add`, a plan-store write. Stories already on that week's Plan are skipped ("Already on next week's Plan"), and so are features and epics ("Plan it adds stories"). Undo removes only the stories this added (`weekplan.remove`). No new action: the UI composes the two. |
| D898 | Done | BH-1: the demo has five open stories untouched since Sep 4 (Points balance page, Power BI refresh schedule, Rate table import, Weekly rate digest, Search analytics), each one whose feature has other recent changes, so Roadmap states are unchanged. |
| D899 | Done | BH-1 (Q-BH1-2): any DevOps edit counts as a change for Stale, a reorder of the backlog (a new StackRank or BacklogPriority) and an edit by a client's automation included, because Stale reads System.ChangedDate. Kept on purpose: an item someone moved in the backlog has had attention. |
| D900 | Done | BH-1 (Q-BH1-1): settings are forward-compatible. `parseConfig` ignores an unknown section or key (a newer build's setting) instead of refusing it, and lists it in the result's `ignored`; nothing else about it changes. The text is never rewritten as a whole: Settings edits change only their own keys and `personalRows` splits the text by section, so unknown sections and keys are saved back untouched, never dropped. This applies to every reader of a person's settings (the plan store's user settings, the developer loop's file, the demo, the overrides edit in `actions/time-actions.ts`). Typos can't come from Settings, whose edits still refuse unknown input (`only` in `config/settings.ts`). Two exceptions: `[secrets]` still refuses any key (no secrets in settings), and `parseConfig(text, { strict: true })` keeps the old strictness for the schema's own tests. Rollbacks to builds from BH-1 on are safe for settings; rolling back to a build before BH-1, after someone saved a setting that build doesn't know, still leaves that person's settings unreadable until the line is removed. |

## HK-4

Housekeeping: the model check, per-span redaction in AI answers, the retention purge, the cleanup list. Numbered D901 to D910 by AI-F1.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D901 | Done | HK-4: the default model stays `gpt-6-luna`. On OpenAI's pricing and models pages (read Nov 6) there is no `gpt-6.1-luna` (its model page is a 404); `gpt-6-luna` is the cheapest current model ($0.10 input, $0.01 cached, $0.50 output per million; "our most efficient model") and lists structured outputs, function calling, image input and the Responses API. The only cheaper text model, `gpt-5-nano` ($0.05 / $0.40), is marked Deprecated (knowledge cutoff May 2024), so it isn't a candidate. The prices table (`DEFAULT_AI.prices`) already matches. The cheaper equivalent is the same model on the Flex tier (`service_tier: "flex"`, Batch prices, half; slower, and a 429 "Resource Unavailable" that isn't billed): not adopted here (the app never sends `service_tier`), proposed for the app's own background tasks once the eval's live numbers are in. No per-task model setting was added, since the default didn't change |
| D902 | Done | HK-4: the model comparison is `scripts/ai-eval.ts`: every AI task's eval cases (synthetic contoso data) through the real service (switches, guards, schema check, redaction) on each model given (`@flex` adds `service_tier` in the eval's own fetch only), with pass rate, invalid answers, tokens, cost and latency per task; results in `docs/azure/ai-eval-results.md` and `.json` (counts only, never prompts or answers). The key comes only from the keyring's `openai-api-key`; the hosted key is never copied down. The committed results are a dry run on the stub (it checks the harness, not a model); live numbers need the key in the keyring (open question). Not covered: `stories.draft` (needs a feature's full DevOps signals), images and transcription |
| D903 | Done | HK-4 (amends GD-1's D734): AI answers are redacted per span. A secret shape (the guard's `SECRET_SHAPES`), the VM's name or resource id, or an email address outside Cooptimize's domains in any string of a parsed answer is replaced by "[removed]" where it stands, and the rest of the answer, every other item of a batch included, is kept (`ai/guard.ts` `redact`, `redactValue`; `ai/service.ts` for `run` and `runTools`). Logged as `ai redact <task>:<secret or email>` with a count, never the text. Object keys aren't touched. A replacement that no longer fits the schema (an enum or a pattern) makes the answer `invalid_output` rather than a wrong value. Another client named in an answer still refuses it whole, as before |
| D904 | Done | HK-4: the retention purge (`store/retention.ts`, `server/retention-job.ts`): one app job on the scheduler, once a UTC day for everyone, claimed with a unique insert in `job_claims` (a new app table: job, period, claimed and done times, counts as numbers), so two replicas or a restart run it once; a run that fails gives back its claim and tries again in an hour. Rules, each by age only and never by person: assistant turns after 30 days and proposals after 30 days or a day after they settle or expire (D758), Teams activity ids after a day (D764), Teams cards a week after they settle or expire (their sweep was never called before), the Teams rhythm after 30 days, Take one and Ahead notes after 14 days, the DevOps change log after 30 days, delivery snapshots after 120 days, its own claims after 30 days, and stored chat messages past the Rule `conv.retention_days` (the shared poll's expire, which otherwise ran only after a poll, so never where polling is on demand). Logged as counts per rule. Delete my data, Clear and the hard delete are unchanged |
| D905 | Done | HK-4: the purge's wake is the next UTC midnight once the day is done, held in memory, so a sleeping server reads nothing for it before then (D431); like the daily people refresh, it wakes the database at most once a day |
| D906 | Done | HK-4: the cleanup list (`docs/azure/cleanup-list.md`) is read only: nothing is dropped (D468). Each item has evidence (no live reads or writes, a fresh database's counts) and a two-step plan: stop using it in a release, drop it in a later one with a `safe-drop` comment and the product owner's OK |
| D907 | Done | HK-4: numbering. HK-4 had numbered the merged steps' decisions still marked "new" (CV-5, UI-8, IB-1, FB-1, DM-1, TB-2, WK-1, SR-1, DX-1, PK-1) as D776 to D887; TD-1 numbered the same rows on main first, with the same numbers. On the merge main's numbers and text were kept for every numbered decision (TD-1's to-do wording included); nothing was renumbered, no number appears twice and none was lost. HK-4's own decisions stay unnumbered |
| D908 | Done | HK-4 (the product owner, Nov 6): `gpt-6-luna` stays the default (`gpt-6.1-luna` doesn't exist). The live eval is skipped: the OpenAI key isn't copied down from Key Vault to a developer machine for it. `scripts/ai-eval.ts` and its dry-run results stay, so a later check can run it with a keyring key (Q-HK4-1) |
| D909 | Done | HK-4 (the product owner, Nov 6; Q-HK4-2): Flex (`service_tier: "flex"`, half price, slower, an unbilled 429 when capacity is short) is approved in principle for background AI work only: conversation sorting (`conversations.update`), promise extraction (`promises.extract`) and image descriptions. Never for interactive work: the assistant, drafts and Break down. Not built in HK-4; a later step adds it with a fallback to the standard tier on the 429. Built in AI-F1 (below). |

## AI-F1

Flex processing for background AI work. D911 to D918 (numbered in HK-5).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D911 | Done | AI-F1 (builds D909, Q-HK4-2): every AI task's processing tier is declared in one place, `ai/tiers.ts`. Flex (`service_tier: "flex"`, OpenAI's Flex processing: Batch prices, half of standard for `gpt-6-luna`, slower, beta, an unbilled 429 "Resource Unavailable" when capacity is short; developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/flex-processing, /pricing and /models/gpt-6-luna, read Nov 6) only for the background tasks nobody waits on: `conversations.update` (the shared poll's sorting, with the chat images' descriptions in the same request), `promises.extract` and `asks.classify` (the request poll). Everything else is standard, and so is any task the table doesn't name (a new one): the assistant (`assistant.intent`, `assistant.answer`, push-to-talk, which Flex doesn't take), Break down (`stories.draft`), Status drafts, conversation drafts and merges, capture drafts (`capture.prepare`), Test AI. Brain dump has no AI task yet; MC-1 hadn't merged, so its transcript pass gets its tier when it does |
| D912 | Done | AI-F1: `conversations.comment` (the follow-through comment, written on the upkeep tick) stays standard: nobody waits on it, but it is a draft a person reads, and the product owner's list didn't name it. One line in `ai/tiers.ts` moves it (open question Q-AIF1-1) |
| D913 | Done | AI-F1: a person waiting runs a background task on standard. Sort again (`requests.sort_again`, `asks.classify`) and Sort unsorted (`conversations.sort_unsorted`, the pipeline) are buttons whose answer the person waits for, so `aiFor(ctx, { waiting: true })` holds them at standard; the polls' own runs use Flex |
| D914 | Done | AI-F1: Flex retries and the fallback. A Flex call has its own timeout (`flex_timeout_ms`, 120 s: Flex is slower; the docs' SDK example allows 15 minutes, but a poll cycle can't wait that long). A 429 that isn't billing (or a 5xx) retries on Flex with exponential backoff from `flex` base (2 s, doubling) and up to 50% jitter, or the Retry-After when longer, until `flex_max_tries` (3, the first included) or `flex_max_wait_ms` (90 s from the first try, waits counted) would pass; then the call runs once on standard with the standard path's one retry, as the docs advise ("set service_tier to auto in the retried request, or remove the service_tier parameter"). A timeout or a 400 (a model without Flex, the beta's limited availability) falls back at once. Quota, any other error, a refusal or a cut-short answer aren't capacity: they stand, one attempt as before. Configurable in `[ai]` (`flex_max_tries`, `flex_max_wait_ms`, `flex_timeout_ms`) and `FOCUSTIME_AI_FLEX_*` |
| D915 | Done | AI-F1: counts only. Application Insights gets `ai/tier` (`<task>:<tier>`, count the Flex retries) and `ai/flex_fallback` (`<task>:limit`, `:timeout` or `:provider`, count the Flex tries); never text |
| D916 | Done | AI-F1: cost at the tier actually used. The prices table gets Flex prices (`DEFAULT_AI.flexPrices`, 0.05 / 0.005 cached / 0.25 USD per million for `gpt-6-luna`; `[ai] flex_*_usd_per_million`, `FOCUSTIME_AI_FLEX_*_USD_PER_MILLION`; half of the standard prices when unset). The tier used is the response's `service_tier` ("flex", or anything else as standard; the asked-for tier when absent), so a Flex request OpenAI served on another tier, or a fallback, is priced and logged as standard. Flex 429s aren't billed and add no cost. The person's and the app's caps sum the logged cost, so they count the half price. The eval (`scripts/ai-eval.ts`) still picks its tier itself with `@flex` and holds the app's table at standard |
| D917 | Done | AI-F1: the request logs keep the tier: a `tier` column ("standard" or "flex", standard for older rows) on `ai_requests` and `app_ai_requests` (migration `20261115T0900_ai_tier`, adds only). Settings › AI's last 20 requests show "· Flex" after the cost. Not in Export (the AI log never was) |
| D918 | Done | AI-F1: Admin › AI › Processing tier lists every task with its tier. A background task has a switch (Flex, or Standard); every other task reads "Standard" with no switch. The switches are one Rule, `ai.standard_tasks` (the background tasks an admin put back on standard, audited like every Admin change); a stored name no build declares is dropped when read, so an older or newer build can read it. An admin can only move a background task back to standard, never an interactive one to Flex |

## MC-1

Meeting capture, slice 1: a client's registered weekly call becomes proposals that its reviewers confirm. D919 to D931 (numbered in HK-5).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D919 | Done | MC-1: FocusTime reads only meetings registered in Admin › Clients › <client> › Meetings (and meetings FocusTime scheduled itself). The Graph client (`sources/graph/meetings.ts`) checks the meeting store's registry (`registered`) and `graphReadable` before every request and refuses anything else before it is sent. A registration is a recurring series picked from the admin's own calendar or a pasted Teams join link, with a name, a role (Weekly status call or Planning), reviewers (by default the registrant and the client's leads) and a transcript source |
| D920 | Done | MC-1: the source is detected from the organizer's tenant: Automatic when Cooptimize organizes the meeting (its transcript is read after each call), Upload when the client does or it can't be told. An admin can switch to Upload at any time; Automatic is offered only for a Cooptimize organizer. For a client-hosted meeting FocusTime keeps only the calendar fields (name, weekday, time, length, zone, the join link and the registrant's event id): no online meeting id, no transcript request |
| D921 | Done | MC-1 (the product owner, Nov 6): delegated only. Transcripts are read with a person's own OnlineMeetings.Read and OnlineMeetingTranscript.Read.All (admin consented, a new token kind `meetings`), the registrant's first, then the other reviewers' in order; one refused (signed out, consent missing, 401, 403, 404) moves to the next. No app-only permission, no application access policy, no change notifications: the job polls each ended occurrence every 15 minutes for 3 hours, then hourly to 12 hours, then asks its reviewers for an upload |
| D922 | Done | MC-1: Upload a transcript (.vtt or .docx, 5 MB at most) for a dated occurrence is always available to reviewers, Automatic or not; both formats give the same lines (speaker, time, words). The bytes are parsed in memory and zeroed; only the lines are stored. A non-reviewer is refused (403) |
| D923 | Done | MC-1: attendees are the Cooptimize users on the registrant's invite, refreshed for each occurrence and stored as FocusTime user ids only. Reviewers and attendees can see a call's occurrences and proposals; only reviewers can edit, skip and confirm. Nobody else sees them, admins included. Enforced by the meeting store's viewer, and commitments from a call are visible through the conversation store's `meetingMember` |
| D924 | Done | MC-1: one AI task, `meeting.capture`, for one client only (GD-1: Cooptimize Internal may name clients), only with AI for FocusTime and the client's Allow AI on, under the person's and the app's caps. Before the guard the transcript is masked (HK-4's `maskTranscript`): other clients' names, projects and item ids become "[another client]" and spoken email addresses "[email]"; the masks are counted, the text never logged. It answers gaps (a draft story or feature under a named parent), commitments (who, what, to whom, due) and comments (a decision or a status on a named item), each with the transcript lines that support it. The poll job runs on Flex (`ai/tiers.ts`); Upload and Propose again run on standard, since a person is waiting |
| D925 | Done | MC-1: the review screen, "Update <client>'s plan · <meeting> <date>": proposals grouped New in DevOps, Promises and Waiting on, Comments on DevOps items, each with its quotes; tick, edit or skip each one, then one Confirm. Confirm shows every write's preview first (the write framework's own previews), then runs the existing writes: a gap is the step 14 create (`proposal_create`), a comment is the step 14 comment, each with `test /rev`, read back, audit log and undo; a comment on a feature-tier item says "Will appear in Kat's Monday report" (her report lists feature comments). A skipped proposal writes nothing |
| D926 | Done | MC-1 (the product owner, Nov 6): a commitment by a Cooptimizer becomes a shared commitment (CV-1's commitments, under the pseudo chat `meeting:<series>`) that shows in that person's own Promises, for them to keep or dismiss; a client person's is my Waiting on. FocusTime never writes into someone else's plan |
| D927 | Done | MC-1: after each occurrence, its reviewers get one in-app nudge (`meeting_review`, Settings › Notifications › Client calls to review): "Update <client>'s plan" when proposals are ready, "Upload <client>'s transcript" when none came through. "No transcript this week" dismisses it for everyone. A new TB-2 Teams kind, Client calls to review (`teams_meetings`), off by default, sends the same once per occurrence through the notification gate (not during focus, quiet hours, weekends or PTO days) |

Proposals, their states and what the confirm made stay. Transcripts are not in Export and never in logs, Application Insights or error messages (counts and outcomes only) |

## HK-5

Flaky tests and slow full runs. D932 to D939, numbered at AS-2's merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D932 | Done | HK-5: the test run's worker count comes from the machine (`test/workers.ts`, used by `vite.config.ts`): 60% of the CPUs not already busy (the 1-minute load average; ignored on CI, where the runner is the job's alone and npm and apt leave the load high), at most one per GB of free memory beyond 2 GB, between 1 and CPUs − 1; `FOCUSTIME_TEST_WORKERS` sets it. Vitest's default (CPUs − 1, 19 here) ran 19 files against one SQL Server container and the CPUs at once: the store isolation test's SQL Server case took 73 to 107 s of its 120 s alone and timed out whenever a second session ran its suite, and two suites at once failed 4 to 9 different tests per run, a different set each time. Measured on 20 cores: 19 workers 117 to 140 s (isolation 83 to 119 s), 12 workers 132 s (isolation 63 s), 10 workers 146 s, 8 workers 164 s. CI's 4-core runners go from 3 workers to 2. |
| D933 | Done | HK-5: CI's browser tests run in six shards (four before). On the 4-core runners the worker rule gives 2 files at once instead of vitest's 3, which took the slowest of four shards from 180 to 231 s (the unit job went from 183 to 142 s with less contention on its SQL Server); six shards bring the slowest back down. No check is required by name on main, so the renamed jobs block nothing; deploy-dev reuses the PR's run by the tested tree, not job names. |
| D934 | Done | HK-5: no retries and no blanket timeout increases. `expect.poll`'s 1 s default stays: once the machine isn't oversubscribed it holds (three runs on 6 cores and the five acceptance runs passed with it); the failures at 1 s under two suites were the overload, not the wait. A targeted timeout stays only where a comment says why. |
| D935 | Done | HK-5: Ahead's QF-1 test picked Priya Shah, who is #1113's assignee in DevOps, while the demo already stages a change on #1113 (to me): the pick put DevOps' value back, so the server rightly dropped the change, and the test passed only when it looked at the seeded mark before the answer came (it failed whenever the answer was quicker, on CI three times). It now picks Sam Ortiz and waits for the name before checking the cell is pending. Not an app bug. |
| D936 | Done | HK-5 (app fix, FB-1's dialog): Send feedback moves focus to the step's button in a layout effect, before paint. With a passive effect, the moment the preview replaced the text box focus sat on the page, so a quick Ctrl+Enter (a person's or a test's) never reached the dialog and nothing was sent. |
| D937 | Done | HK-5: the admin test's DevOps poller runs on the live harness's Monday 9:30 AM, moving with real time from there, so its cadence reads "every minute" again; it used the real clock, read "every 10 minutes" at night and on weekends, and f8bd1fd had loosened the match to accept both instead. |
| D938 | Done | HK-5: Inbox's one-count test waits until the top bar's badge, the rail, the header and the rows agree, before and after Hide; the badge reads its count on its own and could land a moment after the list. |
| D939 | Done | HK-5: the CI flakes from before the fixes they are already in (TB-1's reply order, fbcae17; QF-1's day checks, f8bd1fd) and the older ones from before HK-3 (setup, brand, estimating) were checked against the runs here and not seen again. |

## AS-2

The assistant, smarter: asks instead of giving up, cross-client moves, compound updates. D946 to D955, numbered at merge after HK-5 and WK-2.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D946 | Done | AS-2: one client per AI call stays. A proposal the server builds may span clients when no client's content passes through the AI for it (`move_plan`: the model reads only my words, days and halves; the server finds the to-dos and builds one proposal grouped by client, as D845's send does). Added to CLAUDE.md's assistant rule. |
| D947 | Done | AS-2 (supersedes PV-1's PTO refusal in D571, and D858's note): a day never blocks a move or an add. Weekends, Friday's catch-up, evenings, a day with no working hours, an over-full block and a PTO day are all allowed with a short note, the same words on the Board's drop tip, its toast, `pick.move`, `pick.add` and the assistant ("Saturday isn't a working day: this is flex time", "Friday is your catch-up day", "You're on PTO that day", "Over your Wednesday PM hours by 1h"; `placeNote` and `overNote` in `plan/days.ts`). The only hard block is a time that has already ended (a past day, or today's half after its end). Another client's block and a to-do already there still refuse. |
| D948 | Done | AS-2: a weekend to-do never disappears: the Board already shows a Saturday or Sunday once it has a planned or done to-do (`weekendPlanned` in `actions/dispatch.ts`, `weekendHasPlans` in `week/build.ts`), so no new flex spot was added. |
| D949 | Done | AS-2: when a choice is missing the assistant asks one short question with choices the server builds from my own data (names, dates, blocks, counts, my items' ids and titles), plus "or just type it". The question is kept on its assistant turn (`pending_json`, a new nullable column in `20261117T0900_assistant_pending`; in Export, schema version 30, and removed by Clear) and answered by a tap or words for 10 minutes; after that, or a clearly new request, it starts fresh. Nothing from any client goes to the AI before a client is chosen. Never the capability list after a question. |
| D950 | Done | AS-2: the resolver (`assistant/resolve.ts`) matches my words on the server against my own to-dos, items and features across my clients: `#ids`, whole titles of two words or more (a longer title wins), "the story for …" phrases (one fit only) and parent features or epics of a to-do's item. Two that fit equally are a question, never a guess. One client matched makes it the request's client; several ask. Only with one client do the matches go to the model, in the answer round (quoted, as data); the intent round sees only my words and the screen. |
| D951 | Done | AS-2: Undo for a move the assistant made: `pick.uncarry` on each copy (a move to another day), `pick.move` back within the same day; any that can't go back (worked on since) are listed with the reason. One Undo per proposal, kept on the proposal (`done`, `undone`). In Teams the done card's Undo is claimed once in its own row (`u<proposal id>`). |
| D952 | Done | AS-2: compound updates (`item_update`): up to three parts on one item, its done state from the process (`devops.done_offer` gained an item mode, `{ clientKey, itemIds }` → `itemOffers`, with PK-1's rights and done-state rules), a comment and a target date (Ahead's change set). One Confirm runs each part through the write framework; a failed part says which and leaves the others done with their own undo. More than three: ask to split. |
| D953 | Done | AS-2: `conversations.search_items`, `conversations.unlink` and `my_promises.unlink` were in `actions/` but not in the registry (dispatch answered "unknown action"); registered, with the menu's cases, and reachable from the assistant (`link_search`, `unlink`, `link_comment`). IB-1's `inbox.list` and `inbox.count` are reads; `inbox.seen` and `inbox.snooze` proposals. `my_promises.followup` was not on the allowlist, so nothing was removed. "Write my status updates" opens Week › Status. |
| D954 | Done | AS-2: the fixed instructions carry the glossary and 3 to 5 example phrasings per tool. The examples have no item ids, numbers of two digits or more, or client names: the AI guard scans the instructions with the input, and those would read as another client's work. |
| D955 | Done | AS-2: interactive assistant tasks stay on the standard AI tier (AI-F1). |

Cooptimizers only, by the existing rule (`levels().people.cooptimizers`: guests, other domains and automation accounts are out); a Dataverse user with no email is outside the domains, so everyone listed has an address. |

Last active: the latest request in a live session (`sessions.last_seen_at`), else the last sign-in, as a day in the admin's time zone. DevOps connected: a write PAT for any client (`devops-write-pat:<client>` for a client with a stored DevOps identity, the count Admin › People already had); never which client, the value, scope or expiry. Calendar connected: the Outlook refresh token is kept (the work calendar FocusTime reads in the background); Google is personal and optional, so it doesn't count. Teams app added: the person's `teams_bot_chats` row exists and isn't removed, only where the bot runs (`FOCUSTIME_BOT_APP_ID` in the hosted app); elsewhere the column is left out. |

Outlook: `https://outlook.office.com/mail/deeplink/compose?to=<a;b>&subject=&body=` (one email to all), with "Use my mail app instead" (`mailto:`) as the fallback a person picks, since a page can't tell whether Outlook on the web opened. Copy message. Links carry the recipients' addresses, the subject and the message the admin sees; nothing else. The Teams app link in the message holds the environment's public Teams app id (the same one in the app package), only where the bot is set up. |

Removing a person deletes their row. Only someone in Who's set up who hasn't signed in can be invited. The record follows the open or copy, so an invite the admin then doesn't send still shows; inviting again is fine. |

The rendered HTML is checked against an allowlist of tags and attributes before it is bundled. |
