## HK-6: the flaky late chip test (Nov 7)

Numbered D1048 to D1050 in HK-7.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D1048 | Done | HK-6 (Q-DOC1-8): the flake was in the test, not the app. With network and chip logging on the failing runs, Monday AM's chip went within about half a second of Done (`inbox.today` came back without the promise, as it should). The test's `chipText` read it in two steps, `count()` then `textContent()`: when the chip went between them, `textContent()` waited Playwright's 30 s for an element that wasn't coming back, so the 20 s poll for "no chip" timed out. Under load that window is hit often (20 of 84 runs of that test failed this way, 10 at a time on this machine). `chipText` now reads the page once (`evaluateAll`), so a poll sees each state as it is. Every assertion is unchanged. |
| D1049 | Done | HK-6: no app change. `inbox.today` is already read again after every Inbox change (the Inbox's reload calls the week's reload, and the count reads after it), so the re-read Q-DOC1-8 suggested isn't needed. |
| D1050 | Done | HK-6: MB-1b's test (`e2e/mb1b.test.ts`) didn't read in two steps, but it waited a set 300 ms for the status sheet to slide in before measuring it. It now waits for the sheet's animations to finish. No other e2e reads a chip in two steps. |

## LP-1

Close the loop by Teams, Email, DevOps comment or Call (Nov 7). Numbered D1051 to D1069 in HK-7. Tanner's four decisions (email as an Outlook draft by default or sent now, Teams into a chat with outside people only after a preview that lists them, the three after-send options, posts as the person) are taken as given.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D1051 | Done | LP-1: Close the loop on a promise (either kind: `c<id>` from a chat, `f<id>` one I added or a rule opened) opens a draft, then a preview, then the sent state (`ui/SendLoop.tsx`), everywhere IB-1's form was: the Inbox item, the promise rows in Captures, the block panel and Close out. IB-1's form is kept as "Close without sending", unchanged (`ui/CloseLoop.tsx`, `followup.close`, `my_promises.close`). |
| D1052 | Done | LP-1: the recipient is the promise's Who; it is never me (`recipientOk` in `plan/loop.ts`: by id, any of my names, or my address). With no Who, or Who is me, the draft says so and only Call and Close without sending are offered. |
| D1053 | Done | LP-1: the text is drafted by AI (task `loop_draft`, standard tier, my cap, the client's Allow AI) from the promise, the conversation's title and summary, my own words and the linked item's state; or, with AI off or failing, from a plain template. Always editable; the line under it says which and what from ("Drafted by AI from your promise, the conversation “X” and #N's state (Active)."). The answer passes the GD-1 guard like any AI answer. |
| D1054 | Done | LP-1: the preview runs the GD-1 guard on the final text (`otherClientMatch`): another client's name blocks Send with the reason; Cooptimize Internal may name clients. A Comment's text also goes through the DevOps write preview's own guard. |
| D1055 | Done | LP-1: Teams goes to the chat the promise came from (a registered client chat) or a 1:1 chat with someone at Cooptimize (`POST /chats` returns the existing one). FocusTime never starts a chat with someone outside Cooptimize: for them the Teams option is the prefilled deep link and Mark sent. |
| D1056 | Done | LP-1: "Who sees it" counts everyone in the chat from its cached member list, me included, inside and outside Cooptimize (`isExternal`: another tenant, no tenant or a guest), and lists the outside people by display name (Tanner's decision 2: "Contoso project chat, 7 people, 4 outside Cooptimize"). D485's "never by name" stays for everything else; this preview is the one place outside names show, and they are never stored: `loop_sends` keeps only the two counts. An email shows "Email to <name>" with the same counts. |
| D1057 | Done | LP-1: Teams sends post as me (`ChatMessage.Send`, delegated), then read the message back with `Chat.Read`. Without `Chat.Read` the 201 answer with the message id counts as confirmation (`Sent`, not read back is shown only when the read fails). The link kept is the message link built from the chat and message ids (`messageLink`), since Graph's `webUrl` is often null for chat messages. |
| D1058 | Done | LP-1: Email is an Outlook draft by default (`Mail.ReadWrite`): FocusTime creates it, the send waits as "Draft in Outlook" with Open draft and "I sent it", and the promise stays open until I say I sent it. Send now (`Mail.Send`) creates the draft and sends it, then reads it back by its immutable id (it must be out of Drafts with a sent time). `Prefer: IdType="ImmutableId"` on every mail call. |
| D1059 | Done | LP-1: a draft defers the DevOps parts (comment, state change): they need the message to have gone, so the preview says to add them after sending and "I sent it" only marks the promise kept. |
| D1060 | Done | LP-1: after a confirmed send: Mark this promise kept (on by default; with Undo, which reopens the promise and clears the send's kept flag), Also add it as a comment on #id (off by default), and an optional state change from the item's process states (`devops.states`, Removed left out), each through the DevOps write framework with its own preview shown in the Send preview, and confirmed only after the send succeeded. Kept off means sent and the promise stays open. |
| D1061 | Done | LP-1: the Comment channel is the DevOps `comment` write (preview, `test /rev`, read back, audit log, undo). It @mentions the recipient when they are on the item (assignee, creator or last changer: the only identities whose DevOps id FocusTime has without a new call); otherwise it posts without one and the preview says so. A lookup in the identity API (vssps) would send the PAT to a host other than `dev.azure.com/<org>/`, which the PAT rule forbids. |
| D1062 | Done | LP-1: Call sends nothing: a Teams call link to the person (when their address is known) and a note of what was agreed; Save call note closes the loop with how Call and the note. |
| D1063 | Done | LP-1: failure keeps the promise open and offers Retry. Retry never sends again: it reads back what may have landed (the post, the draft) and only then runs the remaining parts. Partial success names each part ("Posted in X. The comment on #N wasn't added: …"). The preview says sent messages and emails can't be undone. |
| D1064 | Done | LP-1: every send is a row of the new user table `loop_sends` (migration `20261119T0900_loop_sends`, `user_id` first, in `USER_TABLES` and Export, schema version 31): channel, mode, state, where, recipient's name, the two counts, my text, the link, a DevOps write id, the error, kept and the times. It is the audit entry; Settings › Data › Close the loop sends lists them. Graph's own ids stay on the server (`graphRef`). |
| D1065 | Done | LP-1: the four new Graph permissions are delegated and asked for incrementally, per person, from the preview's Allow sending from FocusTime (`teams_send`: `ChatMessage.Send` and `Chat.Create`; `mail_draft`: `Mail.ReadWrite`; `mail_send`: `Mail.Send`), the way AP-1 asks for `Chat.Read` (D490). They are not added to `GRAPH_SCOPES` in `infra/scripts/app-registration.sh`: sign-in asks for `.default`, so that would grant them to everyone, and rerunning the script drops later consents. An admin who wants them for everyone grants them in Entra; `docs/setup/close-the-loop.md`. |
| D1066 | Done | LP-1: a missing permission falls back to IN-1's pattern: Open in Teams (a 1:1 prefilled through `/l/chat/0/0?users=&message=`; a client chat opens as itself, since Teams can't prefill a group chat, with Copy text) or Open in Outlook (`/mail/deeplink/compose`) prefilled, IN-1's builders in `plan/invite.ts`, Mark sent, and the admin note. Mark sent records the send as `link` and runs the kept option only (the DevOps parts need a confirmed send). |

## AH-7: Readiness you can finish (Nov 7)

| D1117 | Done | AH-7: Start here is the 5 most urgent entries across the clients the toolbar shows. The urgency rule: dated soonest first (an item's target, else its start; an overdue date is the soonest of all; undated items come last), then nearest the top of its client's backlog (its place in that client's Readiness list, which is backlog order, or target order where a project has none), then mine, then the item id (`startHere` in `plan/readiness.ts`; the same sentence is the "Why these?" tooltip, `START_RULE`). Places in different clients' backlogs are compared as places (first, second…), since one client's StackRank says nothing about another's. |

Clicking a count sets the toolbar's Readiness kinds to that kind alone; clicking the active one clears the kinds. A kind with 0 is shown dimmed and does nothing. An entry with two kinds counts under both, so the counts can add up to more than the header. |

| D1143 | Done | WK-3: no demo seed for Deployments: a seeded Saturday would put a weekend column in every demo week and every Week test. `e2e/wk3.test.ts` plans the two fictional ones the screenshots show (Northwind's Release 4.2 on Saturday 9:00 to 11:00, Harbor's Portal hotfix on Friday 19:00 to 21:00) through `deploy.add` first. |

## UI-10: Text boxes that grow instead of scrolling (Nov 7)

Numbered D1144 to D1152 in HK-7.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D1144 | Done | UI-10: UI-7 ran (PR #52, merged Nov 5, D725) and nothing later undid it. The arrows were a bug it left in place from CP-1: the box set its height to `scrollHeight`, which leaves out the 2px border under `box-sizing: border-box`, so an empty one-line box was 2px short of its own text, always overflowed, and showed its scrollbar. UI-7's `scrollbar-width: thin` made that bar thin, but in Chromium setting `scrollbar-width` turns off `::-webkit-scrollbar` styling and the native bar keeps its arrows (on Windows). The Assistant's input had `resize: vertical`, so it showed a resize handle. |
| D1145 | Done | UI-10: one shared text box, `ui/TextBox.tsx` (sizing and keys pure in `ui/textbox.ts`): `minRows` (1 by default), `maxRows` (6 by default), `onEnter`. It measures at its smallest height with no scrollbar, adds the borders back, and sets its height between the two; past `maxRows` it scrolls (`overflow-y: auto`), otherwise `hidden`, so the bar is there only while there is something to scroll. It fits again on input, on a change of its width (ResizeObserver) and on a change of text size or theme. Its width is always the layout's. |
| D1146 | Done | UI-10: no textarea anywhere has a resize handle (`textarea { resize: none }`, and every `resize: vertical` is gone). Every textarea's scrollbar is the styled one: `::-webkit-scrollbar` 10px, a rounded thumb, no buttons (Chromium and Safari); Firefox gets `scrollbar-width: thin` and `scrollbar-color` only inside `@supports not selector(::-webkit-scrollbar)`, because setting either anywhere Chromium sees them brings the native arrows back. `ui/textbox.test.ts` fails on a `resize:` other than none or a `scrollbar-width`/`-color` outside that block. |
| D1147 | Done | UI-10: `onEnter` submits on Enter, never on Shift+Enter and never while an IME composes (`isComposing`, or keyCode 229). Each field keeps its keys: Quick capture (Enter saves, Shift+Enter a line, Ctrl or Cmd+Enter saves and opens the preview, through its own `keyAction`, which already skipped composing), the Assistant (Enter sends), Close the loop's note in CloseLoop (Enter closes it), Feedback (Enter previews; it now skips composing too), Break down's note (Enter runs). Everywhere else Enter stays a new line, as before, including LP-1's message. |
| D1148 | Done | UI-10: the fields and their lines (start → most before scrolling): Quick capture 1 → 6 (UI-7's "about 4" becomes 6, as asked); the Assistant's input 1 → 6 (was 3 rows, 2 on the phone); the Assistant proposal's Edit fields 3 → 10, the description's after 8 → 16; Close the loop's note (CloseLoop) 2 → 6; LP-1's message 5 → 12 (4 → 12 compact, as in the phone's sheet), its Call note 3 → 8; SR-1's status line 2 → 10; DevOps write's comment 3 → 10; Send to…'s note 2 → 6; Ask to collaborate's note 2 → 6; Meetings' comment 2 → 8; the draft editor's description 4 → 14 and acceptance criteria 3 → 12; Create in DevOps' description 4 → 14 and acceptance criteria 4 → 12; a conversation's Create DevOps item description 8 → 16 and acceptance criteria 4 → 12; Break down's note 2 → 6, its Regenerate all note 2 → 6, a card's description 2 → 10; Feedback 5 → 12; Admin › Clients' house style 3 → 10; the invite message (Who's set up) 14 → 20; the heads-up draft (read-only, FollowUps) 2 → 8. |
| D1149 | Done | UI-10: three read-only textareas stay plain (no handle, the styled bar, a fixed size): the hidden clipboard helper in the detail panel, the Coop brief and Diagnostics' copy text. They are copy boxes, not inputs. |
| D1150 | Done | UI-10: the ↵ button and the client chip stay grid items beside the box (UI-7), top-aligned at one line of the box tall; the Assistant's mic and Send sit at the foot of its row; none of them changes size as the box grows, and no text runs under them. |
| D1151 | Done | UI-10: the phone. The box behaves the same. `ui/keyboard.ts` sets `--kb-inset` on the root to what the on-screen keyboard covers (`innerHeight − visualViewport.height − offsetTop`, under 80px counts as 0), and the capture sheet, the Inbox's item sheet and the Assistant's sheet rest on `max(--bb-h, --kb-inset)`, so the box and its ↵ or Send stay above the keyboard and the home indicator. iOS Safari needs this (it keeps the layout viewport when the keyboard opens); `interactive-widget=resizes-content` in the viewport meta makes Chrome on Android resize instead, where the inset stays 0. Not tried on a real phone in this step (Q-UI10-1). |
| D1152 | Done | UI-10: the e2e harness can draw scrollbars (`startDemo(…, { showScrollbars: true })`, dropping Playwright's `--hide-scrollbars`) so a test can measure them: the styled bar is 10px, Chromium's native one 15px. |

## GD-2: the client boundary rule, revised (Nov 7)

Numbered D1153 to D1171 in HK-7. Revises GD-1 (D733 to D737) and CLAUDE.md's last hard rule (sort internal chats, guard the exits).

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D1153 | Done | GD-2: why. On dev, in 30 days, 11 `conversations.update` requests were refused by the guard (`refused:app`), none on prod. The 4 since GD-1's per-rule log were all `input:work_item`: a number in a message that happened to equal another client's item id. The 7 before carry no rule (most likely Internal chats, which GD-1 then exempted). Each refusal held its whole batch, so one false match left every message in it unsorted. The logs can't tell an internal chat from a client-facing one, which is why the audience is now computed, not logged. |
| D1154 | Done | GD-2: a chat's audience comes from its members, not its label (`chatAudience` in `plan/boundary.ts`): internal when every member is in the home tenant and not a guest, client-facing otherwise. Computed at each poll from the cached member list (T-2's), so a guest joining changes it at the next poll, and an admin's Delivery or Customer label never decides it. |
| D1155 | Done | GD-2: an internal chat sorts normally, with every message's words, even when they name another client. Old holds in such a chat (GD-1's whole-batch refusals, `LEGACY_CLIENT_REFUSED`, and GD-2's own `HELD_MENTION`) are retried once by the next poll through the normal pipeline. |
| D1156 | Done | GD-2: in a client-facing chat only the message that names another client is held (`HELD_MENTION`), never its batch. The match is on another client's names and aliases only (`mentionsIn`, whole words, case-insensitive). Item numbers, DevOps links, project names and email addresses no longer hold a message: they were the false matches, and a project name or a number alone says nothing to someone outside Cooptimize. |
| D1157 | Done | GD-2: a held message is listed (`conversations.held`) only to the people its message is already visible to (the store's `viewer`, D521), with the name it matched, why, and the message as Teams shows it. Sort for <client> by hand (`conversations.move` into an open conversation or "new") and Not work-related (`conversations.held_not_work`, placed by a person) never call AI. Leave held is no action: retention purges it like any message. |
| D1158 | Done | GD-2: only words travel. A sorting or drafting request may carry another client's name as it appears in the words it is given (`mentions.input: "words"` on the task), never another client's records: no other client's items, projects, summaries or house style go in a prompt, and the guard's item, project and record rules still apply. Secret shapes, the VM and outside addresses still refuse. |
| D1159 | Done | GD-2: AI text that becomes DevOps text (Break down, Create item from a conversation or a capture, the follow-through comment and description, the status report's draft, meeting proposals) is told in its instructions not to name other clients, and is then scrubbed (`mentions.answer: "scrub"`): any other client's name or alias left is replaced with "another client", and the result says so (`namesRemoved`, the UI's "Another client's name was removed"). Scrubbing instead of refusing: GD-1's refusal threw away a paid answer for one word. Internal's tasks are never scrubbed. |
| D1160 | Done | GD-2: every exit is checked (`exitCheck`): Close the loop (Teams, email, DevOps comment, call note), every DevOps write that carries text (create, Break down's batch, comment, a state change's comment, description, the status report's lines) and meeting proposals. A flag names the client and where the text goes, with Replace with "another client" (`mentions: "replace"` on the preview, which rewrites and previews again). Typed text can still go as written: confirm needs `mentionsOk` when the preview was flagged, so the person saw it (409 code `mentions` otherwise). The confirm button reads "Save as written" or "Send as written" while flagged. |
| D1161 | Done | GD-2: an exit checks more than sorting does: besides names and aliases, another client's DevOps projects, its known item ids and any link whose address names it (a link is replaced whole, "another client's link"), because what goes out is read whole by the client. A chat message's numbers and links are left alone when sorting. |
| D1162 | Done | GD-2: in a batch only the flagged rows wait for `mentionsOk`; unflagged rows are written. |
| D1163 | Done | GD-2: undo writes are not checked: they put back what was there. |
| D1164 | Done | GD-2: feedback (Send feedback) still refuses another client's name: it goes to FocusTime's own backlog, which every Cooptimizer can read, and there's no reason for a client name there. |
| D1165 | Done | GD-2: the assistant's proposal text is scrubbed like any AI draft; what the person typed is checked at the exit. |
| D1166 | Done | GD-2: CV-1's follow-through rule stays: lines from an internal chat are left out of a suggested comment unless the person includes them. |
| D1167 | Done | GD-2: meeting transcripts are still masked before AI (Client calls are client-facing by nature). |
| D1168 | Done | GD-2: the "mentions <client>" tag: a conversation stores the clients its messages named (`conversations.mentions_json`, migration `20261121T0900_conversation_mentions`, added only), shown in the Inbox row and the detail pane to whoever can see the conversation. The tag uses the client's display name from the person's clients, else its key. |
| D1169 | Done | GD-2: names come from Dataverse clients plus an aliases Rule per client (Admin › Clients, Other names), the team's, not a person's. The demo seeds each client's full name as an alias where it differs from its key ("Harbor Health" for Harbor), so "Harbor Health" is replaced whole. |
| D1170 | Done | GD-2: a draft that GD-1 refused (`LEGACY_DRAFT_REFUSED`, `DRAFT_HELD`) is prepared again once, now with the scrub. |
| D1171 | Done | GD-2: logs keep counts only: `ai scrub` with the count of names replaced, `held` counts in the pipeline's result, never the matched name or the text. |

## CW-1: Co-working call (Call live · Join, Start a call) (Nov 7)

Numbered D1172 to D1185 in HK-7. On `feat/coworking-call`.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| D1172 | Done | CW-1: Graph. Call events come through the shared poll's existing read, `GET /chats/{id}/messages?$orderby=lastModifiedDateTime desc&$filter=lastModifiedDateTime gt <seen>` with the chat's follower's delegated `Chat.Read` (the documented least privilege for listing chat messages; system event messages are returned by it, per Graph's "Get system messages" page). A call event is a chatMessage whose `eventDetail` has `@odata.type` `#microsoft.graph.callStartedEventMessageDetail` (callId, callEventType, initiator) or `#microsoft.graph.callEndedEventMessageDetail` (also callDuration and callParticipants). FocusTime recognises them by `eventDetail`, not `messageType`: without the `Prefer: include-unknown-enum-members` header Graph labels them `unknownFutureValue`, with it `systemEventMessage`; the poll doesn't send the header and doesn't need to. No new permission, no new request, no Prefer header, no change notifications. |
| D1173 | Done | CW-1: what counts. `callEventType` `call` (a group call started from the chat) and `meeting` (a Meet now in it); `screenShare` alone doesn't. A missing callEventType counts as a call. Several open calls in one chat: the most recently started one shows. |
| D1174 | Done | CW-1: what is kept. One row per call (`chat_calls`, an app table like `teams_messages`): chat, call id, client, started and ended times, the starter's user id and tenant. Never `callParticipants`, a name from the event, the message body, a recording or a transcript. An end seen without its start keeps the start from `callDuration` (and is never live); a start seen after its end doesn't reopen it. Rows go 48 hours after the call started (at the next poll), and with their chat (Remove, Delete stored messages). |
| D1175 | Done | CW-1: "who's on it" isn't shown. Only the call ended event lists participants, so while a call is live Graph's chat messages say only who started it; knowing who is on it now would need the calls or online meetings APIs and a new permission (open question Q-CW1-1). The starter is named from the chat's cached members when they are at Cooptimize; anyone outside Cooptimize (another tenant, no tenant, a guest) shows as "someone outside Cooptimize", never by name, as with external members elsewhere; an unknown starter shows no line. |
| D1176 | Done | CW-1: the stale limit is 10 hours from the start (`STALE_CALL_HOURS` in `plan/cowork.ts`): longer than a working day's co-working call, short enough that a call whose end event never came is gone by the next morning. The live rule also ignores a start more than 5 minutes in the future (clock skew). |
| D1177 | Done | CW-1: freshness. The existing poll: every 5 minutes (the Rule `conv.poll_minutes`, D604) per registered chat with reading on, only in the union of its followers' working hours (D514). A call shows within about 5 minutes of starting or ending, plus up to a minute for the page (it asks `chats.calls` every minute, which reads only FocusTime's own rows). Not made faster: co-working calls run for hours, so 5 minutes is enough to see one and join, and a faster poll would multiply every chat's reads all day for the few minutes it saves. Volume: no extra Graph request at all; the same one read per chat per 5 minutes (12 an hour per chat, about 100 a working day), far under Graph's Teams throttling limits for chat messages, which are counted in requests a second per app, per tenant and per chat (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/throttling-limits). Call events add a few rows a day per chat (a start and an end per call; expect 0 to 4 calls a chat a day). |
| D1178 | Done | CW-1: only chats registered for a client count (both labels, Delivery team and Customer collaboration, are a client's delivery chats in the glossary). A chat with reading off (D523) is never read, so it never shows a live call, but Start a call still works for it. An unregistered chat is never read; `saveCalls` drops events for a chat that isn't registered. |
| D1179 | Done | CW-1: visibility. The server answers `chats.calls` with only the registered chats the person is in now (the cached member list, D521) and whose client they follow (D515), the same rule as a chat's messages. The UI shows a live call only on the person's own blocks for that client (Week) and their own Now card (Today). So someone sees it only when they could see both that client's block and that chat. |
| D1180 | Done | CW-1: where it shows. On Week, on that client's first card today that hasn't ended (the block under way, else the next one), not on every card of the client and not on other days. On Today, on the Now card (what's next) when its block's client has a live call. The block's detail panel shows it too, with Start a call for the other chats. The phone shows the same line on the Week card and the Now card, with Join at 44 px. |
| D1181 | Done | CW-1: Join and Start a call open the same link: the chat in Teams (its Graph `webUrl` when it is a teams.microsoft.com link, else `https://teams.microsoft.com/l/chat/<chat id>/conversations`, the same as Open in Teams). Teams' chat header shows Join for a live call and the call buttons otherwise. Teams' `/l/call/` deep link was not used: it starts a call with listed people, outside the chat, so the call wouldn't be in the delivery chat where co-workers look for it. |
| D1182 | Done | CW-1: Start a call is in the block's ⋯ (a Teams group: Join for live chats first, then Start a call), the block's detail panel (the "on a client's block" spot, so cards don't grow a button each), and the command menu ("Start a call", every chat I can see with its client). Not on every Week card: a button on every block would crowd the cards for something used a few times a day. |
| D1183 | Done | CW-1: never a conversation, promise, chatter or AI input. `messageOf` already returns null for system messages, so call events never reach `teams_messages`, the conversations pipeline, the Inbox or the AI; `callEventOf` reads them into their own list and table. Tested end to end with the real Graph client on fake data through the poll, the pipeline and the stub AI (`actions/cowork.test.ts`). |
| D1184 | Done | CW-1: the command menu's action is `chats.calls` ("Start a call", group Week). It is a read; nothing new is written anywhere outside FocusTime's own table. |
| D1185 | Done | CW-1: the Teams Bot and Meeting Capture scope's Part 3 isn't in the repo (only `docs/scope/ai-and-teams-scope-v0.2.md` and the asks scope); built from the brief's summary of it (indicator from chat system events, Start a call shortcut, never conversations or chatter, never recorded). GD-2 isn't merged; nothing here depends on it. |

## IN-2

Send invites from FocusTime through Close the loop's send path (Nov 7). Unnumbered until merge.

"Send from FocusTime" has Send in Teams and an email button whose label follows the choice beside it: Outlook draft (the default) or Send now, chosen per send, as LP-1 does. |

The sends are LP-1's `SendClient` (`sources/graph/send.ts`: `POST /chats` for the 1:1, `POST /chats/{id}/messages`, `POST /me/messages` with the immutable id, `POST /me/messages/{id}/send`, and the same read backs) on the same per-person tokens, and LP-1's consent probe (`sendAccess`, now exported from `actions/loop-actions.ts`; `sendProbe` in `actions/dispatch.ts` shared by both). The only change to the shared path: a draft may have several people on To (1 to 50), for one email to everyone. Nothing new is asked of Microsoft. |
| Done | IN-2: Teams is always one 1:1 chat per person (the preview lists each message). The 1:1 binds the person by their work address (Graph accepts a UPN), as LP-1 does for a Cooptimizer without an Entra id in the chat; someone who hasn't signed in has no `users` row, so the address is what FocusTime has. |
| Done | IN-2: email to several is **one email each by default**, each with their own first name; "One email to everyone" (one message, everyone on To, "Hi all") is the other choice. Why each by default: a failure is per person and Retry reaches only them, the greeting is personal, and it matches Teams. IN-1's Open in Outlook still opens one email to everyone. |
| Done | IN-2: the preview shows how it goes ("Teams: a 1:1 chat with each of the 3, as you"; "Email, an Outlook draft in your mailbox: one email each to 3 people, as you"), who gets it, the subject and every message's final text (personalized), and that sent messages can't be undone (a draft: that it waits in Drafts). It is held on the server five minutes for that admin only and used once. |
| Done | IN-2: what counts as sent, per message, after read back: Teams, the message is in the chat (without `Chat.Read`, Graph's answer with the message id counts, as LP-1); a draft, the message is in Drafts; Send now, it is out of Drafts with a sent time. Then each of its people is recorded in `people_invites` with a new how (`teams_sent`, `email_draft`, `email_sent`; the column is free text, so no migration) and the row reads "Invited by <admin> via Teams, <day>", "via Email" or "via Email (Outlook draft)". IN-1's own opens and copies keep "Invited <day> by <admin>". A draft counts as invited: the admin asked for a draft and FocusTime made it, as IN-1 counts an open. |

No `loop_sends` row: that table is a person's own record of their promises (a user table, in their Export), and an invite is the team's (an app table, for admins). |
| Done | IN-2: partial failure lists each message with ✓ or ✗ and the reason, and the head line names who wasn't reached ("Sent in Teams to 2 of 3. Not sent to Dee Person."). Only the ones that landed are marked invited. Retry acts only on what didn't land: a message Graph took but didn't show is read back again (never posted twice); one that never got there is sent again; a Send now whose send failed keeps its draft, and Retry sends that draft (never a second email). The result is held in memory 30 minutes for that admin; after that (or a restart) Who's set up shows who was invited, and inviting again is fine. |
| Done | IN-2: without the permission the sheet shows only IN-1's buttons with LP-1's note, its second sentence fitted to an invite: "Sending from FocusTime needs an admin to approve access. Until then, open it in Teams or Outlook prefilled and send it there." (no Mark sent here: opening already records it). With the admin's own Allow link per missing permission (LP-1's `/auth/microsoft/login?consent=…`), not in the demo. The sheet asks `admin.invite_access` each time it opens, so once consent is there the send buttons appear with no code or setting change. When only Send now is missing, Send now is off and the note says so. |
| Done | IN-2: recipients are checked again on the server for every preview: in Who's set up (Cooptimizers only by IN-1's rule, so never a guest, another domain or an automation account), not signed in, a valid address, and that address inside the Cooptimize email domains (refused when no domains are set). One person who doesn't qualify refuses the whole send. |

## AS-3: The Assistant answers real questions (Nov 7)

- AS-3: why it gave the canned paragraph: when the intent round named no tool, `run()` in `assistant/service.ts` returned a fixed "I can look things up (what's next, …)" reply. Any question without a lookup tool (planning, "what can you do", "which promises are late") hit it. That path is gone; a question now goes to the answer path, and nothing falls back to a fixed paragraph.
- AS-3: the intent round's schema gains `ask` (topic, period, date, lateOnly) and `followUp`. Topics: plan_check, capabilities, agenda, promises, waiting, readiness, deployments, status_updates, done, calls, items, general. Periods: today, tomorrow, this week, next week, the weekend, last week, a day.
- AS-3: answers are put together on the server (`assistant/answer.ts`) from a bounded context (`assistant/context.ts`) of the person's own data: week.load, ahead.load, status.load, inbox.list and meeting.mine, each row filtered to mine and to the client asked about. The AI only classifies the words, so an answer across all my clients never sends any client's data to the AI (one client per AI call stays true, and D954's instruction scan has nothing to find).
- AS-3: limits: 14 days, 40 to-dos, 12 promises, 10 waiting, 12 gaps, 12 status lines, 6 calls, 10 after-hours parts; at most 8 sections of 12 lines in an answer; the one-client context sent to AI is at most 16,000 characters. A source that fails is named under "Not known" instead of guessed.
- AS-3: a "general" question about one client (no composer fits) is one AI call, `assistant.question` (standard tier, under the same switches and caps), with that client's context as quoted data and a schema of `{reply, itemIds}`; item cards are made only for ids in the context. A general question with several clients asks which client first.
- AS-3: plan_check's thresholds are defaults, fixed for now and possibly settings later (Tanner, Q-AS3-2): a block is over when planned is more than free plus 30 minutes; a day is called out at 4 hours of meetings or under 2 hours free; PTO days are named.
- AS-3: suggested steps are at most 3, from `SUGGEST_TOOLS` (move_plan, confirm_week) only, FocusTime-only changes. A tap builds the proposal on the server without AI and shows the button's preview; Confirm runs as in AS-1. An over block suggests moving to that week's Friday AM: Friday is the overflow day (Tanner, Q-AS3-3). Capabilities' examples are `say` choices, which ask that question.
- AS-3: promises come from the Inbox's promises view (late and due, with their keys), not the week's list, which missed promises outside the week.
- AS-3: the conversation state is 2 hours (`CONVERSATION_MINUTES`, 120; Tanner, Q-AS3-5, was 30 minutes) in the turn's `pending_json` (kinds "context" and "suggest"): the request, client, topic, period, tools and the items it referred to. No migration: it lives in `assistant_turns`, the person's own, already in Export and Clear and never shown to admins. A stale suggestion tap says it expired.
- AS-3: a follow-up sends the earlier words to the intent round masked (client names and projects as [client], ids as #[id]); the server resolves the combined words and keeps the earlier client.
- AS-3: features resolve by name from phrases ("under the X feature", "the feature name is X", "the X items"), by containment in feature and epic titles; several matches are AS-2's question with choices.
- AS-3: Teams: the same answers; sections become cards with at most 2 link buttons (3 overflowed), suggestions a question card with the same choices, up to 7 cards a reply.
- AS-3: GD-2: answers never leave the panel or my own 1:1 bot chat. Suggestions write only FocusTime's plan; any DevOps write still goes through its own preview and exit check.
- AS-3: answers stay written by FocusTime from data, not phrased by the AI (Tanner, Q-AS3-4). AI phrasing per client is a possible later option.
- AS-3: holidays. Cooptimize's holidays are in Dataverse's `coop_workingdayscalendar` (Working Days Calendar, entity set `coop_workingdayscalendars`, user owned): one row per calendar day. Columns that matter: `coop_date` (Date, DateOnly), `coop_observedholidayflag` ("Holiday Observed", Yes/No) and `coop_holidayname` ("Holiday Name", text). It also has `coop_workdayflag`, `coop_workdayflagexclholidays`, `coop_half`, week, month, quarter, year and pay period columns. No column limits a holiday to some people, offices or regions (no lookup to a user, office or region; only the record owner), so an observed holiday is a day off for everyone at Cooptimize. The rule to build: only rows with `coop_observedholidayflag` true, on exactly `coop_date`, shown as "<Holiday Name> (observed)" or "Holiday".
- AS-3: the holidays reader isn't built: FocusTime's Dataverse app user (role "Coopilot Reader") can read the table's metadata but not its rows (403, `prvReadcoop_WorkingDaysCalendar`). Stopped there as asked; no permission was added (Q-AS3-6).
- AS-3: PTO's source confirmed: `coop_timeofftracker` (entity set `coop_timeofftrackers`, mine by `_coop_employee_value`, dates only) and its day rows in `coop_timeofftrackerdetails` (`sources/dataverse/source.ts` `pto`, columns in `week/pto.ts`).

## SR-3: Report preview you can finish (Nov 8)

- SR-3: Upcoming PTO and Holidays covers the next two weeks from the report's Monday (`UPCOMING_DAYS`, 14; SR-2 used four). Kat's window isn't known (Q-SR3-1), so the brief's fallback is used. Rows are in date order; on one date the holiday comes before people, then people by name. A person's run of days stays one line ("Tue, Nov 10 to Wed, Nov 11"), as in SR-2.
- SR-3: holidays come from Dataverse `coop_workingdayscalendars` (AS-3's table): `$select=coop_date,coop_holidayname,coop_observedholidayflag`, filtered to `coop_observedholidayflag eq true` and the date range, by GET with the app's access (`sources/dataverse/holidays.ts`, `holidays` in `source.ts`). A row counts only when Holiday Observed is exactly true, on the date the row has (an observed day off from the traditional date is kept as it is). It reads "<Holiday Name> (observed)", or "Holiday" when unnamed; names are trimmed to 80 characters. Names and dates only.
- SR-3: each source fails on its own. "PTO couldn't be read (…)" or "Holidays couldn't be read (…)" shows under the section, and the rest of the report still shows. Until the reader role gets Read on the Working Days Calendar (Q-AS3-6), production shows the holidays note. That is expected, not a bug.
- SR-3: inline lines. Every feature in the edit view has "+ Add a line" (UI-10's growing box). Enter adds the line and opens the next empty one under the same feature; Shift+Enter is a new line in the same comment; Esc closes (an empty line is dropped, and an edit that changes nothing is dropped). Tab goes to the next feature's "+ Add a line" and Shift+Tab to the previous one, skipping folded lists. A closed line stays as a pending row (click to edit) until Push all. The rules are pure (`ui/status-lines.ts`).
- SR-3: the header counts what Push all would write ("4 lines to push") beside Push all. SR-1's "N ready to push" in the count line was dropped, so there aren't two counts.
- SR-3: my own comments in the window are edited by clicking their text (SR-1's `comment_edit`). Others' and Coop's stay read only, as in SR-1.
- SR-3: Delete my own comment is a new named write, `comment_delete` (with `undo_comment_delete`), on SR-1's write path. It is allowed only for my own comment, inside this week's report window, never Coop's. The preview shows the text, then a confirm, `test /rev`, read back (the comment is gone), and the audit log keeps the text. Undo posts the text again as a new comment, so its id changes; a Retry adopts the existing one rather than posting twice. This answers Q-SR2-5, since the brief asks for delete, and CLAUDE.md's DevOps rule names it.
- SR-3: storage is rollback safe. A `comment_delete` is stored as `write_kind = "comment_edit"` with `deleted: true` in its ops, and `op` stays "comment" (as CV-1 did for newer ops). There's no migration, and an older build reads it as a comment edit. Its undo kind is newer than the CHECK too and goes in `write_kind`.
- SR-3: Add a feature (client report) is AH-5's filterable list (`CellPopover` and `FilterList`) over the client's whole feature tier. It matches the title or #id and shows each feature's state and owner. Features already on screen (the report, Needs your update, No update yet) aren't offered again. The catalog is in backlog order, 2,000 at most. A picked feature is read again with its comments (`status.report` `added`, 30 at most, this client's ids only) and placed in its stack-rank position with its box open. It stays pending in this browser for that report date (`focustime.status.added`) until a line is pushed or Remove takes it out. It is in Monday's report only once a comment is pushed, as Kat's rule says. SR-2's visibility rule applies. The feature's owner isn't notified (the DevOps comment notifies its followers as usual).
- SR-3: the contribution rule ("You worked on this"), since the client's report window opened (SR-1's watermark), on features only. Any one of these counts:
  - a story or task under the feature (at any depth), or the feature itself, assigned to me and changed in DevOps since the window opened (`System.ChangedDate`, already read by `aheadItems`);
  - my state change or comment on such a story (DV-1's change log, rows marked mine);
  - a story I created under it through FocusTime (the write log's confirmed creates). DevOps' own creator isn't read, so a story created outside FocusTime counts only once it's assigned to me and changed (Q-SR3-3);
  - a FocusTime to-do of mine (not dropped, its day in the window) or a timebox of mine in the window on the feature or an item under it;
  - my comment on the feature itself.
  No new DevOps read: everything comes from what Status already reads plus the plan store.
- SR-3: Needs your update means I worked on the feature and have no comment of mine on it since the watermark (not Coop's). A comment of mine clears it; others' comments don't. Such features get the "You worked on this" badge and a short line of what counted ("2 stories · your to-dos"). In My features they go to a Needs your update group at the top of each client, and in the client report at its top. The header adds "3 features need your update". SR-2's "No update yet" stays for everyone else's features.
- SR-3: "AI suggests" is a new read, `status.suggest`: one AI request per client, for the Needs your update features asked about (40 at most). Its input is that client's facts only: story titles and state changes, my to-dos and close-out notes, and linked captures (SR-1's `myFacts`), plus "Worked on #id title". It runs through `ai/` with the client's Allow AI and AI for FocusTime, under the person's and the app's caps. GD-2 point 5 applies: the prompt says never name another client, and the check after generation replaces one with "another client" and the screen says so. Cooptimize Internal is exempt, by `ai/`'s own rule. Answers are kept in memory 6 hours by a hash of the input (500 at most), so reloading doesn't spend again. Nothing is stored.
- SR-3: AI tier: standard (`ai/tiers.ts` STANDARD_TASKS), not flex. The suggestion is asked when the person opens Status and they wait for it, as with status_draft.
- SR-3: with AI off, or not allowed for the client, there is no AI call. The suggestion is the template "Worked on: <story titles>" (four at most, then "and N more"; the feature's title when no story counted), labelled "Suggested line".
- SR-3: Use puts the suggestion in a pending line; Edit opens it in a box; Dismiss hides it for that report date in this browser. Nothing goes to DevOps before Push all.
- SR-3: Push all previews everything at once, grouped by client and then by feature, each line with what it does (new comment, edit your comment, delete your comment). Each client shows its own "Who can see it", not the first client's for all. GD-2's exit check runs per batch as in SR-1, with the one-click Replace. Then come the write framework's writes (read back, audit, undo per line).
- SR-3: the nudge counts Needs your update first: "3 features you worked on need an update for Monday · 2 more have no update yet" (`nudgeLineFor`), in the app and in Teams. With any, the button is "Open Needs your update" and it opens `#/week/status/needs` (route `status_needs`), which scrolls to the group. The Teams card marks those features "you worked on this".
- SR-3: the phone writes too: Add a line, Use, Edit and Dismiss, and Push all, with 44 px targets. This replaces SR-1 and SR-2's view-only phone (D863's choice for Status). Checked at 390 with 17 px and 21 px root fonts and no horizontal scroll.
- SR-3: the demo adds two fictional observed holidays ("Founders Day" on the report week's Thursday, an unnamed one the next Monday). Its suggestion for Harbor names another client, so GD-2's replacement shows.
- SR-3: the Guide's note is "Finish your status update before Monday" (no real names, as the Guide's test requires).

Two that fit equally are unmatched, never a guess, and the preview says what wasn't matched. No words from the model: it reads what follows "with", "including" and the like in my request. ## IB-2

Inbox: one "Everything" timeline, status filter, Teams-style timestamps (Nov 8).

| ID | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | IB-2: one model. Everything is the list; a view on the left is Everything with only that status on, and lights that chip. All views draw the same rows, so the old Chatter list is gone: a chat's chatter is a row with status Chatter. A chip matches a row in any of its views, so a chip's count is the view's count. |
| - | Done | IB-2: each row has one status tag, the first of Done, Needs you, Handled by teammates, Waiting on, In progress, Promise, Capture, Chatter that it's in (`STATUS_ORDER`), the same whichever filter is on. Late, due, `#id`, Asked you, To sort and the like stay as a second tag; words that only repeat the status are dropped. The kind icon gave way to the status tag. |
| - | Done | IB-2: the place comes from the address, not a setting. A bare #/inbox is always Everything, including for people who had saved another view under IB-1, because the brief makes it the default. `inbox_view` is no longer read for that or written, and stays valid for an older build. A chip remembers the set for Everything; clicking a left view doesn't overwrite it, so Everything comes back as I left it. In a left view, a second chip lands on Everything with both, and the view's own chip goes back to Everything. |
| - | Done | IB-2: new settings keys (`inbox_sort`, `inbox_statuses`, `inbox_grouping`), never new values in the old ones: an older build rejects an unknown value in `inbox_group` as an error but skips an unknown key (BH-1), so a rollback can't break anyone's settings. Group's Kind becomes Status, as the brief lists it. |
| - | Done | IB-2: last looked lives in the person's `kv` row (`inbox_last_looked`), an instant, not a settings row (it's written often and isn't a preference) and needs no migration. In Export and Delete like the rest of `kv`. `inbox.looked` only moves it forward, so an older device can't move it back. |
| - | Done | IB-2: when the marker moves. The page reads it when Everything opens and holds it for the visit. It moves on the server when the divider has come into view, or at once when there is none to reach (nothing new, everything new, not grouped by day, first ever visit). So oldest first with the divider far down doesn't lose it until I've been there. It's set to the time of the list I read, so what arrives after that stays new. The brief's "opened": filters count as opened, so new items a filter hides aren't dotted next time. |
| - | Done | IB-2: the divider only shows grouped by day, the one order where new items are together; the dots show in every grouping. In a view on the left the divider and dots show too, but only opening Everything moves the marker. |
| - | Done | IB-2: list loading. All rows in one read, drawn 120 at a time as the end comes near, memoized rows, `content-visibility: auto` on rows. No virtualization library (a new dependency needs asking, and fixed-height windowing fights the sticky headings and rows of varying height). J and K go past what's drawn. |
| - | Done | IB-2: latest activity. Conversations were already their latest message and chat promises their last update. A promise I added had only opened and closed, so `follow_ups.changed_at` is added (`20261123T0900`, only adds; export version 35) and set by every edit, snooze, close and reopen; older rows fall back to closed, then opened. A draft uses its `updated_at`. A capture can't be edited, so created is its last change. |
| - | Done | IB-2: Done reaches back as far as conversations. Promises in Done followed a 1-day window and conversations the Closed shown days Rule (14 by default), so a timeline would have lost promises after a day. Promises now follow the Rule too (`inbox.list`, `my_promises.list`), and the Done view's words changed to match. Beyond that window an item isn't in the Inbox, as before. |
| - | Done | IB-2: inside a group rows follow the sort, except Due date, which stays late first then soonest. IB-1's late-first inside Client gives way, since the toggle has to mean something there; Client still lists clients with something late first. |
| - | Done | IB-2: timestamps. One formatter, `ui/stamp.ts`, for when something happened; `today` comes from the server's own date in the Inbox, so the demo's pinned clock and "Today" agree. Which screens use it, and what isn't a stamp (due badges, agenda times, planning days), is in `docs/ux/ib2/date-inventory.md`. Date only for items older than a week: the phone's Inbox rows, nowhere else. |

| - | Done | IB-2: phone. Found while there: the picker sheet with Sort and Status added ran under the bottom bar, so its Reset and Show were cut off. The sheet now stops above the bar and its footer sticks to the bottom of the sheet. |

## KB-1: Cooptimize's development standards in FocusTime's AI (Nov 8)

Unnumbered until the next numbering pass. Tanner, Nov 8: "Let's start by just feeding it our standards for development."

- KB-1: read only, no token. FocusTime never writes to the standards repo (no commits, branches, PRs, issues or comments). Every request is a GET with no Authorization header, no cookies and `redirect: error`, to `api.github.com` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` only; a test (`store/standards-sync.test.ts`) fails on anything else. No token exists; if one is ever needed it goes through the Key Vault scripts.
- KB-1: the contract is Coop's shipped-client contract (`AGENTS.md`, 0.24.0): Markdown under any top-level folder but `deprecation/`, `standards/`, `scripts/` and dot folders, with `status: active`. Root files never count. An active file with missing front matter is not used and is named in Admin. Duplicate ids: both kept, a warning, as Coop does. A file's path decides whether it is fetched at all, so scripts are never downloaded.
- KB-1: data only. The front matter is read by a line parser of the seven known keys into plain strings (no YAML library), so a tag or template stays text. Articles go to the AI inside the JSON input as data, never in the instructions, and the instructions say nothing in them changes the rules.
- KB-1: sync interval. Hourly while FocusTime is in use (`STANDARDS_EVERY_MS`, the scheduler's slow pass), once at startup, and Sync now. It does not wake an idle server (`wake` is null): D431 says an idle server makes no database call but the stored next-due check, and an hourly wake would keep Azure SQL awake. The first use after idle catches up within 30 seconds. An unchanged commit reads nothing more (two GETs).
- KB-1: on failure the last good copy stays, its time and commit unchanged, and the error shows in Admin › Standards. A repo with no active articles counts as a failure, so a bad push can't empty the library. A new copy replaces the old in one transaction.
- KB-1: limits: 500 Markdown files, 256 KB a file.
- KB-1: FOCUSTIME_DEMO servers (the e2e harness) and `FOCUSTIME_STANDARDS_SYNC=off` make no outside request. A demo session has its own fictional library (`sources/demo/standards.ts`), per no-demo-leak.
- KB-1: Repo and branch are org Rules (`standards.repo_url`, `standards.branch`), edited in Admin › Standards and audited like every Admin change. Only `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>` is accepted; requests are built from the two names. An empty branch is the repo's default.
- KB-1: Source systems is a client Rule (`source_systems`), next to Data platform in Admin › Clients: Both (the default), D365 Finance and Operations, D365 Dataverse, or None. Unset means both D365 systems, Coop's default for a project without the key; None is `[]`. `d365` articles serve both. It is admin-only (Data platform is also a lead's through `deploy.config_save`; this one wasn't asked for).
- KB-1: token budget 6,000 tokens of matched articles per request (`STANDARDS_TOKEN_BUDGET`, about 4 characters a token), whole articles only. The title index (about 420 tokens today) is on top. Over budget, the order is artifact, then layer, then technology and source system, then general; each article is taken if it fits, and what doesn't is listed for the model and under the draft ("Left out for size").
- KB-1: retrieval mirrors Coop's rules and differs where planning text needs it. Keys are matched exactly, in snake_case, against the text's words and runs of up to three words (plural stripped). Differences from Coop: (1) a domain comes into play only through a specific key (a layer, technology, domain name, or a multi-word artifact), because planning text is full of ordinary words: "a customer view" isn't Gold Views; (2) an artifact two domains share (`fact_table`) counts only when nothing else says which, so "Gold fact table" is SQL; (3) a technology every article of a domain shares (`power_bi`) only names the domain, and that domain's layer-agnostic articles count as its general ones; (4) a data platform's article (`fabric_*`, `azure_*`) joins by the client's platform or its exact technology, never a loose title word; (5) Ask takes a source-system article only when the question is about it.
- KB-1: source-system articles ride on any SQL or Power BI work, as in Coop, or when named. None excludes them even when named.
- KB-1: precedence. Client or project requirements override the library inside that project. The instructions say to follow the client and say so; Break down states it in `rationale` (the Coop v1 schema is shared, so it isn't changed), capture and conversation drafts in a new `standards_note`, added to their schemas only when standards go with the request. A "Client override:" line shows under Standards used.
- KB-1: standards carry no client data, so they may go in any client's prompt; GD-2 still applies to everything else. An article's text goes with `mentions: { input: "words" }` tasks as those tasks always did.
- KB-1: after the AI, FocusTime does three things in code: adds the article's link to a "Follows <title>: ..." line (the model never writes a link, and any it did is replaced); drops a "Follows" line that names a standard not sent with this request; rewords hand-written Bronze or Silver SQL as "Configure or regenerate" and says so under the draft ("Create fact.Sales from Silver tables" stays).
- KB-1: links in DevOps. `textToHtml` makes `https://github.com/...` a real link, so a citation is clickable in the item; it is a link to a public repo.
- KB-1: Standards used lists the articles sent with the request, not only the ones cited, because all of them shaped the answer. A title opens the article at the synced commit.
- KB-1: a draft feature keeps its "Follows" lines when standards were sent (a feature otherwise has no acceptance criteria from AI).
- KB-1: where the AI drafts DevOps text: Break down and its Regenerate (`stories.draft`), Draft story and Draft feature (`capture.prepare`, also reached by the assistant's `capture` proposal), and create item from a conversation (`conversations.draft_item`). The assistant's other proposals (`comment`, `description`) carry the person's own words, so they don't use standards. Not changed: `meeting.capture`, `conversations.comment`, status drafts (Q-KB1-3).
- KB-1: Ask. A new `standards` topic in the intent round and one task, `assistant.standards`, with no client (no client data goes). No matching article is "Not in the standards" without an AI call. The answer must name an article that was sent and quote it word for word (markup and spacing aside), checked in code, or it is also "Not in the standards". A failed AI is said plainly, never guessed. With no sync yet, it says the standards haven't been read.
- KB-1: Export. `draft_stories.ai_standards` is a new user column (schema 36); `standards_articles` and `standards_sync` are app tables, never exported.
- KB-1: logs carry outcomes and counts only (`sync` `updated`, `unchanged`, `failed`).


## GD-3: re-check held messages under GD-2, show why each is held (Nov 8)

Unnumbered until merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | GD-3: why they were still held. Dev's last chat poll was Nov 7 08:01 UTC (the logs); GD-2 merged about 18 hours later and polling runs only in working hours, so GD-2's retry of old holds had not run on dev. The retry itself covers client-facing chats too (`legacyRefused` picks `refused_client` and `other_client` in every chat), so the brief's "only internal chats" holds only for GD-2's own `mentions_client`. The 4 guard refusals logged on Nov 5 and 6 were all `input:work_item`: one number held its whole batch. |
| - | Done | GD-3: the re-check (`actions/held-recheck.ts` `recheckHeld`) puts every held message (`HELD_REASONS`), internal and client-facing, through the normal pipeline, so there is one rule: a name or alias in a client-facing chat stays held, the rest are sorted (Flex tier, the app's cap), an internal chat sorts everything. It reads only stored messages, never Teams. |
| - | Done | GD-3: how it runs: once, at the first start of this build (`heldRecheckOnce`, marked in `org_settings` scope `meta`, key `held_recheck_v1`, with its counts, in the admin audit like CV-3b's repair). Not tied to working hours, since it reads nothing from Teams. Marked only when every chat could be worked out; a chat whose boundary failed leaves it unmarked and the next start runs again. Idempotent: released messages are no longer held, and a real mention is only placed again, with no AI call. |
| - | Done | GD-3: the re-check never releases on no knowledge: no follower with a config, a boundary that fails, or an empty directory in a client-facing chat leaves the messages held and counted `unresolved`. It matches against the union of the chat's followers' directories, so a client only one of them follows still matches; this is the safe side for a one-time release. |
| - | Done | GD-3: what a message matched is recorded when it is held (`conversation_messages.held_match_json`, migration `20261124T0900_held_match`, added only): the client's key and the word as the message has it. Recorded rather than worked out on reading, because directories differ per person, and everyone who can see the chat must see the same reason. Moving a message off held clears it. Logs carry counts only. |
| - | Done | GD-3: the row says "Mentions <word as matched>" with that word marked in the message (`highlightMatches`, on the server, only in text between tags of the already sanitized HTML), shown through the same viewer as before (GD-2's rule). With no recorded match: "Reason unknown", logged as a count (`held_reason_unknown`). Chats with reading off are skipped by the re-check, so a message held there before reading was turned off can still show it until it expires. |
| - | Done | GD-3: a guard refusal on the client rule now happens only when a chat's boundary couldn't be read (with it, a chat's words pass the rule). No client was matched, so it is no longer stored as held: it becomes `no_boundary`, retryable, and is tried again once the boundary reads. So no new `other_client` rows are written. `legacyRefused` stays, as a second path for any old row the re-check skipped. |
| - | Done | GD-3: wording. "They're checked again with the chat's next messages" is gone. A held message "stays held until someone sorts it or marks it not work-related, or a client's names change" (`HELD_STAYS`), in the Inbox line, the list and the Guide. |
| - | Done | GD-3: editing a client's Other names or Match only as written re-checks, in the background, the held messages that concern that client: those in its own chats and those matched against it. An added alias doesn't re-hold anything already sorted; holding only happens as a message is first sorted. |
| - | Done | GD-3: bulk actions, per client, one line each at the top of the list (`conversations.held_bulk`): "Sort all for <client> by hand" makes one new conversation per chat (a conversation belongs to one chat), first message's title, each named client tagged, no AI; "Mark all not work-related" moves them to Chatter, placed by a person. Each asks first with the count, inline so it works on the phone, and the action sends that count: if the list changed since, it is refused and nothing moves. They act on every held message I can see, past the list's 200. |
