## TX-1: XS size, timeboxes as work sessions, progress check-ins (Nov 8)

Unnumbered until merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | TX-1: "All went to plan", the rule: for each to-do still unanswered, add up the length of its ended timeboxes. If that covers its time left (timeboxed >= left), it is Done (spent goes up by the timeboxed time, left 0, PK-1's offer applies). Otherwise the timeboxed time counts as spent and left goes down by it; the to-do stays planned. It never asks anything and never touches an answered row. Undo is per row. |
| - | Done | TX-1: Spent and left live on `picks` (`spent_minutes`, `remaining_minutes`, null until the first answer) and are written on every pick of the same DevOps item, so a carried or re-picked item keeps its progress; a typed to-do has only its own row. Week stories read theirs from those picks. `pickNeed` is the one place that says what a to-do needs: done or resolved 0, then left, then its own minutes, then its size. Spent never comes from the focus timer: focus time stays what Close out reports, and only a check-in answer moves time left, because a session can be interrupted or be on something else. |
| - | Done | TX-1: XS is stored as a flag (`size_xs`) next to the existing `size` column, which holds S for those rows. The column's CHECK and SQL Server's nvarchar(1) take only S, M and L, a rebuild of `picks` isn't an adding migration, and this way a previous build reads an XS as S, so a rollback is safe. Reads return XS. Minimum useful time for XS is 15 minutes, not a setting. |
| - | Done | TX-1: FocusTime never writes S, M or L to DevOps (only Ahead's committed numeric size), so XS has nothing to map there. Ahead's choices for a numeric size field gain 0.5 so an XS-sized story can be recorded in hours; T-shirt or story point fields are unchanged. |
| - | Done | TX-1: A timebox's length is its own. Placing in a gap (drag, Timebox my day, + time) defaults to the smaller of time left and the gap, on the 15 minute grid; a drop onto a meeting keeps its length (TY-1). Stretching or shrinking never changes the size. The shortest useful time (`minSizes`) still decides which gaps are offered. |
| - | Done | TX-1: A block's fit keeps step 12's rule: a timeboxed to-do counts by its timebox's length, an untimed one by what's left. A short timebox on a 2h story means "only this much here", and the existing plan tests encode that. Week capacity counts what's left. An answered partly or none session stops counting in the block. |
| - | Done | TX-1: A check-in lists a to-do once, adding up its ended timeboxes on that day. Not asked: a timebox with no to-do, a to-do already done, resolved in DevOps or carried, one already answered. Midday is about today only. End of day is any day up to today that hasn't been closed out; once closed out, the unanswered stay as they were and are never asked again, which is the "after end of day, left" rule. Skipped rows roll into the next moment because nothing marks them. |
| - | Done | TX-1: Didn't get to it: the timebox is removed and the to-do is back to not timeboxed, left unchanged. An Outlook timebox is left on Outlook (nothing external changes from a check-in) and only freed from the to-do. |
| - | Done | TX-1: Answers apply at once and Undo restores from `timeboxes.settle_before` (status, spent, left as they were). There's no toast: Undo stays on the row until the page closes, and in Teams on the card. |
| - | Done | TX-1: Re-sizing work under way moves the time left by the same amount, at least 15 minutes, on a to-do and on a week list story; size is the estimate and left is the state. |
| - | Done | TX-1: The DevOps offer is end of day only, once a day (`markOnce("progress_offer", date)`), for partly answers only: a Done goes through PK-1's own offer, and DevOps clears Remaining Work when the item is closed. Didn't get to it has no news. Offer is read from the process model per type: `Microsoft.VSTS.Scheduling.RemainingWork` if the type has it (written in hours, 0.5 steps kept), a short comment otherwise. One preview for the batch, confirm, `test /rev`, read back, audit log, Undo per row. Comments go through GD-2's exit check. Close out only, so nothing asks at midday. |
| - | Done | TX-1: Stock Agile's User Story has no Remaining Work (Task and Bug do), so the comment is the common case on those processes. FocusTime doesn't write to child Tasks: the story is what was timeboxed. |
| - | Done | TX-1: Teams: the card has the same three answers plus All went to plan. The DevOps offer is one line pointing at Close out ("Review it in Close out."), because the preview lives in the app and a card never writes to DevOps without one. Done on a card uses PK-1's `offerDevOps`. Partly re-renders the row with quick picks and a text box, since ShowCard can't be used inside an ActionSet here. |
| - | Done | TX-1: `namedSize` now returns XS for 30 minutes (SZ-1's unsized default can be 30). The focus timer's suggested length uses what's left. Dragging to a block on Week counts what's left. |
| - | Done | TX-1: The demo gives Cooptimize's stories a Remaining Work field and leaves other clients without one, so both paths can be seen there. |

## TX-1b: fixes from the TX-1 review (Nov 8)

Unnumbered until merge. These change TX-1's rows above where they say so.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | TX-1b: An answered timebox keeps its to-do whatever else changes. The store ignores any new link on a settled timebox (a drag, an Outlook toggle or a sync). A read still shows no `pickId` once a partly or none session stopped holding the to-do; `sessionPickId` is always the link. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: Done means the story is done. A check-in Done takes the story's planned to-dos on later days off the plan (status `dropped`, their timeboxes off unless on Outlook), says so once on the row ("Removed from Tue, story is done") and the row's Undo puts them back. A tick outside a check-in is unchanged: ticking off one day's slice of a story was already a normal thing to do. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: Replaces TX-1's "written on every pick". Time left is written on the to-do answered and the item's to-dos still planned. A Done writes only its own to-do and keeps the last time left in the column, read as 0 on a done to-do, so a story planned again picks it up; with none before the Done it uses its size. Undo restores each to-do's own values from `settle_before`. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: A week story's time left is its latest to-do in that week (done: 0), otherwise the last time left it had, otherwise its size. It is read per client for that week's items through a new index, `picks_client_item`, not across the person's history. An open to-do never reads as 0 left, which also repairs rows TX-1 left at 0. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: All went to plan works from the rows as the server reads them when it's pressed, in one transaction: all of them or none. A row answered elsewhere leaves the list, one that ended since joins it, and each applied row carries its own Undo ids. On a failure the row list stays as it was, with the error and Retry. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: TX-1's rule says PK-1's offer applies to All went to plan's Done rows, and the code didn't make it, so the code changed. At midday they get PK-1's batch in the app, and on a Teams card the first one's proposal plus a line sending the rest to Close out, since a card holds one proposal. Close out still asks once for the day. The end of day Remaining Work offer is unchanged. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: Undo of a Done on a Teams card offers to undo the DevOps change it made, the same `item_undo` proposal as the plain row. If a DevOps proposal is still open on the card, the card says the Undo doesn't change DevOps. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: A re-size moves the time left once: not again when the item's other planned to-do or week story already has that size that week. It never goes past the new size; a number the person set above the old size stays theirs and a re-size leaves it alone. A timebox's length never moves it. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: The assistant's to-do minutes use `pickNeed` for planned to-dos, matching the day and block totals. A done to-do still reports what it took. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: Each "How much is left?" box on a Teams card has its own id (`sessionLeft_<pick id>`). A card already in a chat with the old id still works. |
| - | Done | TX-1b: A jump to one setting no longer takes focus if a new search has started by the time the section's data arrives. That late focus was what made the Settings search browser test fail intermittently. |
| - | Done | TX-1c: A plain tick follows the check-in's rule: Done on a to-do takes the same story's planned to-dos on later days off the plan, with their FocusTime timeboxes (one on Outlook stays, as in the check-in), and says so once with Undo. This covers the row's tick, the command menu, focus's Done and the Teams card. The assistant's close out says it in its preview. |
| - | Done | TX-1c: Unticking puts back exactly what that Done removed (`picks.done_removed`, numbers only), including after a check-in's Done. It never brings back a to-do dropped by hand, and never adds a second one on a day the story was planned on again. |
| - | Done | TX-1c: A removed to-do's time: spent stays on the story and the time left is unchanged. A to-do with time on it is kept as dropped instead of deleted, so the week list and the next to-do for that story still have it. A carried copy handed back gives its time to the original. A removed to-do never says whether the story is done. |
| - | Done | TX-1c: A new to-do takes the story's latest time left and the most time spent, from any of its to-dos. The time left is capped at the new to-do's size (the person's own assumption when unsized), unless the time left was already above the size it had, which only the person's own answer can do. |
| - | Done | TX-1c: Full local browser runs failed because another session's cleanup, `pkill -f 'server/main.ts --no-open --no-reopen --headless'`, matched every run's demo servers. The harness now puts `--port` first so that pattern can't match them, and a server stopped from outside fails the next page with a message that says so. No retries. |
| - | Done | Main CI (#306, #308): a jump to one setting scrolls, highlights and focuses once. It waits for the section's data the first time; a later reload doesn't do it again, and the person clicking, pressing a key or moving the focus first cancels it. The late focus could land between Playwright's focus and its typing, so the text went into the setting and the search box stayed empty. This replaces TX-1b's check for a non-empty search. |

## TB-3: the Teams bot matches Ask, with a parity test (Nov 9)

Unnumbered until merge. Why (Tanner, Nov 9): the Teams app could do far less than Ask. Both already called the same service; the gap was in what sat in front of it and in a lossy adapter behind it.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| TB-3.1 | Decided | **One path.** Every 1:1 message goes to the same `assistant.handle` as the panel, as the same person with the same allowlist, GD-2 boundary and AI settings. The bot's own intent handling (`server/teams/quick.ts`) is gone. |
| TB-3.2 | Decided | **What stays only in Teams, and why.** `capture:` (a hard rule since TB-2: saved at once, no AI, with Undo) and the reply to the Close out prompt (a free-text answer to a card only Teams shows). The Close out reply now only applies to a message that isn't a question or a request: before, it swallowed anything sent for 3 hours after the prompt. |
| TB-3.3 | Decided | **The no-AI phrases moved into the Assistant, for both channels.** "help" and "open FocusTime" answer always; "what's next" and "today" answer from the plan when AI is off, not set up or over a cap, and otherwise go to the AI like any question. The panel's box now shows with AI off so they can be used there too. |
| TB-3.4 | Decided | **One result, one renderer per channel.** The Assistant returns neutral results: links are app addresses (`#/…`) or outside URLs, never built for a channel. `server/teams/render.ts` draws every card kind, as an exhaustive switch, so a new kind without a Teams drawing doesn't compile. No answer logic lives in the renderer. |
| TB-3.5 | Decided | **Links in Teams** go only to FocusTime, DevOps (`dev.azure.com`, `*.visualstudio.com`) and GitHub (the standards articles), `safeLinks` in `server/teams/cards.ts`. Before, DevOps and standards links were dropped. |
| TB-3.6 | Decided | **Preview in Teams is the whole preview:** summary, target, who sees it, the account it writes as, every row before and after, notes, Confirm and Cancel. |
| TB-3.7 | Decided | **Undo on a DevOps write is that write's own undo, previewed and confirmed on the card** (tool `write_undo`, button only, never offered to the model, and only for a write the proposal itself made). It replaces the link to the audit log. A move's Undo is the same one as the panel's. A capture keeps TB-2's Undo. |
| TB-3.8 | Decided | **Old cards never act on old data.** Expired: says so and offers Refresh, which builds a new preview from what the proposal held, and the old values are never written. Changed since: the card becomes the new preview under "It changed since", and it can be confirmed (it used to be dead, because the service keeps the same proposal id; `reopen` in the card store). Confirmed elsewhere: says so and writes nothing. |
| TB-3.9 | Decided | **A tapped choice names the question it answers** (`asked`, the question's turn). A tap on a superseded card says "That question isn't open any more" and leaves the open question alone; choice values repeat (`c1`), so before this it could answer the wrong one. A tap after the 10 minutes looks again instead of using the old choices. |
| TB-3.10 | Decided | **One memory.** The 2-hour conversation memory has no channel, so a question asked in Teams can be followed up in the app and the other way round. It was already so; the parity suite now holds it to that. |
| TB-3.11 | Limit | **A message over 24 KB or 9 cards** shows the first part and "Open in FocusTime", a deep link to the same answer (`#/ask/<turn>`, the panel at that turn). Older turns come back with the panel's history when opened that way. |
| TB-3.12 | Limit | **A preview over 20 rows or 12,000 characters.** A FocusTime-only change keeps Confirm with "And N more" and Open in FocusTime. A DevOps write that can't be shown whole has no Confirm in Teams, only Open in FocusTime and Cancel: a description edit must always show the full before and after. |
| TB-3.13 | Limit | **Edit** isn't on the card: "Edit in FocusTime" opens the same proposal in the panel. Adaptive Cards could carry inputs, but one editor per field kind would be a Teams-only copy of the panel's. |
| TB-3.14 | Limit | No answer today has a wide table, so there is no table case. A new one would go through the 24 KB rule above. |
| TB-3.15 | Decided | **The parity test is the rule** (`assistant/parity.test.ts`): every Ask topic, every tool, every golden question in `assistant/answers.test.ts` and AS-2's flows run through the panel's entry and the real bot core on fakes, and must give the same result, a drawing of all of it, and the same writes. A topic or tool without a case fails, and so does a new golden question. |
| TB-3.16 | Decided | "What's next" with AI off is now Ask's `today` read, so Teams loses the Now strip's next-meeting line it had. Ask doesn't show it either; adding it there would change both together (open question). |
| TB-3.17 | Limit | Teams turns saved before this release have absolute links in history. They open in a new tab from the panel, and age out in 30 days; not migrated. |
## Part 0: a shared owner and state per conversation (Nov 9)

Unnumbered until merge. Up for grabs is a person's view here; the spec's Up for grabs requests don't exist as a separate kind in the code yet, so the record is on conversations.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | Part 0: State is never stored. Closed is `closed_at`, Owned is `claimed_by`, otherwise Open, so the two can't disagree. Every change appends a row to `conversation_events`, an app table with ids and names only, never message text, and goes through the store's `viewer(userId)`, so visibility is what it was. |
| - | Done | Part 0: `owner_id` stays the AI's guess at who is driving a conversation and never makes anything Owned. Only a person's action (or a Give to, or the backfill) sets `claimed_by`. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Behavior change: an Open conversation where someone said "I'll do it" shows as Up for grabs to their teammates until that person taps I'll take it. Before, it went straight to Handled by teammates. This is what "never claims silently" asks for. |
| - | Done | Part 0: The offer "You replied. Take this?" (or "You said you'd do it. Take this?") shows in the detail pane only while nobody has it. A claim from it is recorded on its history row (`via`), so the timeline says "took it after replying". |
| - | Done | Part 0: Handled by teammates is now Teammate has it (the address and code name stay `teammates`), folded out of Everything's active part and its count. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Claim: a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE claimed_by IS NULL` inside the transaction. No row changed means someone else won, and the loser gets "<First name> has this" (409), reloading the item. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Give to and Release: the owner or one of the client's leads, and Give to by anyone while nobody has it. Give to and Add helper offer the Cooptimizers on that chat who use FocusTime and follow the client, so the new owner can always see it. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Helpers only once someone has it. A helper sees You're on it and is told through the notification gate. Anyone who can see a conversation can Close it out, as with Resolve before. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Undo: only the newest change still in force, and only by whoever made it. Otherwise "That's changed since". It restores the record from the row's `before_json` and marks the row undone; rows are never deleted. |
| - | Done | Part 0: A new message on a Closed one reopens it only when its author is neither the last owner nor whoever closed it, so the people who closed it can still write in the chat. It reopens to the last owner, with "Reopened: <Name> asked again". On an Owned one, the owner is notified (`conversation_activity`, once per latest work message) and it stays with them. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Assistant and Teams: `conversation_owner` is a proposal with Confirm and Undo, not at once, because the hard rule says every write the assistant offers runs only on Confirm. The person comes from the chat's own team list, by name, never an id from the model. The buttons apply at once. |
| - | Done | Part 0: An Open conversation with a linked DevOps item stays In progress (CV-5), unless a suggestion is waiting for me. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Backfill: one job, once (`job_claims`, job `conversation_ownership_backfill`, period `once`, kept out of that table's purge). Only an unclaimed conversation is ever written, so a claim made meanwhile stands. Closed stays Closed; a linked item or a New one stays Open. What's left goes to the AI's owner, an open commitment's author or the last Cooptimizer who spoke, unless its messages name another Cooptimizer (that one stays Open: it's the Asked you signal). No teammate to find means it stays Open. Counts are logged as `ownership_backfill` and kept in `counts_json`. |
| - | Done | Part 0: A promise keeps its own person-owner. A conversation can be Closed while a promise from it is open, and the promise box says so. |
| - | Done | Part 0: Close out is in the action row when it's mine, and under ⋯ otherwise. Not work-related, Not a request and Resolve now go through the same record, so they appear in the history. |
| - | Done | Part 0: HeldBack stays on screen while open, so "Nothing held back now." can be read after the last one goes. It used to unmount as soon as the count reached 0. |

## CX-0: the FocusTime and Coop comment contract v1

Unnumbered until merge. Proposed: nothing reads or posts these comments yet. [The contract](technical/coop-contract.md), its schemas and fixtures.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Proposed | CX-0: DevOps comments are the mailbox (Phase 1 of the Nov 3 design). Each is a header line and one fenced JSON block, with exact case-sensitive prefixes `[Coop brief v1]`, `[Coop update v1]` and `[Coop answer v1]`. The JSON decides; the header's words are for people. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: the header must be the comment's first non-blank line, so a person quoting a brief further down isn't read as one. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: writers post with `format=markdown`, and readers still accept a comment stored as HTML. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: no fourth prefix. Proposals travel inside the `done` update, so "proposals delivered" and `done` are one comment and can't half-happen. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: proposal v1 is FocusTime's `COOP_PROPOSALS_V1` (D529), with text limits added. Unknown fields are allowed on the wire (the version rule), unlike the strict schema the AI provider needs. A test keeps the two in step. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: a published story carries `Coop-Proposal: <brief_id>/<proposal id>` as the last line of `System.Description`, written in the create patch. It is atomic with the create, so a retry is safe; a tag would fill the shared tag list, a comment is a second write, and a custom field is a process change per client. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: a brief or answer counts only if the comment's `createdBy.id` is the requester. An update is untrusted input that only sets a display status and offers proposals. An approval typed in a comment never counts. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: the requester is a display name and a DevOps identity id. The Nov 3 draft's tenant id, object id and UPN are left out because comments are readable by the client. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: v1 has no action that writes: `permitted_actions` is `investigate`, `propose_stories`, `ask_questions`. Adding a value to any closed list is a v2. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: FocusTime applies GD-2's replacement per string value before building the JSON, and treats text from Coop as AI text under GD-2 point 5 if it is published. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: 32,000 characters per comment, this contract's own limit (Azure DevOps publishes none). A comment is never split. |
| - | Proposed | CX-0: no JSON Schema library was added. `scripts/coop-contract.test.ts` carries a small validator that throws on any keyword it doesn't implement. |

## FN-1: Story card, Copy DevOps link, Wrap it up

Unnumbered until merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | FN-1: one central link, `ui/WorkLink.tsx`. A DevOps edit URL with an item id opens the story card (`ITEM_CARD_EVENT`, App puts `#/item/<client>/<id>` in the address over the place underneath). The href stays the DevOps address so hovering shows it; a click or middle click opens the card. A link with no item id still opens through `link.open` as before. The Inbox's raw links and Conversations' linked item use `ItemTitleLink` (the id without a URL). |
| - | Done | FN-1: the card is a modal, not a side panel: the Break down panel is the only side panel and stays as it is. Esc and × close it and focus goes back; a parent or child opens its own card and Back returns. The O key and the command menu's open go to the card too. |

Nothing in the card opens DevOps. `item.card` reads the item fresh with the company read PAT and keeps it a minute per person (`fresh` after a write). The description, acceptance criteria and comments are sanitized on the server with the chat sanitizer's allowlist (no scripts, styles, event handlers or images). |

A cancelled preview takes the staged change back out. Add comment is the `comment` write. No new write kind. |

PlanWeek's two small links say Open card. LP-1's sent-result link (Open in DevOps for a DevOps comment it posted) stays: it shows the comment just sent, not an item. |
| - | Done | FN-1: a tick still lands at once everywhere (TX-1c, with its Undo), and Wrap it up opens over it. This kept every tick site (Today, the Board, Plan today, the focus timer's Done, the midday check-in) on the one `ticked` hook PK-1 added, instead of intercepting each. Closing the sheet is Skip DevOps: the tick stays and nothing is written. |
| - | Done | FN-1: the sheet opens only when there's something to write: a to-do with a DevOps item, on a client with my write PAT, that canEdit lets me change and isn't done in DevOps already, with "When I tick a story" on Ask. Without a write PAT the tick is plain, as before (no sheet asking for something it can't do). |
| - | Done | FN-1: a story ticked on a client with DevOps writes off says "DevOps updates are off for this client" once a browser session (session storage, with an in-page flag when storage is blocked), with Turn them on in Settings › DevOps. Not with "Always skip DevOps", and not for to-dos with no item. |
| - | Done | FN-1: the setting is PK-1's `devops_on_done`, relabelled "When I tick a story: Ask (Wrap it up) / Always skip DevOps". No new setting, no migration. |
| - | Done | FN-1: Finished offers the type's Completed states then Resolved ones, from the process model, never a name. The one chosen is the Completed state I set most on that client (from my own write log), else the first. Remaining Work 0 is offered ticked where the type has the field. |
| - | Done | FN-1: Made progress keeps the state, except a Proposed (New) item moves to the type's first In Progress state, ticked by default. Quick picks 30m, 1h, 1.5h, 2h or typed; Remaining Work in 0.5h steps, offered ticked. On the plan it ticks today's to-do and keeps the story's later ones with the time left (`tickDone` with `progress`); untick puts back what they had. |
| - | Done | FN-1: the comment gets the outcome word ("Done: ", "Progress: ") unless it already starts with one. Finished's comment rides in the state write's patch; Made progress posts it as a comment, or with the state change when a New item moves. |
| - | Done | FN-1: "Also add to <feature>'s status for Monday" is off by default and posts "#<id> <title>: <comment>" as a comment on the parent, the same comment Week › Status counts. |
| - | Done | FN-1: one preview of every write, one summary line ("#504: state Active → Closed · add comment · Remaining Work 0"), one Confirm. Writes run in order; a later rev-tested write on the same item is previewed again on the rev our earlier write made and stops if anything else changed. Each write gets its own result; a failed one offers Retry (or Preview again). GD-2's flag with Replace applies to the comment. |
| - | Done | FN-1: Undo on the sheet unticks the to-do and offers each DevOps write's own undo, newest first. Unticking later offers the newest write this to-do made (state or Remaining Work), then the next after that one lands, because the undo stack takes them in order. |
| - | Done | FN-1: Suggest is the `wrapup_suggest` AI task (interactive tier, the person's cap): this to-do's own facts only, KB-1 standards when they apply, GD-2's rule and scrub. With AI off it fills a template instead. |
| - | Done | FN-1: Teams uses the same writes: Done on a check-in card proposes `wrap_up` Finished, Partly proposes Made progress with the time left (a button-only assistant tool, run on Confirm through `wrapup.apply`). Teams items show Story card (FocusTime) instead of Open in DevOps. Suggest, another done state and the feature line are in FocusTime, which the card says. |
| - | Done | FN-1: the Teams card has an optional comment box (Input.Text) on the Done and Partly proposals. A comment typed there goes as the proposal's edit; since it changes what would be written, the assistant's own Confirm check shows the preview again with it ("With your comment. Confirm to write it.") and only a Confirm on that writes. The text is never logged. |
| - | Done | FN-1: the midday check-in's Partly opens the sheet on Made progress (only), since the check-in already wrote the time left on the plan. Close out keeps its one batch for the day's done to-dos (DoneBatch): a sheet per to-do at the end of the day would be a wall of dialogs. |
| - | Done | FN-1: Close out's batch asks Wrap it up per row: Finished (with its done state) or Made progress (with the time left, the same quick picks or typed), and an optional comment. Preview shows every row's writes at once (`wrapup.preview` per row, nothing written); one Confirm writes them, each row through `wrapup.confirm` (test /rev, read back, audit log, undo), Made progress first giving the plan its time left (`wrapup.tick`). Another client's name in a comment is flagged with Replace or Send it as it is, as on the sheet. The batch has no Suggest or feature line; those stay on the sheet. |

## CAL-1: Add any calendar by ICS link (Nov 10)

Unnumbered until merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | CAL-1: why. Pilots need personal calendars without Google sign-in (Google's Testing mode blocks it) and Apple has no comparable API. A subscription link (ICS) works for Google's secret iCal address, Outlook's published ICS, iCloud's public calendar link, and school or activity apps. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: the parser is `ical.js` 2.2.1 (Mozilla's, used by Thunderbird; MPL-2.0, no dependencies, its own types). It reads RRULE, RDATE, EXDATE, RECURRENCE-ID overrides and VTIMEZONE. No iCalendar parser was in the dependencies. `node-ical` was the other candidate; it pulls in `rrule` and `moment-timezone`. ical.js' time zone registry is global, so a feed's VTIMEZONEs are registered and removed inside one synchronous parse. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: times: the feed's VTIMEZONE first, then an IANA TZID (through Temporal), else the person's zone (floating times). All-day events stay dates with an exclusive end; no DTEND is one day (RFC 5545 3.6.1). STATUS:CANCELLED drops an event or one instance; TRANSP:TRANSPARENT is free; STATUS:TENTATIVE is tentative. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: the kept window is 8 weeks back to 26 weeks ahead of the fetch (Week's history, Plan ahead's 12 weeks, collaboration's look ahead), at most 5,000 instances a link and 20,000 steps through one rule. A week outside the window shows nothing from the link. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: limits: a 15 second timeout for the whole request, 5 MB (by Content-Length, then as it streams), at most 3 redirects, links of at most 2,048 characters, at most 10 links a person. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: the SSRF guard. A link must be https (webcal:// and webcals:// become https://), on port 443, with no user name or password, to a host that isn't localhost, `.local`, `.internal`, `.home.arpa`, a name without a dot, or a literal IP in a blocked range. The fetch's own DNS lookup (`server/ics-fetch.ts`) refuses a name when any answer is unspecified, loopback, private (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16), carrier-grade NAT, link-local (169.254/16, the metadata services; fe80::/10), unique local (fc00::/7), multicast, reserved or documentation, including IPv4 inside IPv6 (mapped, NAT64, 6to4). The check is the socket's lookup, so it holds at connect time (no DNS rebinding window). Redirects aren't followed by the HTTP client: each hop goes through the same rules again. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: content types: `text/calendar`, or a generic type (`application/octet-stream`, `text/plain`, none, `application/ics`, `text/x-vcalendar`) when the body starts with BEGIN:VCALENDAR (some school and club apps). HTML, JSON and anything else is refused, as is a body that doesn't parse. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: the link is a secret. It is sealed in `calendar_links.sealed` with the app's data key (`store/user-secrets.ts` seal, AES-256-GCM, bound to the person and the link id), read back only by the fetcher, and never returned, logged, exported or sent to the AI. Settings and the status show only the host and "…" ("calendar.google.com/…"). The paste box is a password field and is cleared once saved. Replace link checks the new one before keeping it; Remove deletes the link and its events. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: Busy only (the default) never stores a title, location or description: the parser doesn't read them into its result, the store writes NULL whatever it's given, and switching a link to Busy only deletes the titles it had at once. Events show as "Busy" (or "Free"). Show titles keeps titles only (200 characters), never locations or descriptions. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: refresh. Like Outlook and Google, links are read when FocusTime reads the week (Week, Today, Plan ahead, the focus timer's warnings, collaboration, the assistant's context), at most every 15 minutes per link, and at once on Refresh. Conditional with If-None-Match (else If-Modified-Since); a 304 keeps the stored events. Not in the background: nothing is fetched while nobody uses FocusTime (the idle rule, D431), so "outside working hours" needs no rule of its own. A week read waits for a due link (15 seconds at most), never two fetches of one link at once. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: events go into the same `CalEvent` list as Outlook's and Google's (source `ics`, with the link's name and color), so Week, Today, the at-risk rules, the timer's warnings, collaboration's busy time and AS-3's answers treat them alike. Personal links follow the Personal events switch; Work links count as meetings. Read only: the event menu says "Events from a calendar link are read-only in FocusTime". |
| - | Done | CAL-1: the status shows one entry per link by its name: "Family · live · 12 events · updated 6 min ago". A link that answers 404, 410, 401 or 403, isn't a calendar, or points somewhere blocked "stopped working": its line says "Family: The link stopped working" with Replace link (to its row in Settings), and the last good events stay with their age. A timeout or network failure is a passing problem with Retry. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: per person: `calendar_links` and `calendar_link_events` are user tables (`user_id` first, scoped by `store/scope.ts`); the isolation test covers them. Admins never see them (no admin view reads them). They're left out of Export (the link is sealed with this store's key; the events come back with the next fetch) and removed by Delete my data's hard delete; Sign out keeps them. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: the demo answers three fictional links on calendar.example.com in memory (`sources/demo/links.ts`: a family calendar with an override and an EXDATE, a school day off, one that stops after its third read). Anything else says only the sample links work. Nothing is fetched in demo mode. |
| - | Done | CAL-1: a link's color is one of six swatches (violet, teal, rose, amber, sky, slate) on its events' edge and its status dot, not a free color picker (readable in both themes). |

## MC-1c: Meeting registration fixes (Nov 11)

Unnumbered until merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | MC-1c: the cause of "That isn't one of FocusTime's clients": `meeting.register` checked the key against the signed-in admin's own client settings (`cfg.clientSettings`), so an admin registering a call for a client they keep no personal settings for (or whose settings key differs in case) was refused. `meeting.list`, `meeting.candidates` and `meeting.register` (the three meeting actions that take a client key; the others take a series, occurrence or proposal id) now ask `meetingContext.client`: the organization's clients from `clientAccounts()`, the list Admin › Clients shows, matched case-insensitively and answered as the organization's key. A key that isn't a client is still 404 and reads nothing. Without the team runtime, the configured clients stand in |
| - | Done | MC-1c: a registered call's default name uses the organization's name for the client (its key, as Admin › Clients shows it), never the admin's personal label |
| - | Done | MC-1c: the cause of "Bad request": the meeting actions let anything Microsoft Graph or the sign-in threw (`HttpError`, `NetworkError`, `AuthFailure`) escape, and `server/app.ts` answers any escaped exception with "bad request". A Graph refusal while listing my calendar's series or registering one now answers in plain words with a `code` the form answers: signed out or 401 "Sign in with Microsoft again to see your calendar's meetings" (409, `signin`, with Sign in again beside it); 403 names Calendars.ReadWrite and that a Cooptimize admin grants it once for everyone (`consent`); 400 and other 4xx "Microsoft didn't accept that request. Paste the meeting's Teams join link instead" (`graph_refused`); 429, 5xx and network "Microsoft isn't answering right now. Try again in a minute" (503, `busy`, with Try again); 404 the existing "isn't a weekly Teams meeting on your calendar"; FocusTime's own sign-in not set up (`setup`). Each is logged as `meeting_graph_failed` with its kind only |
| - | Done | MC-1c: a likely trigger of the reported 400: `seriesOnMyCalendar` filtered `/me/events` on `type eq 'seriesMaster' and isOnlineMeeting eq true`. Microsoft's docs ([list events](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/user-list-events?view=graph-rest-1.0), [event](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/event?view=graph-rest-1.0), [$filter](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/filter-query-parameter), checked Nov 11, 2026) don't say either property can be filtered there ("support by individual resources and its properties ... varies"). The candidates now come from the next 5 weeks of my calendar (`/me/calendarView`, no `$filter`, up to 500 occurrences): the online occurrences that belong to a series, then each distinct series master read once (at most 40), all filtered in code. A weekly call that has no occurrence in the next 5 weeks isn't offered; its link can be pasted |
| - | Done | MC-1c: a pasted link Microsoft couldn't check says why and asks for the weekday, time and length (422, the form shows them); with them it registers now, organizer unknown, so Upload. A link that isn't on my calendar is unchanged |
| - | Done | MC-1c: in Admin › Clients › <client> › Meetings a refusal shows beside the control that caused it (the calendar list, under the pasted link, or by Register for anything else), stays until the next try, and the form keeps what was typed |
| - | Done | MC-1c: non-organizers. Microsoft's [calltranscript-get](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/calltranscript-get?view=graph-rest-1.0) (checked Nov 11, 2026): "This API is also available to users who are part of the meeting calendar invite, which applies to both private chat meetings and channel meetings", with delegated OnlineMeetingTranscript.Read.All. [onlinemeeting-get](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/onlinemeeting-get?view=graph-rest-1.0) lists `GET /me/onlineMeetings?$filter=JoinWebUrl eq '{joinWebUrl}'` for delegated OnlineMeetings.Read with no organizer-only restriction, and says the by-id form accepts "both the organizer's and the invited attendee's user token". [list transcripts](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/onlinemeeting-list-transcripts?view=graph-rest-1.0) says neither way. So an attendee on the invite can read the meeting and its transcript: D921's order stays (the registrant, then the other reviewers), a call a Cooptimize teammate organizes is Automatic like one I organize, and no organizer notice or reviewer is added. No new permission, no app-only access, no change to consent; the registry gate is unchanged |
| - | Done | MC-1c: the meeting job closes the same gap: a throttled, down or unreachable Microsoft (429, 5xx, network) is `busy` and looked at again on schedule, no longer counted as "no transcript yet"; a layout that fails is logged with its kind. When the registrant's calendar can't be read (signed out, consent), the call is laid out from its stored pattern so the other reviewers' sign-ins are still tried for the transcript (a cancelled occurrence then ends as "no transcript was recorded") |
| - | Done | MC-1c: one line of proof. Each occurrence keeps the job's last look in one added column, `meeting_occurrences.last_read` (JSON: found, not yet, no sign-in, busy or failed; the FocusTime user whose sign-in read it, or who must sign in again; when). Admin's Meetings shows under each call: "Transcript read Nov 9, 2:15 PM through <name>'s sign-in", "Transcript uploaded …", or the latest call's reason ("waiting for the call to end", "<name> needs to sign in with Microsoft again", "no transcript was recorded. A reviewer can upload one", "Microsoft wasn't answering at the last look"), or "Not read yet: the first call is Tue, Nov 17". It's health, not content: no transcript words, proposals or attendees |
| - | Done | MC-1c: the demo's Microsoft account has a fictional calendar (`sources/demo/meetings-graph.ts`): a call Priya organizes at Cooptimize, a pasted link Jordan organizes, and one link that stands for Microsoft not answering, so the registration and its failure can be tried without Graph |
| - | Done | MC-1c review: the client check fails closed. With the team runtime, a client list that can't be read answers "FocusTime couldn't read the client list just now" (503), never the admin's own settings; only without a team runtime do the configured clients stand in. A non-admin is refused (403) before any client lookup |
| - | Done | MC-1c review: "bad request" can't come back from a meeting action: anything unexpected answers "FocusTime couldn't finish that. Try again" (500) and is logged as `meeting_action_failed` with its kind. A signed-out admin who pastes a link is asked to sign in (or for the pattern), not told it isn't on their calendar |
| - | Done | MC-1c review: one call is registered once. Join links compare by `joinKey` (percent-decoded, lowercase, without its query or a trailing slash). The check runs again inside the insert's transaction, holding an update lock on the live series on SQL Server, so two admins registering at once can't both get through; the candidates leave out calls any admin registered, by join link (event ids are per mailbox). No unique index: no migration for it |
| - | Done | MC-1c review: the job records why the first person's sign-in couldn't read the transcript (signed out, the permission refused (403), or the call not found for them (404)), and Admin's line says it in those words; when that person is no longer a FocusTime user it says to change the reviewers. The pattern layout runs only when the registrant is signed out or refused consent, never on a 404 (a deleted, ended or re-created series), so no upload is asked for a call that didn't happen |
| - | Done | MC-1c review: the review screen, Client calls and Week's nudges name the client by the organization's name, not a person's own label. A series with no call in the last 3 weeks says "No call in the last 3 weeks. The next one is …" instead of "Not read yet". Switching between From my calendar and Paste a Teams link clears the last refusal |

| - | Done | CF-1 (C1 to C4): the reminders read what's on the plan, not a confirm. Start of day: while today has no to-dos, closed once it has one. Midday check-in: today has to-dos and isn't closed out. Monday's "Add work to the week" and Thursday's and Friday's "Look ahead": while that week has nothing placed (`weeksWithWork`). Carry-over goes to the next day that can take work; the automatic open is once a day. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (D1): what's on my week is published for teammates (Team) and the status report's "active this week" on every change to which features are on it (the actions in `WEEK_CHANGES`, `actions/dispatch.ts`), for this week and later, never a past week. Only the feature, its type, its title and its DevOps link are shared. A failed publish leaves the plan change standing; the next change publishes again. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (D2, D3): a teammate's week shows once they're on FocusTime and have anything on it ("shared"), "Nothing on their week yet" otherwise ("empty"), or not on FocusTime. No "Not committed yet", no tier beside a feature. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (E1, E2): no automatic client heads-up offers. The slip rule (a to-do moved off its day, step 13.5) and the week heads-up (a confirmed must dropped or made a nice to have) create nothing; `weekplan.remove` offers none and `weekplan.headsup` returns an existing one or nothing. Promises opened before stay and close normally. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (G1 to G4): musts are gone. Every feature on the week counts the same: one total against the client's free time (fit, the Board, Plan, Ahead's weeks, Budget), no separate musts line, no star, no Make it a must or a nice to have, no Move up or Move down; Add work lists features in backlog order. "Musts done. See you tomorrow." became "All done. See you tomorrow." when every to-do today is done. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (G5): a send's own Must or Suggested stays: it is the sender's word about priority, not a tier on my week. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (F1 to F3): old tabs, cards and actions answer plainly and change nothing. `planday.commit` answers "There's nothing to confirm now. Your plan is what's on your day."; `weekplan.commit` "There's nothing to confirm now. What's on your week is your plan."; `weekplan.set_tier` "Every feature on the week counts the same now. Nothing changed."; an old tab's tier on `weekplan.add`, `weekplan.reorder` or `weekplan.draft_tier` is accepted and ignored. An earlier Teams card's Confirm today answers with the day's words. The command menu hides the retired actions; the registry keeps them so an old tab still gets the answer. |
| - | Done | CF-1 (F2): the assistant has no `confirm_day`, `confirm_week` or `add_must`; `add_to_week` replaces `add_must`. "Confirm my day" or "lock in my week" gets the plain answer with or without AI and no proposal (`asksToConfirm`). The glossary says nothing is confirmed and every feature counts the same. |
| - | Done | CF-1: stored data stays. `day_plans.committed_at`, `week_plans.committed_at` and the tiers (`week_features.tier`, `week_drafts.tier`, `week_commitments.tier`) are kept in the database, unread and (for confirm times) unwritten, so the idea can come back. No migration, no dropped column, no deleted row. New rows still fill the tier columns with one value (`KEPT_TIER`, "nice"), which nothing reads; a week draft's row marks it as on the week. |
| - | Done | CF-1: Confirm on an external write's preview (DevOps, Close the loop, Outlook, the VM, the assistant's and the bot's proposals) is unchanged. |
| - | Done | CF-1: the "two demo people" check is a server test (`actions/status-report.test.ts`: Sam places a feature, Dana's report lists it as planned; Sam takes it off, it goes), because every demo session is the same fictional person. The browser check is the demo person's own report (`e2e/cf1.test.ts`). |

| - | Done | CF-1 review: a week planned before CF-1 (never confirmed, or changed since) was published only on Confirm. The first `week.load` publishes this week and next once (a `cf1_published` once-mark per week, no migration), so Team and the status report don't show it empty or stale until the person's next change. |

"Did I commit to anything this week?", "can you confirm the meeting time tomorrow" or "…, I'll confirm" go to the model as before. |

## DV-2: DevOps changes show up on their own (Nov 11)

Unnumbered until merge.

| # | Status | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| - | Done | DV-2: why. Aaron (Nov 10) made epics and features in DevOps that FocusTime didn't show; principle #1 is DevOps first, so a change made in DevOps has to reach every open screen without a reload. The before and after, screen by screen, are in `docs/ux/dv2/freshness.md`. |
| - | Done | DV-2: each client has a DevOps version (`devops_poll.version_at`, an added column, migration `20261129T0900_devops_poll_version`). It moves only when a check writes an added, changed or removed item, or a FocusTime write's read back stores one. A check that finds nothing leaves it, so a screen never rereads for nothing. |
| - | Done | DV-2: one signal for every screen. The page asks `devops.versions` every 30 seconds while it is visible, and again when it comes back into view (at most every 5 seconds). The answer is each of my clients' version, check times and trouble: never an item, a title, or a client I can't see (the same viewer check as Ahead). It is a background call, so it never counts as use and never wakes the database on its own (D431). Screens subscribe (`ui/DevOpsFresh.tsx`, `useDevOpsChanged`) and reread quietly only when a client they show moves; none keeps a timer for this. Day, Week (Board, Plan), Add work and the Details panel follow the week load; Ahead, Status, the Inbox and the story card reread their own data. |
| - | Done | DV-2: a quiet reread keeps the place. Ahead keeps its scroll, selection, filters, folds and staged changes, with no "Reading…" and no error banner (a failed quiet reread keeps what shows; the next signal tries again). An item with my staged change that changed in DevOps underneath says "Changed in DevOps just now" with Show the new values ("Start: DevOps has no value, yours Mon, Nov 16 PM"); mine stays staged and Review's conflicts work as before. |
| - | Done | DV-2: Refresh, the R key and the status bar's button, works on every tab (R was Week only) and is the shared check: `devops.refresh` for the clients on screen, 30 seconds apart per client for everyone (D592), waits up to 8 seconds, then says what it found: "DevOps checked just now · 3 changes", "· no changes", "Northwind is slowed by DevOps, next check in 4 min", "Northwind's DevOps sign-in was refused". Ahead's and Status's own Refresh buttons say it beside the button; R and the status bar say it in the bar. |
| - | Done | DV-2: the status bar says "DevOps checked 40s ago" (the stalest of my clients), each client's check or trouble on hover, measured on the server's clock (the answer's `serverNow`), as RF-1 does for the dot. |
| - | Done | DV-2: outside working hours a client keeps the working-hours pace while one of its followers is using FocusTime (`activeFor`, from the use tracker); others keep 10 minutes. Nobody using it still means no checks. |
| - | Done | DV-2: Find an item, in Settings › DevOps (and the command menu, "Find an item"): one of my clients and an item number. It answers from FocusTime's store only, which the poller fills from the client's whole project, so a person's lookup never sends a request to DevOps and the company read PAT stays on the poller's own checks. In order: DevOps as of the last check, FocusTime's store, the scope, Day, Week, Ahead and Status, each with why not and what to do. Status's line comes from my plan and the item (never Status's own read, which reads comments from DevOps). An item the store doesn't have and one in a project FocusTime doesn't read for that client give the same answer ("FocusTime's last check of X's project (40s ago) found no #N. If you just made it, Refresh checks now…"), so it can't be used to probe numbers; another client's key reads as "Choose one of your clients". At most 10 a minute per person. Admins get no extra view. |
| - | Done | DV-2: a bug fixed on the way. The poller set a check's in-flight promise a microtask late, so a forced view never waited the 8 seconds D592 promised; it is set synchronously now (`start(c)`, tested). |
| - | Done | DV-2: the demo has a teammate. `demo.devops_change` (demo sessions only, 403 otherwise) changes a title, state, assignee or target date, or adds an item of a type the process has, in the session's own fictional DevOps, so the e2e and a demo script can show a change arriving. |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: App renders the signal's provider, so App's own subscription (the week load) reads the flow directly; through context it saw nothing and Day and Week never reread. Caught by the e2e. |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: the status bar's DevOps entry called a hook after an early return; moved above it. |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: the first build of Find an item read the item from DevOps with the company PAT on the person's request, and its Status line ran Status's own read (each feature's comments, live, up to 60 per client). Both are gone: the store only, and Status from the plan. |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: rereads that cost DevOps calls on a person's own request wait: Status at most every 5 minutes, the story card and the Inbox every minute, the week load every minute (it also reads calendars). Moves inside the gap land together at its end. |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: a tab left visible with nobody using it (no input for the Rule's idle minutes, HK-3) asks for versions every 5 minutes, not 30 seconds, as the old quiet refresh did, so it never keeps the database awake. |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: Refresh says only what it checked. Within 30 seconds of another check it says "DevOps was checked 20s ago", never an earlier count as new; a client DevOps slowed keeps its slower pace (no forced checks while slowed) and says so; a Retry-After hold says "DevOps asked FocusTime to wait". |
| - | Done | DV-2 review: Ahead's quiet reread that overlaps my own stage, unstage or skip keeps my changes and skips (a counter, `local`); "Changed in DevOps just now" goes once the item isn't staged any more. |

What each of them did and where it went is in `docs/ux/wd2/inventory.md`. |

From the top: "<client> · Wed AM", then "8:00 to 12:30 · 1h free" (or "a little over"), a ⋯ and close; ON THIS BLOCK (each to-do with its checkbox, title, size, + time and ⋯; "Nothing here yet."); one meetings line ("2 meetings · 1h 15m") that opens the agenda in place; ADD; and at the bottom, collapsed, "N to place" and "You sent". Today and every later day with working time takes work. A PTO day or a day off takes none, as before; its block opens read only. `panelMode` decides (add, past, off). |

One new action, `pick.take_off` (nothing covered it), on my own plan store only. |
